smart specialisation and the creation of networks of excellence for effective cohesion policy is something that can help our regions in a decisive way. better management of available resources goes hand in hand with specialisation in certain well-defined priorities, which will enable the region to gain a competitive advantage. research and innovation are the future of europe's regions, which must constructively assess their strengths and weaknesses. intelligent specialisation is naturally intended to contribute to the development of the region concerned in both economic and social terms. specialization strategies must therefore be democratically determined on the basis of expert analysis enriched by public debate with stakeholders and civil society, of course. in no case should individual priorities be dictated centrally without constructive consultation with the addressees living in the region.
lithuania took over the presidency in a difficult situation. europe was plagued by major social unrest and a severe economic and debt crisis, high unemployment and, in particular, growing dissatisfaction among citizens of the member states with the ineffective and ineffective responses of the eu to rising social tensions. however, the lithuanian presidency has shown that it can take responsibility for matters of importance to the whole of europe. i personally regard the adoption of the multiannual financial framework for 2014-2020 and the annual budget for 2014 as the greatest achievements, as well as the strengthening of the eastern partnership mentioned above. lithuania focused its efforts on strengthening the eu's capacity to provide an adequate and rapid response, whether to economic, financial or social problems. it also managed to reach agreement with the european parliament on directives on bank recovery and resolution and on deposit protection schemes for citizens, which will provide a solid basis for greater financial stability in europe in the future. i hope and believe that the greek presidency will be just as successful.
although the proposed framework for the financing of the european union's consumer policy measures for the coming budgetary period is very modest, the importance of a good allocation of these funds will certainly also be reflected in the assessment of the credibility of the union's work. we must therefore consider very carefully which of the basic objectives we will support more financially in the future and in which areas we will try to save a little. on first examination, the amount planned for the administrative expenditure associated with the implementation of the programme appears to be disproportionately high. the effect of a large number of studies and analyses concerning consumers and this area, which often do not lead to concrete measures, is also questionable. i therefore think, commissioner, that we should really pay more attention to monitoring the general safety of products and to strengthening consumer rights in the retail sector. the activities of various speculative groups operating in the field of internet trading or non-banking financial services would also deserve greater attention from our control and repressive elements.
in writing − on 24 january 2013, lara comi, member of the european parliament, took part in a panel discussion with the leader of the political party, antonio ingroi. the main topics were public procurement and organised crime in connection with the bankruptcy of coopcostruzioni. meanwhile, a discussion has opened up about robert soffritti, who has filed a criminal complaint for the crime of slander. however, laura comi claims that she was only commenting on the subject of public procurement, which she has always dealt with in the european parliament. under article 8 of protocol no 7 on the privileges and immunities of members of the european parliament, they shall not be subject to any form of inquiry, detention or legal proceedings in respect of opinions expressed or votes cast in the performance of their duties. according to the court, a statement made by a member of the european parliament outside the premises of the european parliament, which gave rise to criminal proceedings in his member state of origin for an offence connected with the expression of an opinion, constitutes an opinion expressed in the exercise of the powers of a member, which is subject to the immunity concerned only if that expression corresponds to a subjective opinion directly and manifestly connected with the exercise of those powers. the committee on legal affairs finds that the statements made by ms comi are clearly connected with the performance of her duties.
in writing − the assent procedure was introduced in 1986 by the single european act in two areas: in association agreements and in agreements governing the accession process. article 81 does not specify the exact form in which the recommendation of the committee is to be submitted. the recommendation takes the form of a legislative resolution by which parliament gives or withholds its consent to the act in question. unless otherwise provided in the treaties, a majority of the votes cast shall be required for parliament to give its consent. in some cases a majority of all members is required, for example in the case of the accession of a new member state or in the case of an electoral procedure. the reasons shall form part of the recommendation submitted by the committee responsible to the plenary. the report proposes that they should no longer be considered inadmissible but that the committee responsible should be able to attach a non-legislative resolution to the recommendation to the designated plenary. this would allow the committees, but also the plenary, to explain their position in more detail. according to the wording of article 81, amendments are also inadmissible on the ground that the statement of reasons is inadmissible. the recommendation of the committee shall not contain any statement of reasons and any amendments should be confined to the operative paragraph. the possibility of tabling amendments is indeed important in order to avoid any doubt as to the precise position of the committees.
in writing − setting emission targets for light commercial vehicles will help reduce fuel consumption and the sustainability of european innovation production. on the other hand, the emission reduction is a clear step forward in the long-term environmental protection target 2020. after several negotiations with the european commission and the council, i believe that the parties have managed to reach an appropriate compromise in setting an emission ceiling of 147 grams per 100 km by 2020. if the original targets set by the commission were to be met, we could easily end up with a global isolation of european producers. by encouraging innovation and improving the performance of cars, we will be able to strike a balance between the economic and environmental requirements of the next generation of commercial vehicles. despite the extremist views faced by the envi committee responsible, i believe that the report presented is in the interests of car manufacturers, who will retain their competitive advantages, and consumers, who will save money on fuel and ultimately protect the environment. despite the fact that this is a very technical and complicated subject for the average user, it is essential that the final consumer is informed of planned changes in legislation directly affecting his personal and labour costs.

in writing − regulation (ec) no 774/94 empowers the european commission to adopt the necessary implementing measures and to amend the regulation if it is necessary to adjust the volume of quotas and other provisions on quotas. the treaty of lisbon has established a system of delegated and implementing acts, which it is necessary to align with those powers and to confer on the european commission the implementing powers concerned. the majority of the legislation in the field of trade has already been aligned with articles 290 and 291 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union and it is therefore necessary to reflect those changes. they shall in particular concern the involvement of the european parliament in the preparation and implementation of delegated acts. for the sake of alignment, it is necessary to remove references to the comitology procedure and to the provisions of the future harmonised single cmo regulation. those provisions should be directly inserted in the amending regulation, thus ensuring that the committee on international trade retains the right to scrutinise the implementing acts.

in writing − the european food sector, worth €715 billion a year, puts food on the table every day for 500 million european citizens who deserve high-quality and safe food. unfortunately, these sectors have been rocked by a number of major scandals in recent years, which have eroded consumer confidence in the european agro-industry. i consider it absolutely unacceptable that cases of serious food fraud are appearing in the eu, such as the labelling of horse meat as beef, the use of fats with dioxins or other harmful substances in the production of feed, the mislabelling of meat and fish products, the confusion of pasta and food salt, the falsification of the guarantee period or the re-freezing of meat, and others. enforcement of relatively detailed food law must therefore be reinforced by increasing regular unannounced inspections at all stages of the production and distribution chain. if infringements are found, firm action and appropriate fines should be imposed. for these reasons, i am voting for the report on the food crisis and fraud in the food chain.
in writing food safety and consumer interests and public health and food safety as a whole have always been at the heart of the eu's concerns. cases such as the use of pasta salt in polish food or the recent horse meat scandal point to a persistent structural problem. food fraud has a negative impact on consumer confidence in the food chain and on distributors. food is safer than ever before, yet consumer confidence is still declining. the eu does not have a generally accepted definition of food fraud, as current eu legislation focuses mainly on food safety. therefore, it is difficult to determine the current extent of food fraud in the eu, although according to several sources it is increasing. the most common types of food fraud include counterfeiting, substitution, manipulation or imitation. the most endangered products are fish, oil and organic food. it is therefore necessary to establish a network to combat food fraud and to define clearly the concept of 'food fraud'. until now, the indication of origin has only applied to fresh meat, but in the interests of safety, such indication should also apply to processed food. food fraud is becoming more frequent and member states must therefore act as soon as possible. checks must be more effective and more frequent, and food fraud must be adequately punished. their main objective is to safeguard public health and restore consumer confidence.
in writing − i supported the report because food fraud has become very common in recent times. it is not just the horse meat instead of beef scandal, but we can give many more examples. we focus on food safety in eu legislation, but we also need to include the concept of food fraud in the legislation. consumers buy organic eggs that are caged, olive oil that is not olive, quality coffee that contains coffee substitutes. we're eating road salt instead of food. fraud also occurs when a manufacturer markets a seemingly identical product on two separate markets, but with different quality ingredients. the risks of food fraud being detected are very low and the penalties are low. the proposed increase of penalties to twice the estimated economic profit will not eliminate fraud in the food chain either, and it is therefore necessary to criminalise the concept of food fraud in order to protect consumers. increased vigilance, consumer awareness and systematic monitoring could also play a better role in detecting and combating fraud in the food chain.

public procurement is a significant part of the economy, accounting for almost 19% of the union's gdp. their effective implementation can make a significant contribution to boosting investment and overcoming the european economic crisis. it is important to award public contracts in an efficient and socially sustainable manner. failure to comply with obligations in the field of social and labour law and environmental law shall be considered as a reason for exclusion. the concept of the lowest price should be definitively abolished and replaced by the concept of the most economically advantageous tender. it is also important that smes are effectively involved in the form of effective subcontracting. the introduction of the principle of responsibility throughout the subcontracting chain is necessary in order to ensure respect for fundamental rights, the health and safety of workers and the applicable provisions of labour law. the goal is to simplify procurement. member states should provide contracting entities with the technical and financial means to adapt to online procurement procedures.
public procurement is an integral part of a well-functioning economy and as such must stimulate competition, innovation and growth. the single public procurement rules will significantly increase transparency and equal treatment, opening the door to greater participation by smes, which are the backbone of the european economy. in this context, i stress the need to simplify public procurement and eliminate red tape, for example by systematically using electronic procurement. as regards environmental and social standards, i am convinced that the criterion of the lowest price should not be the sole criterion for awarding contracts. the contracting authority must deal with a particular situation in a comprehensive manner with a long-term perspective. the most economically advantageous tender must therefore be assessed in terms of the economic but also the social and environmental benefits.
public procurement contracts are an important part of the economy and therefore the modernisation of the public procurement directives should strike a balance between simplifying the rules on the one hand and introducing orderly and efficient tender evaluation procedures on the other. however, we must guarantee a higher participation of smes and also increase the use of electronic public procurement. in order to increase transparency, any entity that does not comply with obligations arising from social, labour and environmental law, whether defined in national or european legislation and in collective agreements concluded in accordance with union law, should also be excluded from public procurement. it is also inadmissible for contracting authorities to award a contract to a tenderer who, although submitting the most favourable tender, is unable to provide, for example, updated information on the payment of his social security contributions or is left in arrears to his subcontractors.
in writing. to boost economic growth, employment, social inclusion, fight for greater transparency and fight against corruption, we also need to modernise public procurement, which represents 19% of the union's gdp. successful revision and implementation of public procurement rules will therefore help to restore investment in the real economy and overcome the european economic crisis. it is high time that we replaced the concept of 'lowest price' with the concept of 'most economically advantageous tender'. in slovakia, too, some firms distort competition by deliberately undercutting prices in order to succeed at all costs. then they're not able to complete the contract. they don't pay their subcontractors. it causes a chain of insolvencies and the liquidation of the weakest. often the investor, in many cases the towns and municipalities, will lose access to the funds and get into huge trouble because of the non-closure of the contract. provisions on abnormally low tenders need to be strengthened to prevent any possibility of subcontracting which does not respect labour rights o.the european union must be able to give preference to domestic producers in certain specific cases when awarding public contracts. v.in the context of simplifying public procurement, the expansion of electronic procurement will benefit in this area, we expect annual savings of between 80 and 100 million eu r. and
the european commission's 2011 judgment that pollen should be considered as a component of honey and not a natural constituent, whether one likes it or not, and whether this is true or not, made it clear that strict labelling requirements for the indication of genetically modified content should also apply to honey. but the european commission's current proposal declares pollen to be a component of honey, which may be true, but it means that it will never be labelled as containing gmos. at first glance, this seems to be a simple legal issue, but upon closer examination, the proposal has serious adverse consequences. it removes the freedom of choice for gmo-containing and non-gmo products. the absence of a labelling obligation would result in the loss of the eu standard guaranteeing the safety of non-gmo honey production. by bypassing labelling requirements, the commission's proposal would thus favour trade in honey from third countries, mainly from non-eu producers and exporters of honey containing gmos. therefore, the interests of european beekeepers and consumers of high-quality honey should be protected so that they can continue to be free to choose whether to consume products containing gmos on the european market.

in writing − tachographs are used to monitor road traffic and compliance with rules to ensure safety and decent working conditions for drivers. the new regulation will improve the use of the digital tachograph, a device installed in a vehicle and used to monitor its operation. a balance has been struck between greater road safety and working conditions on the one hand and respect for the private life of drivers on the other. smart tachographs will be put into practice, which will serve as a means to improve enforcement and also reduce the administrative burden on transport companies. within 15 years of the introduction of smart tachographs on the market, all vehicles should be equipped with such a tachograph. the proposal to merge driving licences and driver cards was considered at this stage to be underdeveloped and unprepared and was therefore omitted from the text. according to the agreed text, the use of tachographs is not mandatory for trucks of less than 7,5 tonnes used for the transport of materials, equipment or machinery used by the driver in the course of his work and used only within a 100 km radius of the company's base, provided that driving the vehicle is not the driver's main activity.
in writing − if you have although the eu and aetr states have established a common system of recording driver activities for the purpose of checking compliance with social legislation, on average 9% of vehicles inspected are found to be in breach of the rules, according to the european commission. in particular, about one quarter of them were found to have infringed the tachograph rules. i support the report because it comes with new proposals which i believe will make it at least if not completely difficult to eradicate fraudulent practices with tachographs, reduce the administrative burden, simplify the technology which will be available at an acceptable cost and limit member states' investments as much as possible. i am pleased that some new provisions on the functions of tachographs have also been incorporated into the regulation and that the proposed regulation has strengthened the protection of personal data and the protection of drivers' privacy stored in the system. i also welcome the fact that the regulation allows for the exemption of the use of tachographs for certain users within the uniformly extended circuit.
in writing. − if you have in december 2011, the european commission presented an important package of legislative proposals on public procurement and a new directive on the treatment of works and service concessions, as announced in single market act i. public contracts, unlike concessions, have specifically defined rules. concessions, on the other hand, are subject only to the basic provisions of existing directives or to the general principles laid down in the treaty. experience has shown that a legislative initiative in the field of concessions is necessary to strengthen the legal framework, stability and certainty. it is necessary to focus on simplifying and clarifying the existing directive in order to ensure a systematic legal framework, adapt the concept of the concession to the concept of the open contract, the independence of public authorities in the choice of the legal framework and in the setting of quality criteria for service concessions and, last but not least, to maintain the necessary transparency necessary for equal treatment of economic operators. the internal market of the european union is marked by a lack of legal certainty and by clientelism in the form of direct agreements between public authorities and selected partners.
in writing − public procurement is an important part of the economy, accounting for up to 19% of eu gdp. the modernisation of the public procurement directives should lead to a revival of investment in the real economy and to overcoming the crisis. similarly, a balance should be struck between simplifying rules on the one hand and introducing sound and efficient procedures based on innovative and sustainable criteria for evaluating tenders on the other, while also ensuring greater participation by member states and extending the use of electronic public procurement. any entity that fails to comply with an obligation under social, labour and environmental law, as defined in national and european legislation and collective agreements concluded in accordance with european union law, should be excluded from public procurement. similarly, contracting authorities may not award a contract to the tenderer who submitted the best tender if that economic operator is unable to provide up-to-date information on the payment of its social security contributions. each contracting authority should have at its disposal a set of tools to enable it to choose the most appropriate procedure in the light of its needs. member states should provide contracting authorities with the technical and financial means they need to adapt to electronic procurement procedures and to draw up calls for tender.
in writing − i supported the proposal for a european parliament and council directive on public procurement, as it creates the conditions for reviewing and modernising public procurement in order to increase the efficiency of public expenditure. i very much welcome the replacement of the concept of 'lowest price' by that of 'most economically advantageous offer', which must take account of environmental and social aspects without compromising the primary quality criterion. the report also introduces further simplifying rules for local authorities and higher territorial units. the rules for suppliers and subcontractors eliminate the chain of exploitation or degradation of public procurement contracts. more flexible and transparent rules, in turn, create space for smes by lowering the turnover criteria. the proposal also creates, among other improvements, a reduction in red tape so that their participation in competitions does not cost too much effort.
in writing − if you have to maintain employment, social inclusion and sustainable growth, the potential of public procurement needs to be exploited to the maximum extent possible. it is necessary to introduce technical specifications containing tender documents in order to enable the contracting authority to achieve the sustainability objectives it has set. in the context of effective and transparent public procurement, it is necessary to strengthen the grounds for exclusion of a tenderer who breaches its obligations in relation to social and labour law and gender equality as defined in national and european legislation and collective agreements. similarly, contracting authorities cannot award the contract to the best tender unless the economic operator is able to provide up-to-date information on the payment of social contributions. in order to meet the objectives of the procurement, it is important that member states are able to control the performance of the economic operator that has been awarded the contract. national managing authorities should be relieved of any additional administrative burden in order to guarantee the proper functioning of public procurement and not to slow down their activities.
in writing − third countries are not willing to open their public procurement markets to international competition. currently, suppliers from the european union face a variety of restrictive procurement practices. the european union, on the other hand, has kept its public procurement market largely open. on 21 march 2012, the european commission presented an initiative on international procurement to leverage bilateral trade negotiations with third countries in the context of the opening of public procurement markets. the european commission is thus responding to the ever-growing discrepancy between the ambitions and interests of the major trading partners on the one hand and the practices of the major trading partners on the other. the european commission's proposal consists of two procedures. the first procedure is a mechanism that allows a national contracting authority to exclude third-country tenders if it feels that european companies do not have equal access to the third-country market. the second procedure allows the european commission to open its own investigation into the equal access of european companies to the contracting market of a third country and subsequently to take the necessary measures.
the court's ruling raised fundamental questions concerning the coexistence of genetically modified crops and beekeeping. the court has held that the placing on the market of honey containing genetically modified pollen requires an authorisation under article 4 (2) of regulation 1829/2003, and that conclusion applies irrespective of whether the pollen is defined as an ingredient or a constituent. european consumers deserve to be informed in the truth about which foods contain genetically modified ingredients so that they can make an informed decision. i would point out, however, that this directive, on which we voted, is mainly aimed at importers from other countries, such as the usa, where gmos (genetically modified food) are widespread and whose products are flooding the european market. that is why i voted not to harm beekeepers from small countries, including slovakia.
in writing − the commission's proposal is a response to the recent ruling of the court of justice of the european union in the case of the land of bavaria v k.h. bablock association of amateur beekeepers. the eu ds has decided that pollen is an ingredient, not a natural part of honey, and therefore food containing genetically modified components such as pollen must be labelled as a genetically modified product if the percentage of genetically modified components is higher than 0,9%. honey in circulation in the eu, including imported honey, should be checked for the presence of genetically modified pollen. the proposal from the european commission would allow it to amend the technical parameters concerning the names, product descriptions and definitions. the power to adopt delegated acts in respect of non-essential elements should continue to be conferred on the commission. for example, as regards the establishment of a method for verifying that honey complies with the provisions of the directive.
written − the hercule iii programme is designed to protect the financial interests of the european union. the programme in question was launched at the end of 2011. the main objective of this programme is to make more effective the fight against cigarette smuggling and counterfeiting, to which the european commission has committed itself through international agreements. while it is important to prevent activities that threaten the financial interests of the european union, the effectiveness of similar programmes in the context of the financial and economic crisis is questionable, particularly in terms of respecting budgetary responsibility. for the 2014-2020 programming period, it is important to avoid overlaps between similar programmes, in particular with a budget of eur 110 million, which represents a significant increase over hercule ii. the total financial envelope will depend on the outcome of the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework.

colleagues, some countries have traditionally been stirring up hysteria about the free movement of persons within the eu. now it's great britain, in the past it was germany or austria. it doesn't matter. the problem lies elsewhere: these are the countries that are happy to attract skilled workers and offer them various benefits. qualified forces who have been trained at the expense of another country and now have their skills to pass on to another country. well, today we're in the eu, we're basically one country, and we're citizens of the same "country". but then it has to go both ways: those countries that attract skilled labor from poorer countries cannot deny access to people with less skills or choose who can and cannot come into the country. just take the cream. they must have an equal share in the support of the poor, in their social support, but also accept competition in the labour market. this is solidarity, but not just that: it is justice, equal for every eu citizen, no matter what country he or she is from. you can't just take the benefits and ignore the costs. the rich countries of europe must recognize this and act accordingly.
the sale of maltese and european citizenship to third-country applicants by the maltese administration is a less sophisticated form of similar arrangements in other countries offering the right of residence for business investment, real estate or government bonds. although decisions on citizenship are a matter for member states, there are certain aspects of malta's citizenship trading regime which are seriously embarrassing. malta became the first country to simply put a price tag on european citizenship, put it on the market and start making money off the rights that the 28 member states have committed to give european citizens. from the point of view of other states, such an act by the maltese administration is not neutral. other member states will have to recognise and grant the right of establishment, freedom of movement, the right to stand for election to the european parliament and so on to the holder of a maltese passport. i am therefore convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that the ongoing trade in european citizenship must be stopped in time.
i would first like to thank commissioner füle and the two rapporteurs for their positive contribution to the enlargement of the european union, because the example of serbia and kosovo shows that this policy is continuing and is producing results. the progress made by serbia in the past year is remarkable. i appreciate the commitment and the actions of the current government in relation to reforms. i just want to point out that we expect real reforms, no tinkering, no cleverness, and we expect full implementation. but with it comes greater responsibility. responsibility for commitments to european partners, to eu values, and to its own citizens. the negotiations awaiting serbia will be long and arduous. however, i believe that the serbian government will show the confidence it has received from its european partners and from its voters in the successful launching of accession negotiations and in their successful conclusion. as far as kosovo is concerned, last year's agreement between pristina and belgrade is a historic event. however, the shift in relations between them must continue. the union should monitor and control its implementation. the start of negotiations on a stabilisation and association agreement, well-run local elections and progress on some reforms are signals that kosovo is also making progress. on the union side, we should seek the widest possible support for kosovo's participation in regional and international organisations, because this gives more responsibility to political leaders, also for domestic affairs, and we should make progress on visa liberalisation, because kosovo remains an isolated country in the balkans and this is not good for them or for us.
i welcome the progress in the negotiations between serbia and kosovo. the slovak republic does not recognise kosovo's independence precisely because it is not the result of the belgrade-pristina agreement. the violent disappearance of new states is an unacceptable precedent for the european union, but the european union must make its own political decisions to ensure that a similar process cannot be repeated anywhere in europe. this is a timely challenge, as separatist movements are becoming more and more active in the european union. the problem is not to be solved by putting it down to the level of member states on the one hand and the un on the other. the european union must reject illegitimate and illegal practices of separation of territories, minorities and ethnic groups. only an agreement can be a ticket to the eu. i can assure not only mr posselt or mr bokros, but all of you, that the moment belgrade and pristina agree, the slovak republic will accept the agreement.
i believe that today it is necessary to acknowledge serbia's progress towards integration into the european union. we'll open accession negotiations virtually with an ngo conference next week and serbia will become the integration leader in the region. serbia has done a great deal in the past to demonstrate that it can reform the political system, transform the economy and modernise the judiciary. the serbian government's constructive approach to its neighbours has helped stabilise the region and is becoming a fitting example for neighbouring countries looking to their political future. i think we all realize that serbia is now facing the most difficult stage of the country's transformation and that it will require not only our challenges and recommendations, but also qualified advice and perhaps sometimes patience and understanding, so that the changes made in the structure of the country are not just a matter for the state administration, but that they are also accepted by the citizens of this country.
in writing. i wish to join my colleagues in urging the five eu member states greece, cyprus, romania, slovakia and spain which have not yet recognised kosovo as an independent state to do so as soon as possible. i am making this urgent appeal because i believe that these member states have no reason to fear that the case of kosovo could set an example within the eu. the eu member states have all recognised the protection of minorities as a common value by the treaty of lisbon. only a country that respects minority rights can become a member of the eu under the copenhagen criteria. member states that have not yet recognised kosovo should recognise that minorities living on their territory can only become truly cooperative partners if their rights are adequately protected in the linguistic, cultural, educational and political fields. by consistently ensuring internationally guaranteed minority and linguistic rights, minority groups in the member states can feel at home in their homeland. our task is to further develop this kind of minority protection within the eu for the sake of european cultural diversity and peaceful coexistence (as well as ensuring equal rights for eu citizens).
the deteriorating economic situation in europe and the increasing influx of migrants have highlighted the problems of homelessness in several member states of the union. surviving on the streets during the winter months without a dry, warm bed is challenging even for a healthy, well-groomed man. this is especially true when mothers and children are in this situation. the social system must therefore be prepared to register such cases promptly and, within its means, to provide the necessary assistance to the persons concerned. although our current mechanisms for providing aid to people in need have long provided aid to the homeless, they are not able to respond quickly to the surge in homelessness today. i therefore think that we need to examine rapidly the possibilities of increasing the efficiency of our mechanisms so that they are able to provide more effective assistance to these cases even during this period.
i support the resolution, although i have doubts about the meaning of many of the recommendations contained in the motion for a resolution. in my view, it is not possible to develop a common strategy for homelessness for all member states of the union. just as it is not possible to introduce a single minimum wage, a single pension or a single social security system. the reason is always the same: huge differences between member states in the performance of their economies, in labour productivity, in the traditions of state administration, or in the different levels of family relations and solidarity between generations. however, i agree that the member states alone have the competence to deal with the situation of the homeless. european legislation should not deal with the issue of homelessness, because the result can only be a series of flattering statistics and meaningless recommendations, and that does not help the homeless.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, with full respect to article 168 of the lisbon treaty, the ppe group is standing firm on the position that sexual and reproductive health and rights come under subsidiarity and, as such, under the competence and responsibility of the member states. it was clear that different traditions, situations and legislation in the member states divide european public opinion – as well as this house – just as it was clear last time when parliament rejected the estrela report. we cannot impose or recommend solutions which are profoundly unacceptable in different member states on such a sensitive issue. what we can and should do is to support improvements in healthcare for pregnant women and mothers, and to improve the conditions for reproductive health, as maternal mortality is still high in some member states, as was stated by the commission. on the other hand, the statistics show that 15 % of couples are infertile. in this field, the commission should facilitate the exchange of best practices. what we can and should do is follow the principle of non-discrimination as one of the main values of the union in the accessibility of health services. what we can and should do is step up efforts to eliminate violence against women and girls: not just because it often results in a deterioration of sexual and reproductive health, but also because it is a serious offence against human dignity. what we can and should do is strengthen the fight against female genital mutilation (fgm) as a special form of violence which is also present in the european union and which often stays hidden and goes unpunished. there is a lot of room for better law enforcement, because many violent acts against women and girls go unpunished, while victims are stigmatised.
the will of parliament was expressed twice when it rejected estelle's scandalous, ideologically motivated report. legislation in the field of health, including reproduction, falls under the exclusive competence of the member states, under the current treaties on the european union. the judgment of the supreme court of luxembourg of october 2011 in the trial of experiments on living embryos says that human life begins with fertilization, conception and that from that moment it also has an inalienable human dignity. and as a doctor, i sign that too. in pregnancy, not only the rights of the woman are decided, but also the rights of the unborn child. this child already has full legal right of inheritance, but interestingly, no birthright. and the left here is fighting for the systematic elimination of healthy human beings.
today's debate offers us the opportunity to reaffirm what we decided in our resolution of 10 december 2013: that the development and implementation of policies on sexual and reproductive health and rights and on sexuality education in schools is a competence of the member states. but to circumvent our resolution, the issue of abortion is placed on the agenda of non-discrimination in this debate, so that some competence is granted to the european institutions. this procedure shows the limits of the european system: political efforts are reinterpreted until they correspond to pre-established political solutions. we're trying to disrespect the member states on sensitive ethical issues. this discussion essentially serves as an attack on the christian-democratic government in spain. i'm sorry i can't speak spanish, but i feel solidarity with the spanish government and millions of spanish citizens. in conclusion, i call on the spanish people and all citizens who defend life before birth and the right of women to become mothers not to lose faith in the european project. exercise your right during the european parliament elections in may.
written − the european union has been going through a period of economic and financial crisis in recent years and needs to kickstart economic dynamism. the crisis has hit the industry hard, with 3.8 million jobs lost. the european union is lagging far behind japan and china in industrial strategies, which have presented industrial plans and plans for the strategic development of their industries. the european commission took the lead with a communication entitled 'a stronger european industry'. europe needs to launch a modernisation offensive in industry, which still accounts for 4/5 of european exports and 80% of private sector investment in r & d. it is important to invest in innovation and skills and to abandon the old style of industrial policy based on subsidies for enterprises, state ownership and customs protection. one of the most striking shortcomings of european industrial policy is the lack of investment in research and human capital, which is significantly slowing down the shift towards an innovation-based economy. efficient use of resources could lead to cost savings of 20 to 30%. europe cannot compete with emerging economies on wages, it must focus on reducing its energy and resource costs through their efficient use. member states must catch up with innovation leaders like germany, france or denmark. europe must prepare for new challenges and changing market dynamics.
in writing − the current rules governing the immunity of members do not reflect the relevant case law of the court of justice of the eu. like continental legal systems, european union legislation recognises the substantive and procedural nature of parliamentary immunity. the substantive nature of the matter can be understood as meaning that no liability can be attributed to an elected member for any acts which he has committed as a member of the legislative body during the term of office. the procedural nature, on the other hand, suggests that it is only with the consent of the legislature that an elected member can be prosecuted for acts committed outside his office. the court of justice of the european union dealt with the immunity of members in the marra, patricello and gollnisch cases. in the light of the case-law of the court of justice of the european union and the opinion of the committee responsible, it is necessary to recast the relevant provisions in order to make their structure more coherent and their content in line with the latest case-law and practice of the committee responsible.
i supported the motion for a resolution because i am convinced that the free movement of persons as a fundamental principle of the functioning of the european union is beyond question. the fears of mass migration of workers from romania and bulgaria, for whom the labour market opened on 1 january this year, are, i think, unfounded and unnecessary. member states may not discriminate against mobile workers in the european union by wrongly linking the right to free movement for work purposes to alleged abuse of social security systems. in a year when we want to call on the citizens of the european union to elect their representatives to the european parliament, we must emphasise more than ever the very benefits of the european union's functioning, which undoubtedly include the free movement of persons, goods and services.
europe as a community of states and citizens will only exist as long as there is a consensus on fundamental freedoms. there is no compromise. the so-called social tourism is the result of a combination of the inability to apply existing rules and frustration with the high costs to social systems in times of economic crisis. the commission has prepared a series of practical guides for authorities in the member states on how to apply european legislation to combat abuse of social benefits. this is a clear signal that the problems that arise are not related to free movement itself but to the application of existing regulations in practice.
in writing − if you have twenty years ago, the maastricht treaty extended the right of free movement to all eu citizens, regardless of whether they are economically active. for 56% of european citizens, free movement is the eu's most positive achievement. more and more europeans are taking advantage of this right and living in another member state: at the end of 2012, up to 14.1 million citizens were living in a member state other than their own. the european commission is committed to ensuring that european union citizens can enforce their right to work and live in any country of the european union in practice. member states and the european union must join forces to ensure that free movement rules continue to bring the greatest possible benefits to citizens and our economy. the european commission is aware that the sudden arrival of large numbers of people from other countries in a geographical area can cause problems in that location. such a situation may, for example, place undue burdens on education and housing or infrastructure. as from 1 january 2014, temporary restrictions on the free movement of romanian and bulgarian workers will be lifted. however, in some member states the possibility of extending these restrictions is being discussed. the uk, germany, the netherlands and austria want to be able to expel citizens of other eu member states who abuse the right of free movement of persons in the community for social tourism.
in writing − the free movement of workers within the eu's internal market is one of the union's fundamental achievements, enabling citizens to work or reside freely in another member state. however, respect for the fundamental right of free residence implies effective equal treatment of workers from another member state with their own nationals in the fields of employment, social security and taxation. in particular, in the field of social security, many employees feel that there is a lack of coordination, which naturally creates a certain barrier to labour mobility. the right to free movement is enshrined in the treaty on the functioning of the eu and further regulated in secondary legislation, with a wealth of case law, yet discrimination persists. i therefore join the call to the member states to incorporate the right to free movement more fully into their legislation and to create a favourable environment for its application in everyday life.
after 2013, the european year of citizens, it is hard to hear that an increasing number of member states are selling citizenship to third-country nationals who are said to be bringing or will bring investments to the member state concerned. i believe that trading and profiting from citizenship is dishonorable and tramples on all european treaties. moving around europe is a privilege that is part of the fundamental freedoms that european countries have been working towards for decades. as we will explain in this context to citizens of romania and bulgaria who have met the schengen criteria and are now at home, others can buy citizenship and also benefit from the benefits of schengen. the commission must therefore assess all citizenship systems in the light of european values and european union law and take effective measures to protect this european privilege. i voted in favour of the motion.
in writing any person who has the nationality of a member state of the european union is automatically also a citizen of the european union. citizenship of the european union complements and does not replace nationality. each member state shall determine the conditions for acquiring and withdrawing its nationality. malta's plan to start selling european union passports for €650,000 has raised security concerns among member states. but this is not the first country to come up with such an idea. in times of economic crisis, several member states are trying to attract wealthy people from abroad through various investment schemes. in the last two years, several european countries, such as greece, portugal and cyprus, have introduced programmes to attract investors to real estate and construction. russians and chinese make up the majority of investors.
in writing. − some eu member states have introduced schemes that allow eu citizenship to be sold to third-country nationals. this possibility offered by the member states requires careful examination in the light of union values. the malta case may seem unique in terms of the emergence of eu citizenship, but surveys conducted in the context of the eu citizenship year 2013 show otherwise. acquiring or losing the citizenship of an eu member state is not a new issue, and we have already encountered a number of situations in which the rights of eu citizens have been violated. the eu treaties do not allow for discrimination on grounds of nationality within the eu. all eu citizens, therefore, enjoy the same rights as citizens of the union. that's the essence of cooperation and mutual trust. i think it's important to say that although the issue of citizenship is a matter of member state jurisdiction, as we see it now in the case of malta, it is closely linked to eu law, to eu citizenship. it should therefore be emphasised in each such case that member states should respect the union legal order when acting in such a capacity. i therefore supported the report and the call on the european commission to examine the rules of the member states on citizenship in the light of european values and the rule of law.
written − the progress report on serbia is part of the enlargement package adopted by the european commission on 16 october 2013. the year 2013 can be considered as a historic year for the development of relations between the european union and serbia. accession negotiations were opened on 28 june and the stabilisation and association agreement entered into force on 1 september. the european commission has concluded that serbia fulfils the necessary political criteria for the opening of the negotiations in question. the european commission has contributed to this finding by continuing to standardise relations between serbia and kosovo, including the implementation of agreements concluded so far. serbia will have to undertake far-reaching political and economic reforms in the coming years, and also focus on aligning its national legislation with that of the eu. the independence of key institutions should be improved, with particular regard to the judiciary. the effective implementation of strategies and action plans in the field of justice and the fight against corruption is testing serbia's readiness and willingness to move forward, including the prospect of a comprehensive reform of the public administration. political support for independent institutions and bodies that serve to protect vulnerable groups is also expected to increase. the country is continuing the process of aligning national and european legislation on the basis of an updated national plan for the adoption of the acquis.
in writing − this is the third resolution on iceland's progress since the opening of accession negotiations for that country four years ago. on 27 july 2010, accession negotiations with iceland were opened. there are currently 27 negotiating chapters open, of which 11 are provisionally closed. the newly elected icelandic government has temporarily suspended the accession process in order to discuss developments in the european union and postponed them until after the national referendum on accession has been held. iceland has all the capacities to assume the obligations that would result from membership, in particular through its membership of the european economic area. the european commission appreciates the transparent and inclusive conduct of the negotiations and welcomes the prospect of a country with a well-functioning economy, democratic tradition and civil society becoming a new member state.
written − south sudan became an independent country on 9 july 2011 and is striving to establish a prosperous and functioning state. however, the country is in a turbulent period as the president has dissolved the supreme bodies of the sudan people's liberation army. the situation escalated in late december 2013 when clashes broke out between the presidential guard and the forces of vice president riek machar. the fighting is spreading to several regions. un investigators have discovered a mass grave in the rebel-held city of bentiu. in south sudan, ethnic violence is escalating and a state of emergency was declared in two states in early january 2014. some 200,000 people have been forced to flee south sudan and hundreds of thousands more have been indirectly affected by the fighting. the un is currently strengthening its presence in south sudan and doubling the number of armed forces present. the european union has committed eur 170 million in humanitarian aid for 2013 and 2014. ongoing fighting over vast oil reserves has left hundreds dead and wounded and displaced more than three thousand people. the situation in south sudan is significantly destabilising the entire region and constitutes a threat to international peace.
i supported the motion for a resolution on a european union strategy on homelessness because i consider homelessness to be an extreme and the worst manifestation of poverty and social exclusion. in the advanced europe of the 21st century, we must be able to find appropriate ways, from prevention, counselling and social housing to affordable housing for the most disadvantaged people who cannot afford their own homes. to this end, member states have the possibility to use the european fund for the most deprived persons, as well as the european social fund, to improve the situation of homeless people and to pave the way for their social inclusion and gradual integration into the labour market. we must all recognise that eradicating poverty is in the interest of the european union as a whole. therefore, all sectors of society must be involved in this process and the public and private sectors must also be given the space.
in writing − homelessness affects people in all member states of the european union. the eu 2020 strategy has set the objective of rescuing at least 20 million people from the threat of poverty and social exclusion by 2020. the committee of the regions has presented a social housing programme which focuses on financial and social housing, the use of structural funds and the use of energy efficiency. member states have committed themselves to take all necessary steps to eliminate street homelessness by 2015. in the future, it is essential to establish a high-level working group to deal with the issue of homelessness. it is necessary to condemn any criminalisation of homeless people and to create a comprehensive database on homelessness in europe. the european union strategy on homelessness should be fully in line with the treaty of lisbon, which establishes a fundamental role and broad discretion for national, regional and local authorities in the provision, procurement and organisation of services of general economic interest which best meet the needs of users. it is for the member states to lay down the conditions for affordable and social housing. the european union strategy on homelessness should be fully consistent with the social housing policy of the member states, which fully embodies the principle of promoting social diversity and combating social segregation.
in writing − homelessness is a serious social problem closely linked to many types of human rights violations, in particular the right to human dignity and the right to health. homelessness often prevents access to employment, further exacerbating extreme poverty and social exclusion of homeless people. the problem of homelessness deserves a comprehensive policy solution at the level of local and regional authorities, the member states and the union should, of course, contribute its political and legal coverage to a coordinated approach throughout the eu. finally, i would like to commend the activities carried out by the union to combat social exclusion through european funds. for these reasons, i welcome the report on the eu strategy on homelessness.
in writing − homelessness affects people in all member states of the european union. in the 21st century, it represents an unacceptable violation of human dignity and one of the worst forms of poverty and deprivation. the european union has set itself the objective of rescuing at least 20 million citizens from the threat of poverty and social exclusion by 2020. member states have committed themselves to do everything possible to eliminate homelessness by 2015. in the future, it is essential to set up a high-level working group to deal with this issue. i condemn any criminalization of the homeless, and i also consider it necessary to create a database on homelessness in europe. such deplorable criminalization has recently occurred in hungary. changes in the hungarian constitution have made it possible to enforce a law allowing the prosecution of people 'living in public places'. i consider it a very unfortunate step when an eu member state targets homeless people, a particularly vulnerable group facing a very high risk of social exclusion, because it goes against human dignity and it also goes against the fundamental values on which the european union is founded. as a member of the european parliament, i therefore feel it necessary to remind those who make such legislation to respect international and european human rights agreements, both at national and local level. i regret that the observations on the facts mentioned were not adopted in plenary, and i therefore abstained.
for the efd group with the tax money of our citizens, we support the democratization processes in many countries of the world. we should therefore make it clear to the political leaders in these countries that the objective for which the citizens of the european union have chosen to help their country is to establish a civilised democratic administration of the country, in which the citizens have the final say, and not the security forces or the self-styled political leaders. the crackdown on a peaceful demonstration by textile workers, in which the demonstrators were shot by security forces, is a serious failure of the state administration that cannot be excused in any way. likewise, the intimidation of opposition leaders, the manipulation of election results are problems that cannot be tolerated in any country. we should therefore use our economic and political influence to reverse this political tension and improve the working conditions of the workers producing products which also reach our european market. i am convinced that our external action service can contribute to improving and mitigating these conflicts through increased diplomatic activities and communication with the economic and political leaders of the region.
i join my colleagues in supporting the efforts of activists in cambodia and laos. their work in both countries is of immense importance, as they represent the citizens and are part of society. that is why they deserve our constant attention. if their work is impeded by attacks by armed groups or kidnappings, the european parliament must draw attention to this and condemn it strongly. in cambodia, violent clashes have resulted in the deaths of several peaceful protesters. this is simply unacceptable. the july 2013 elections were peaceful, but i appeal to political leaders to address the shortcomings in the conduct of the elections. i also join the call for an immediate investigation into the disappearance of sombath somphon, whose whereabouts are still unknown, and to investigate this matter.
for the efd group the continuing open conflicts between the main political forces in the country are leading to an escalation of tensions and the disintegration of the still fragile political structures. the fact that the bangladesh national party boycotted the january elections due to the ongoing violence and the very low voter turnout combined to make the electoral victory of the ruling awami league party unrepresentative. it is therefore necessary, even after the failure of the un commission mission, to continue diplomatic activities in order to create the conditions for the launch of a substantive dialogue between the main political forces in order to avoid further escalation of tensions. the sensible way out of the situation may be to form a government of experts or to calmly repeat the badly politically managed last elections. but we must start by stopping the repression of political opponents and stopping the death penalty.
the critical situation in bangladesh is the result of several problems that the country has been struggling with for a long time. first of all, i want to strongly condemn the violence that accompanied the elections in early january this year. the government should be called upon to clearly define the sphere of action of the armed forces. the government must respect its international obligations. elections accompanied by violence and attacks on minorities and women and children are unacceptable. the government must also ensure an independent investigation into the deaths of 18 people who lost their lives in clashes with the police. i further appeal to political leaders to try to find a compromise with the opposition forces. bangladesh must not slacken in its democratic efforts, at least not efforts. the reconciliation, the participation of all the political forces in the country is the first prerequisite for a functioning democracy. for the first time since '91 the opposition has no representation in parliament. this parliamentary composition is problematic because it does not reflect the real choice of the bangladeshi opposition. we therefore call on the government to resume dialogue with... on behalf of the ppe group. first of all, i would like to thank my colleague rapkay for his cooperation in the work of the parliament. i'd like to start with an example. in order for eu citizens from two different member states to marry, they need to present certain official documents at the place of the marriage. such is the case with a birth certificate, which is accepted by the authorities of another state only in its own official language and with an apostille. this means that eu citizens can expect at least one translation and apostille cost. and i could go on and on. we're used to these circumstantial procedures, we take them for granted. however, communication between member states' authorities could be simplified to save considerable time and money. in this context, i welcome the commission's idea of simplifying the acceptance of public documents within the eu, which is intended to facilitate the free movement of citizens and businesses. i believe that parliament's work has adequately complemented this original proposal. as a shadow rapporteur, i considered it important to strike a balance between further facilitating the free movement of eu citizens within the framework of the regulation and legal certainty and mutual trust between member states. i feel that we have achieved this and that the report is balanced in this respect. eu cooperation is steadily strengthening. therefore, cooperation between the authorities of the member states on the basis of mutual trust in the adoption of notified public documents is also desirable at this level. thanks to the multilingual forms in the regulation, among others the above example will soon be a thing of the past. i hope that, once the regulation is adopted as soon as possible, the commission will also look at the inclusion of names in public documents in the appropriate minority language. the single system would reduce fraud rather than increase it, and that is also an important aspect, so i would certainly recommend that we support it.
for the efd group. first of all, i would like to commend the commission's efforts to introduce multilingual forms for the most frequently reported facts in citizens' contacts with the authorities. such forms, which are recognised throughout the union, can help our citizens to declare their name, origin, marital status, residence, nationality, etc. in other union countries without any additional translation and verification. of course, we must be careful when introducing multilingual forms and we will need to find effective mechanisms to review them in the event of suspected falsification. the templates proposed in the annexes seem to me to be rather opaque. it might be worthwhile to put all the language mutations directly in the box where the entry is made. i would also suggest that we test the use of such forms directly in the european parliament. in principle, forms are available in english or french at the workplace where visits are accepted. a regular visitor coming from different countries of the union is not required to know other languages and would certainly appreciate if the information on the form in which he is to enter his data was also in his mother tongue. the union has 24 official languages, which should be equal, and it is the european institutions that should be the model for the correct application of multilingualism. unfortunately, the european parliament is the most visible example of the violation of language equality in the european union. i will give you an example of the work of some committees. for example, the petitions committee often discusses topics of interest to citizens from many countries around the world. however, interpretation for internet broadcasts of these committees' proceedings is only provided in 12 languages. most eu countries therefore do not have a secure translation of negotiations for example on human rights violations or violations of other economic activities. for example, a petition was discussed on the cyanide gold mining method in romania, where citizens from my country wanted to know what the defence mechanisms are from the point of view of the citizens against the use of this cyanide in real mining, and they could not learn from the petition committee meeting what to do because the translation into slovak was not available. similar problems can be seen in several countries of the union. multilingualism must therefore be respected and fulfilled in the institutions of the european union.
the joint debate will assess how the eu institutions and member states are delivering on eu legislation and its implementation. as the guardian of the treaties, the commission has the data that summarises the infringements of eu law by member states, but unfortunately - and this report highlights this - not every case of infringement of eu law is known to the commission. it does not have sufficient means to detect them, so the commission's data do not give an accurate picture of the actual shortcomings. that is why i consider the role of parliament to be important in detecting cases of infringement and in fighting for the correct application of eu law. we, the representatives, deal with a lot of different kinds of complaints, requests. one example is the work of the committee on petitions. the volume of petitions shows that member states are not transposing or applying eu law properly. improving the quality of legislation can also have a positive impact on the application of the law. i agree that the crisis-hit member states, and their authorities, are in a difficult situation. the same applies to their citizens and small and medium-sized enterprises. we cannot burden them with more complex and costly legislation, and therefore the legislator has a great responsibility in the eu. only legislation that is simple and easy to apply and therefore effective should be proposed. the dumping of meaningless legislation is creating a negative feeling among eu citizens and that is not our aim, but a better, more understandable and functioning europe. by improving the conditions and means, we will contribute to enhancing the democratic legitimacy of the eu and, in my view, save considerable funds.
last week, i had the honour of leading a delegation of young european leaders to russia. we have had several meetings in the past with our colleagues from the russian federation. i can tell you it wasn't quite the cold weather that was in moscow, but i really felt that our relations were starting to cool off in a way. i think that as the european union we should realize that we cannot orient our policy only towards the west and consider the united states as our great model, but we must realize that we have a closer neighbour, which is the russian federation, which will always be our neighbour and from which the sea does not separate us. we must therefore try to improve these relations with the russian federation. perhaps it is very good to see this in the conflict that is going on in ukraine today. it's a meeting of our positions that we would like to defend in this policy, but our common solution should really be a common solution that would also bring good to ukraine. i think that young people both in the european union and in russia see their common future as a positive thing and want to work on it. they want it to be good. so we as politicians really should try to understand each other and understand that we really should be good neighbors for a better future for all of us.
my colleagues may recall the politically motivated amendment to the law banning dual citizenship in slovakia, which was swiftly adopted in 2010. this amendment caused unpleasant administrative experience for dozens of slovak citizens, not to mention the ongoing harassment by the authorities. according to the slovak constitution, no one may be deprived of his citizenship against his will. the case was heard by the constitutional court in recent days, with a decision date set for february 26. the issue is being followed with great interest by the public, which is why we were shocked to hear the news the other day that the constitutional court had postponed its decision on dual citizenship indefinitely. it's an election year in slovakia. there is no question that we are witnessing a political game and we are also citizens who are being harassed. it would be good if the constitutional court would finally uphold the civil rights guaranteed by the slovak constitution.
