in writing the quality and efficiency of the judicial system are important factors in the creation of a modern democratic society. national justice systems play an important role in restoring confidence and returning to growth. i therefore welcome the ec's comparative overview, which notes improvements in all the areas of national justice systems mentioned. i consider it very important to ensure that disputes are resolved within a reasonable time in the future, as delays in proceedings are a serious problem for the citizens of the member states and they consequently lose confidence in the functioning of the judicial system and legal certainty. it should also be borne in mind that the more efficient the judicial system, the more attractive the member states are to foreign investors, which is a significant factor in times of social and financial crisis. every citizen of the european union must have easy access to an efficient, timely and independent judicial system. i consider that the comparative overview in question is a very valuable tool for identifying the difficulties faced by the member states. the court of justice has also been called upon to provide a legal basis for the implementation of the european convention on human rights, which was adopted by the council on 25 june 2005. i believe that this survey is a success and will be continued in the future, taking into account the equal treatment of member states, but also the objectivity and comparability of data.
intelligent energy systems bring opportunities for the regions. in particular, for the marginal and disadvantaged to not only reduce their energy costs but also to become producers and exporters of energy. i therefore fully support the decentralised implementation of energy strategies with active support from the structural funds and full use of public-private partnerships. local innovation takes account of local ecological and economic specificities, brings jobs to the region and increases its competitiveness. however, the challenges remain in storage, distribution, interoperability of technologies and existing networks, and exchanges with neighbouring countries. member states should take account of the benefits of such cross-border cooperation, in particular by aligning existing legislation in a favourable way, thereby facilitating access for their regions to mutually beneficial energy partnerships.
the proposal for a directive on criminal sanctions for insider dealing and market manipulation is intended to improve the mechanisms of directive 2003/6/ec on market abuse, as recommended by the financial supervisory panel. the european commission's analyses have shown that the sanction mechanisms of this directive are ineffective. it is shown that the criminal liability of legal persons for market abuse must be part of the sanctioning systems of all member states. given that the definition of criminal offences, insider dealing and market manipulation differs between member states, it would be appropriate to find a common definition of both administrative and criminal offences so that market participants know in advance clearly which actions they are liable to be criminally liable for. it would also be good, given the diversity of current national systems, to set at least a lower limit on the maximum penalty, so that perpetrators of these offences do not escape punishment by seeking a more appropriate court for their responsibility. the millions of dollars in losses we are incurring from speculative derivatives trading must be stopped as quickly as possible.
in writing, the european union is committed to respecting and improving the kimberley process. this is a process designed to prevent the inflow of controversial diamonds rough diamonds used by rebel movements to finance wars against legitimate governments. diversification of the economy and a focus on increasing mineral extraction are important for greenland. the european commission wants to achieve greenland's participation in the kimberley process by establishing a special regime for the movement of rough diamonds between the european union and greenland and adopting a set of amendments to make the territory of greenland and the territory of the european union considered as one entity without internal borders. the accession of greenland as an overseas country will make it possible to improve the efficiency of the already successful process of combating the disputed diamonds.
in writing - the single area for payments in euro, which does not distinguish between cross-border and national payments in euro, was to be implemented by 1 february 2014. the main objective of the regulation is to move from national credit and debit schemes to harmonised systems. the european commission and the european central bank have closely monitored the sepa transition. despite the considerable efforts of member states and the ecb, it is highly unlikely that all market participants will be able to meet the requirements on 1 february 2014. the priority is to avoid disruption of payments resulting from the fact that the full sepa switchover would not take place by 1 february 2014. payment service providers will be allowed to process euro payment transactions in formats other than those of the harmonised systems until 1 august 2014.
in writing - each year, some 24 000 people die in the fishing sector. the 2012 cape town agreement should allow for the implementation of the 1977 torremolinos international convention for the safety of fishing vessels, signed in 1977, and its 1993 protocol, which never entered into force due to the lack of ratification by a sufficient number of states. this convention lays down safety standards, but also specific requirements for marine fishing vessels of 24 metres in length and above. the cape town agreement should contribute to the safety of fishing vessels of 24 metres in length and above, but also create the conditions to address the problem of fishing vessels of less than 24 metres in length, which make up the majority of fishing vessels.
i did not support the resolution for a very simple reason. the principle of subsidiarity is being less and less respected by this parliamentary assembly. the european parliament questions its recognition, even when it is about the application of community law. it's obvious, and i'm sorry. i have no doubt that the application of eu law is important, but this resolution stresses above all that our member states are not entitled to legislate without community law. this will be another signal for the european parliament elections. i propose to set up training seminars to better understand eu decision-making in favour of national parliaments so that the principle of subsidiarity is fully implemented.
the credibility of the european union is directly proportional to the credibility of the application of its law in the member states and by all its institutions and bodies. compliance with the law contributes to legal certainty for citizens and businesses and creates a favourable environment for the development of individual rights and prosperity in the internal market. as the democratically elected institution closest to the citizen, the european parliament must be concerned that the law it adopts is actually applied in practice. there is no real point in passing laws on paper, producing regulations just to create the appearance of a procedure. citizens deserve a more efficient, functioning legal system without unnecessary obstacles, complications and delays. the european parliament must therefore be informed of the shortcomings so that appropriate measures can be readily incorporated into the legislation being prepared, and i also stress the absolutely enduring and imperative principle of subsidiarity and its respect where necessary.
in writing - what ? compliance with european union law should be a priority, to be implemented in close cooperation with parliament. the european parliament must monitor the political accountability of the european commission and improve its legislative work. the european parliament must exercise its powers of scrutiny more fully. a major problem is the marked increase in cases of late transpositions by member states, which rose by up to 60% compared with the previous year. petitions submitted by citizens point to fundamental breaches of european union law, in particular in the areas of fundamental rights, the environment, the internal market and property rights. the eu pilot platform, which serves as a forum for discussion between the european commission and the member state concerned, does not have legal personality and sufficient legal status. the european parliament must in future be given access to the database containing all complaints in order to strengthen its powers of control.
in writing - one of the fundamental pillars of the european union is the free movement of persons. simplifying the movement of european union citizens by removing various administrative barriers and increasing the chance of better employment in a foreign member state is a priority for the european commission. studies show that 12 million european union citizens live in another member state for study or work. it is much more difficult for these people to cope with the administrative burden of local government in the form of a certified translation or an apostille. the european commission is also considering abolishing the certified copy, which is currently considered a formality because it is quite time-consuming and costly. legal certainty is losing its relevance as a result of modern technologies and a certified copy no longer represents absolute protection against the risk of falsification of documents. the verification of copies may also be replaced by other procedures in case of doubt, such as the possibility for the office to request the production of the original or to apply to the mutual assistance institute. simplifying the receipt of authentic instruments significantly reduces red tape and charges for citizens.
in writing - today, more than 14 million eu citizens live in another member state, and the right to move and reside freely within the eu is therefore one of the most cherished by europeans. there are many reasons why eu citizens move between member states, but the most common reason is for work reasons, which bring benefits not only to individual citizens but also to the economies of the member states. unfortunately, it is quite common to hear individuals and business representatives point to the overly complex framework of public documents in the eu, the lengthy and unclear administrative procedures that stand in the way of exercising their rights of free movement and the possibility of taking up employment opportunities in other member states. i supported this latest report because i think it contains measures that could be a tool to remove these obstacles and could be a sufficient signal to citizens and businesses that the eu is really working to put citizens at the centre of european policies. i believe that this report is a step in the right direction, so that we no longer have to say that despite high unemployment, some 2 million jobs remain unfilled in the eu.
written - european union citizens enjoy a high standard of health protection and are adequately informed about the classification and labelling of chemicals. in order to ensure the protection and safety of workers, it is necessary to align regulation (ec) no 1282/2008 of the european parliament and of the council with the ghs. this system was developed by the un to identify hazardous chemicals and inform users of the associated risks through symbols and labelling of packaging. the adoption of that regulation automatically implies the need to make technical adaptations to the relevant directives, which contain references to the european union legislation on the classification and labelling of chemicals.
in writing - the european commission has been interested in the harmonisation of collective rights management since 1995. the european parliament repeatedly requested the adoption of a draft binding legal instrument in this area between 2004 and 2008. the current legal framework lags behind the dynamic digital society. creating a level playing field for governments by introducing governance and transparency standards that apply to all companies will facilitate access to online music. reform of collective rights management is the most effective way to improve and benefit rights holders.
in writing - since 2003, the european union has been working to establish a comprehensive framework to tackle insider dealing and market manipulation. the european commission is making use for the first time of new powers in this area, which allow it to adapt to the objective of effective implementation of harmonisation measures in criminal law. the sanctions applied in the past have proven insufficient in the fight against market abuse offences. harmonisation of the lower limit of the minimum sentence should be sufficiently dissuasive. large-scale criminals often seek the most favourable court of jurisdiction in the member state where the definition of the offence of insider dealing is associated with the lowest sanction. it is the level and form of sanctions that have the most serious impact on deterring potential offenders from committing a crime.
in writing - what ? an effectively functioning financial market relies on the trust of investors, businesses and the public and therefore requires a high degree of integrity. financial markets have become a normal part of everyday economic life and are now closely linked to the sound functioning of national economies. economic growth or far-reaching economic difficulties depend on them if these markets are manipulated or abused. the experience of recent years has clearly shown that the professional ethics of financial market participants cannot be relied upon alone. billions of dollars in damages caused by the state, businesses and the public are reason enough for the strictest legal regulation. i am convinced that establishing the factual basis for market manipulation offences and thus criminalising the perpetrators is the only effective way to combat such harmful conduct effectively. even high administrative fines are not enough for white-collar offenders. they shall be subject only to a criminal sanction, whether preventive or punitive, in the form of a custodial sentence.
in writing - the original regulation was annulled following a call by the european parliament, as the court concluded that the regulation in question had been adopted by consultation and not by co-decision. the new proposal is almost identical to the previous one, but contains the necessary adjustments to reflect the new legislative process and incorporates the european parliament's proposals from the 2010 consultation procedure. however, the new regulation maintained the obligation for member states to report data and information on investment projects relating to the production, storage and transport of oil, natural gas and electricity every two years. the collection of information and data is of great importance to the european commission in the compilation of inter-sectoral analysis and in the performance of its energy tasks. this regulation, together with the connecting europe facility, aims to complete the internal energy market.
in writing. - the new regulation is the same as the one that was annulled, but with some amendments that were necessary in any event as a result of the new legislative process. i voted in favour of the report to implement these amendments. member states shall communicate to the commission every two years data and information on investment projects relating to the production, storage and transport of oil, natural gas and electricity. the aim is to ensure that the commission receives accurate and regular information on investment projects in the european union that affect energy infrastructure in order to fulfil its energy tasks. the commission shall, every two years, carry out a cross-sectoral analysis of the structural evolution and prospects of the union's energy system. this will allow the identification of future supply and demand gaps and obstacles to investment. this will increase the transparency of expected demand and available supply. other important amendments to the draft report include the extension of the scope of the regulation to coal, the reporting of infrastructure investments in third countries that have an impact on the energy policy of the union, the imposition of reporting obligations on capacities for import and export of compressed natural gas and liquefied natural gas, the reduction of the threshold for data reporting for onshore wind capacity, the extension of the reporting obligation to capacities related to electricity storage facilities. it is hoped that the new regulation will improve the processes for the above information and analysis.
i respect my british colleague, sayyad karim, and i supported his report. i agree that the european union gains nothing if the legislative burden is too great. a less restrictive europe should be the motto for economic recovery. in slovakia, as elsewhere, small and medium-sized enterprises, especially family businesses, are the engine of the economy. it is hardly acceptable to me to curb business enthusiasm with excessive eu regulation. finally, the principle of subsidiarity must also apply where this principle necessarily leads to a decline in the importance of the european institutions compared with national institutions, which are much closer to the citizen. better regulation is less regulation, and that is why i welcome this report.
the european institutions have a legal obligation to make regulations only in accordance with the treaties and the fundamental principles of european union law, while ensuring that they are proportionate, clear and do not create an unnecessary administrative burden for citizens and businesses. the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality in the development of legislation aim to ensure that decisions are taken as closely as possible to the citizen, thus avoiding a centralised management from brussels that is undemocratic, grossly ignores national or regional specificities, or imposes disproportionate and unwelcome obligations. unfortunately, the commission does not sufficiently address such a requirement in its impact assessment. i therefore support the proposal to establish appropriate criteria for evaluation, respect for the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality, and i consider it very desirable to remind the legislator himself, and thus the european parliament and its committees, of this.
in writing - what ? in order to reduce regulatory and administrative burdens in the european union and the member states, it is necessary to take account of the new legislative environment. the renegotiation of the interinstitutional agreement should lead to an agreement which should be binding and in accordance with article 295 tfeu, which was introduced by the treaty of lisbon. the european union is facing a decline in the level of democracy and therefore a broad debate on the development of the powers of national parliaments must take place. the institution of a stronger red card, which was initiated by only a few national parliaments not on the basis of subsidiarity but of absolute control, is tailored to the conservative governments in europe and is not a step in the right direction. the conformity of european union legislation and the ten regulations of the european union represent progress in the better regulation agenda and take account of several previous requests from the european parliament in the past.

in writing - the actions taken by the european commission to ensure equality on the basis of sexual orientation and gender identity are insufficient. neither european nor national legislation provides adequate protection to the lgbt community. transgender people face discrimination in the labour market, but also outside it. the lack of engagement of gender equality organisations in the member states creates fertile ground for the inclusion of the lgbt community in society. the poor equipment and the minimal information lead to the fact that the persons concerned do not trust in the positive handling of their complaints. member states shall ensure that gender equality organisations have the appropriate equipment and information necessary to deal with complaints from the citizens concerned. it is important to create a safe and free environment for all people, regardless of gender identity or sexual orientation. the strategic data indicate the need for a plan to combat homophobia and discrimination on grounds of sexual and gender identity.
in writing - the directive defines unfair commercial practices that are prohibited in the european union and integrates provisions from the directive on misleading advertising and the directive on distance contracts. the main objective of this directive is therefore to protect the economic interests of consumers when they enter into commercial transactions. the directive has a positive impact on consumers and traders entering into transactions within the internal market. the european commission must now monitor the application of the directive by the member states and, if the member states do not comply with their obligations and apply the directive correctly, it must act and apply appropriate sanctions. a bad practice can never bring profit and the penalties must always be greater than the possible profit. in the interest of legal certainty, consumers need quick, effective and inexpensive means of redress. consumers must assert their rights fully and without fear of appropriate compensation.
in writing - over the past decades, the eu has adopted many rules to protect consumers, setting the framework for fighting unfair trading practices. i believe that despite the clear obligation of member states to effectively implement the directive into national legislation, there are still many cases where citizens have become victims of unfair commercial practices through the incorrect introduction or application of key provisions. i am convinced that full harmonisation of the current unfair commercial practices directive is essential if we are to achieve the positive effects of a single set of rules in the internal market within the eu. i supported the report because it proposes to remove the barriers that prevent consumers from having easy, legal and affordable access to the products and services they want anywhere in the eu. i expect that, in the interests of consumer protection, the commission and the member states will ensure better and more effective information for businesses so as to maintain a high level of consumer protection.

in writing - what ? in the context of the emerging crisis, i would like to emphasise that eu justice policy has undergone fundamental changes in recent times, and that this has also strengthened the foundations of a genuine european area of freedom, security and justice, serving above all european citizens. unfortunately, despite the positive progress made in the area of justice, i have to say that in some member states there are still problems with the duration of judicial proceedings and the organisation of the judiciary. i believe that these shortcomings cannot be a matter for the particular member state concerned but must become part of eu policy as a whole, as they can affect not only the functioning of the single market but also the whole eu legal system. i voted in favour of this current report because i believe that the eu justice scoreboard is the right step and can play a key role in restoring confidence, economic growth and job creation in this reform period. i also supported the report because i consider comparing and exchanging best practices in the implementation of provisions on an effective justice system to be a good non-binding tool that can not only help to identify problems but can also help member states and eu institutions to define better justice policies.

in writing - what ? energy consumption and production vary across the different regions of the european union. there are opportunities for geographical and territorial transformation of the energy network for disadvantaged regions. these regions will in future move from being energy consumers to being producers. the member states concerned shall invest in smart local networks with the assistance of the european structural funds. this is a long and difficult process requiring a stable policy framework. the european commission's role is to ensure ambitious targets for 2030 in the field of energy efficiency and renewable sources. citizens of the regions must be involved in all phases of planning. at the same time, there is a chance to transform the poor districts on the periphery of the member states into regions symbolizing stable and secure energy supplies and symbolizing sustainability. communication between local authorities, businesses, industry and the local community and citizens will be needed to achieve this. joint planning and implementation efforts in a process involving all actors create synergies and deliver better long-term benefits for the regions, not only in terms of security and accessibility of energy production and supply, but also in terms of economic, social and environmental benefits.
in writing - over the last period, many european regions have managed to invest more than €5.5 billion in some 300 smart grid projects, offering opportunities for individual member states to boost innovation, research and development, create new jobs and improve the competitiveness of european industry at local and regional level. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that the lack of energy infrastructure and the dependence of regions on existing networks oblige us to look for new opportunities for innovative digital technologies. i believe that the current rise in energy prices, climate change and socio-economic problems should also force eu member states to look for ways of providing a stable and secure energy supply that is of interest to the citizen not only in terms of its availability but also in terms of meeting the most stringent environmental protection criteria. i expect that the integration of smart grids into regional energy plans will enable citizens not only to participate actively in the energy planning of the networks, but also to benefit from the financial and social benefits.

written - small farms are exposed to a strong competitive environment in times of economic crisis and need help to increase competitiveness and performance. according to some statistics, small farms account for more than half of the agricultural holdings in the european union. in some of the new and southern member states, they are even in the overwhelming majority. the owners of these businesses are mostly old people who have trouble finding successors in running the farm. it is precisely in the context of the generational change that support should be given to these undertakings in order to enable the owners of agricultural undertakings to continue to operate in this sector until the end of their productive life. small farms in the future must develop by expanding their area and increasing production, distributing income resources more efficiently, increasing attractiveness for the next generations. the european union's cohesion policy, which calls for the financing of the necessary infrastructure in rural areas, plays an important role in addressing the problems of small farmers.
in writing. - the economic crisis has caused a lot of job losses, and the unemployment rate is rising. as a result, many choose to return to rural life, where people can work and earn a living within the agricultural sector. this also shows that small farms are an important and solid foundation for rural areas. farming families pass on knowledge from generation to generation, as well as job opportunities that can be a secure source of livelihood. these forms of farming have their own traditions and customs. the sale of products on local and regional markets provides consumers with direct access to low-cost and fresh products. small farms play a key role in the development of the countryside. however, their effective operation requires development, for which there is no adequate support. it makes it difficult for them to get financial support. this is for a number of reasons, but the main one is that large firms are the ones who have the most equity capital needed to benefit from eu programmes. i agree that member states should develop appropriate proposals for the development of small farms. direct payments under the cap support mechanism should be reviewed in order to improve the situation of small farmers, which could provide them with a more effective opportunity for development. i supported the report.
in writing - what ? i voted for this current report because it introduces new forms of support for small farmers (smes) and also because it introduces some of the simplifications introduced by the cap reform. as i consider agriculture to be an integral part of the eu's european economy, i expect the cap reform to continue in a way that makes the cap more competitive and flexible than in the past. i am fully aware that the kics do not have a stronger impact on the market, but i am nevertheless convinced that if we are to stop the continuing depopulation of the countryside and ensure its attractiveness, it is necessary to improve the so-called soft factors of the quality of life in the localities concerned by offering young families, for example, housing, education, leisure and culture, etc. having lived in the countryside for a long time and still returning to the countryside often, i have personal experience of how the countryside has changed in recent times. i can openly state that there has been a significant decline in agriculture, which was the main source of livelihood for many rural families, which has had and still has an impact on the quality of life of the rural citizen, especially in terms of unemployment, migration to work outside the region, mass abandonment of small livestock farming and the cultivation of specific local crops.
in writing - what ? fragmentation of media and significant shortcomings in safeguards for cross-border deliveries undermine consumer confidence in national and cross-border e-commerce. there are concerns that the internal market is not sufficiently developed for the needs of the current supply chain. a more appropriate supply system design should be able to address consumer information, protection and enforcement issues and create a level playing field for all retail operators in the european union. the economic importance of the internet economy is unquestionable. in 2012, the european union's e-commerce market grew by 20% to reach eur 250 billion. differences in delivery services between member states as well as between regions within a country are also an important factor, putting consumers living in less accessible and remote areas at a disadvantage. in order to improve conditions for consumers, it is essential that member states and the european commission ensure compliance with the postal services directive and the european union competition law and consumer rights directives.
in writing, the european coal and steel community was the birth of modern europe and one of the first steps on the road to european integration. the european steel industry is one of the building blocks for the automotive, construction, engineering and many other sectors. in the current period of global economic crisis, it is important to maintain a competitive steel industry, which is a guarantee of employment and economic growth. the steel industry today employs 350 000 people directly and several million in related sectors. to improve the current situation, it is necessary to reduce energy costs by supplying safe and sustainable energy, to reduce the environmental burden of the steel industry and to make high capital investments in the future, in particular in the upgrading of breakthrough technologies, which are however associated with significant risks and long-term returns.
in writing - the european steel industry is the world's second largest steel producer and directly employs some 350 000 workers. several million additional jobs are created by related sectors. the strategic importance of the steel industry lies in providing products for other sectors in which european companies are often world champions. i would remind you that even european energy and defence are directly dependent on the steel industry. the european union must take concrete steps to demonstrate its interest in maintaining european manufacturing, which remains key to european industry. in this context, i would highlight the fact that we have highly qualified and talented specialists and workers, given the long tradition of steelmaking in europe. this competitive advantage must not be lost by the lack of system solutions. i therefore fully support the use of european funds for research, development and skills development in the steel industry. the loss of high-quality expertise, know-how and competence is nothing but a path to the demise of the european steel industry. for the reasons i have given, i shall vote in favour of the report.
in writing - what ? the first step towards european integration was the coordination of coal production through the establishment of the european coal and steel community. in addition to this historical role of steel, the european steel industry forms the basis of key industrial value chains such as the automotive, engineering or electrical industries. the steel industry in europe provides 350 000 direct jobs and several million jobs for workers in related sectors, unfortunately as a result of the crisis, unemployment in this sector has increased significantly. this negative trend has been accompanied by a loss of skills in the workforce, which will not be easy to restore. the role of social dialogue involving workers is therefore very important in securing the future of the european steel industry. as well as the need for the workforce to be involved in the restructuring and innovation of this important sector. any future trade agreements should include measures to improve export opportunities and make access to european steel and its products more efficient. balanced and sustainable trade in the steel sector must be based on respect for workers' rights and environmental protection standards.
in writing - the steel industry in europe is currently in a very difficult situation, as evidenced by the significant decrease in production activity in recent years and the consequent loss of more than 40 000 jobs. i voted in favour of this current report because i am convinced that it is good news that can help the steel industry to overcome its difficulties and increase its competitiveness on world markets. as a slovakian, where the largest steel producer is u.s. steel košice, which employs over 13,000 workers, i know the problems of the steel industry. last year, an american investor was threatening to leave slovakia and sell the company. i am pleased that the representatives of the government of the slovak republic and the representatives of u.s. steel signed a memorandum of understanding during this period to keep the company in kosice for at least 5 years. i consider this agreement to be of particular importance, particularly because u.s. steel has committed to maintaining employment and not to engage in mass layoffs. i also want to point out that this company is not only involved in the production of steel, but also supports public benefit projects in health, education, financially supports science, culture and also charity.
in writing one of the necessary prerequisites for the efficient implementation of the necessary investments in low-carbon technologies and solutions, new production capacities, smart transport networks and transmission systems is the stability of the 2030 climate and energy framework. as regards the overall framework, it should be sufficiently flexible to take account of new knowledge and economic developments. while ensuring the transition to a low-carbon economy, the proposals should not impose a disproportionate burden on national economies. i therefore support a binding target for greenhouse gas emissions of 35% earlier than in 1990. the ets should play a central role, but the free allocation scheme would have to continue in order to protect businesses exposed to international competition. the final ghg target for member states shall be set in a manner reflecting national conditions and different starting situations. the role of nuclear energy as one of the options for reducing co2 emissions should be strengthened, as the elimination of carbon emissions will depend on the different share of low-carbon technologies in each member state. new financial instruments and mechanisms should be established for the implementation of the 2030 climate and energy package.
air transport has become more accessible to all passengers in the liberalised european market. however, the quality of the services provided has declined significantly and some companies have even resorted to unfair commercial practices to the detriment of passengers. the current legislation does not provide effective protection and legal certainty for passengers. the rules are considered to be unclear and so-called loopholes allow airlines to avoid their obligations or liability. for example, i consider it unjustified and outrageous to refuse to carry a passenger on a part of the journey on a return ticket simply because he has not travelled on all the sections of the journey for which the ticket was duly purchased. i support strengthening passenger rights, in particular by introducing an obligation for airlines to provide clear and timely information on passenger rights where and within what time limits they need to be exercised. i also welcome the idea of introducing a standardised eu-wide complaint form that is understandable in all languages.

in writing - many illegal immigrants who are in the european union illegally find themselves in a legal vacuum and cannot access basic services because they risk subsequent deportation. they also have difficulty getting basic health care or housing because they could be reported to the relevant immigration authorities. the situation of illegal immigrant women is even more serious, as they face double discrimination on grounds of gender and legal status. women are much more vulnerable to physical and sexual abuse and have difficulty accessing gender-specific healthcare. member states are challenged to recognise women illegal migrants as an explicitly vulnerable group exposed to trafficking in human beings, discrimination and exploitation in the labour market. member states are about to ratify the un international convention on the protection of the rights of all migrant workers and members of their families. migrants are held in detention centres in degrading conditions where they may suffer psychological trauma. in addition, irregular migrants are often subject to sexual and physical abuse.
in writing - what ? the first instrument to combat migration will target low-skilled and low-paid workers. the new directive is necessary because a large number of seasonal workers in the european union work in substandard conditions and are subject to discrimination by employers. a legal migration framework can bring positive results and limit illegal immigration and improve the labour market position of migrants. in the near future, an accelerated procedure for the recruitment of seasonal workers will be introduced, the situation of third-country nationals on the labour market will be improved and the awareness of seasonal workers will be improved so that they do not become victims of exploitation. the european commission's proposal sets a maximum length of stay for a seasonal worker at 6 months, a longer period could lead to abuse of the social system of the selected member state. the strengthening of sanctions against employers in the form of compensation for non-compliance is one of the new instruments for the protection of seasonal workers. the proposal also includes provisions on adequate accommodation, payment of reasonable costs or the introduction of checks to verify that employers are fulfilling their obligations towards seasonal workers.
in writing - the influx of seasonal workers from third countries is a common phenomenon for most member states of the european union. despite high unemployment in many member states, we still have a large number of jobs, sometimes in entire sectors, that european citizens are not interested in. a harmonised framework for managing such migration at eu level could therefore really bring added value. women, for example, are a particularly vulnerable group in this case, as they are the most frequent victims of exploitation or abuse. human trafficking, known as the white meat trade, is infamously the result of various types of abuse. enabling legal ways of migration can play an important role in curbing illegal immigration or the exploitation that often accompanies it. a framework for migration management at european level would be, inter alia, the first such instrument since the adoption of the treaty of lisbon. i therefore consider it very important that we succeed in ensuring fair conditions for third country workers and their protection against ill-treatment by employers. it is therefore necessary that designated third parties, such as trade unions or non-governmental organisations, have the possibility to lodge complaints on behalf of seasonal workers in order to ensure effective enforcement of the laws.
in writing, the european union is giving increased attention to preventive measures and creating a culture of prevention that raises citizens' awareness of natural and man-made risks. prevention is the most important factor in terms of protecting people. in the future, it is essential to intensify research into understanding and preventing environmental risks and to develop partnerships between insurance companies and research institutes in order to prepare citizens to face the risks associated with natural disasters. a higher degree of involvement of local authorities in decision-making on spatial planning and urban development will ensure improved management in the event of natural disasters. natural and man-made disasters require two different types of insurance and therefore two insurance markets must cover them. member states and regions within them are ultimately the ones to bear the costs of natural or man-made disasters and should therefore pay greater attention to risk prevention in order to minimise the consequences of disasters.
in writing - this agreement represents the first of a second generation of agreements between the european union and another country. following the conclusion of competition and cooperation agreements with the usa, canada, japan and south korea, the first of the second generation of agreements is between the european union and switzerland. unlike previous agreements, it contains new provisions on the exchange of evidence obtained by competition authorities during the investigation of the same case. this agreement is very important for the european union, as switzerland is the european union's third largest economic partner. in the event of a positive impact of the agreement in question, the european union will continue to conclude second generation agreements with other countries as well, which may be more complex than the agreement with switzerland due to different rules and procedures.
in writing - the proper functioning of the eu's internal market is based on the principles of competition, which are strictly monitored by the commission and the antitrust authorities of the member states, and are subject to heavy fines for infringements. to ensure a level playing field for our european businesses, we must also require the countries where our trading partners are located to respect certain minimum requirements. otherwise, european businesses and consumers will be disadvantaged and even harmed. i therefore advocate closer cooperation on competition, not only with the developed western countries but also with the developing countries. our priority should be particularly in fast-growing economies where competition rules are often poorly known or inconsistently applied and enforced. this situation is not always due solely to the reluctance of antitrust authorities to enforce the legislation in force but rather to a lack of experience in detecting cartels or abuses of a dominant position, prosecuting such acts and imposing appropriate sanctions.
in writing, the trade treaty is a step forward and aims to regulate the international trade in conventional weapons, which, unlike weapons of mass destruction and landmines, has not been the subject of any global legal instrument. despite its shortcomings, the treaty establishes common international standards to regulate international trade in conventional arms and in ammunition, parts and components of arms. it also aims to prevent and eliminate the illicit trade in these weapons. rapid ratification by member states is necessary for rapid ratification and implementation by all contracting parties. however, the member states cannot ratify this treaty separately, since it provides for import and export control measures which fall within the scope of the common commercial policy and thus within the exclusive competence of the european union.
the system of import duties must be continuously improved, but it must also be modernised, because the internal market in the european union must be protected from low-quality products from other countries, especially from asia. the impact on trade policy through anti-dumping and anti-subsidy rules must be such that the growing concerns of the european union producers are not justified. in order to ensure that threats of retaliation by third country producers following complaints of dumping or subsidisation are not real and that the union can protect our producers in a real way. the commission's proposed measure of partial repeal of the lesser duty rule may have an impact on retaliation and i think the rapporteur has rightly suggested that partial repeal of the lesser duty rule be dropped from the proposal. i have therefore decided to postpone the final vote and to leave enough room in the trilogue for negotiations.
in writing, the european union's trade defence system is based on world trade organisation rules and allows member states to tackle unfair practices, price dumping and subsidies by third countries, thereby maintaining a market balance and a level playing field for all participants. the european commission set itself the objective of modernising the current rules and introducing new amendments, in particular concerning the abolition of the lesser duty rule in anti-dumping proceedings where a violation is found in the area of raw materials, the reimbursement of duties collected during an expiry review and the prior notification of the imposition of provisional duties. the modernisation by the european commission aims to increase the effectiveness of the instruments, reduce the risk of retaliation by third countries and increase transparency. it is now the rule that trade is confronted with obstacles which are not even essentially trade related but which nevertheless affect trade policy.
in writing - the airline industry has experienced a steady increase in passenger numbers and flights over the last 50 years. however, this positive development brings with it problems caused by the unclear interpretation of regulations and the increasingly frequent violations of legislation by airlines to the detriment of passengers. the national authorities responsible for checks do not sufficiently enforce the rights and claims of passengers. passengers are exposed to legal uncertainty which makes them reluctant to go to court, given the complexity of the process and the high costs which airlines use as an alibi to avoid liability for inadequate service. the revised regulation clearly strengthens passengers' rights to assistance and compensation in the event of long delayed flights. the european commission is working to improve the situation of passengers who miss a connecting flight because of a change in flight plan or a delay. these passengers should be assisted and rerouted by the previous air carrier which caused the delay at the transfer point leading to the passenger missing his flight.
in writing - regulation 1346/2000 established a framework for crossborder insolvency proceedings. it determines which member state has jurisdiction to open insolvency proceedings, lays down uniform rules on the applicable law and provides for the recognition and enforcement of insolvency-related decisions and the coordination of secondary insolvency. experience has shown that this regulation is fulfilling its function satisfactorily, but that the application of some of its provisions needs to be improved. the draft report sets out the minimum criteria that the trustee's pledge to local creditors must meet in order to be enforceable and binding. the creation of a european union register, which would allow creditors and courts to determine whether bankruptcy proceedings have been opened in another member state, is also promising and would thus increase publicity and transparency. it will also be important to address the conduct of bankruptcy proceedings in the case of groups of companies.
