madam president, as this discussion is showing, we all agree that data on women and girls affected by fgm are more than shocking. it was mentioned that more than 140 million women have experienced this type of violence. the most affected countries are egypt, ethiopia, somalia and sudan, where prevalence rates can be as high as 98%. in other countries, such as nigeria, kenya, togo and senegal, the prevalence rate varies between 20% and 50%, but it is not present only in africa. fgm takes place in parts of the middle east too, for example in yemen, oman, iraqi kurdistan, and among some bedouin women in israel as well. statistics released recently by unicef, analysing data from 29 countries in africa where fgm is most prevalent, show that 18 of these countries are in west and central africa and that fgm affects one fifth of young girls in sub-saharan africa itself. bearing in mind that fgm is mostly carried out on young girls between infancy and the age of 15, we should speak about a serious breach of children’s rights as well as equally serious violence against girls and women. as a result of immigration and refugee flows, fgm is now being practised by ethnic minority populations in other parts of the world, including the european union. as mentioned, we have an estimated 500 000 women and girls at risk. ten member states have specific criminal law provisions on fgm, but on the basis of existing criminal codes it can be prosecuted as a criminal offence in all member states. in addition, all member states, in the process of prosecuting fgm, have recognised the legal principle of extraterritoriality. in certain member states, high prevalence was detected, for example in france, where the estimated number is more than 60 000, and in the uk, with more than 65 000. although, as mentioned, there is provision for prosecution in fgm cases, in the eu this is still rare, so what we need is law enforcement.
for the efd group the renewal of the inland transport scheme gives operators in the sector hope of obtaining the necessary support from the european union to increase the competitiveness of goods transport by waterways. the transport of goods by sea is energy efficient and it is therefore important to create the conditions for it to remain an integrated part of the supported transport system of the union. the continuation of the naiades inland waterway programme must be directed both at improving the infrastructure of inland waterways and at the technical modernisation of vessels providing transport services. inland waterways are part of six of the nine corridors of the ten-t core network and therefore the building of multimodal transport requires the creation of missing links and the use of the capacities of the connecting europe facility to address bottlenecks. it will also be important to promote the integration of waterborne transport information systems into the information flows of other modes of transport in order to effectively integrate shipping into the integrated inland multimodal transport system. as in the automotive sector, we must also support the introduction of modern technologies in the shipping industry in order to increase the environmental performance of shipping. the naiades programme, in my view, can be of great help in revitalizing the waterway transport of goods within the european union.
i supported the proposal to establish common rules for the system of compensation and assistance to passengers in the event of denied boarding and other compensation, because it is a good measure to protect citizens whose only means of transport is often the airplane. during my five years in the european parliament, i spent a lot of time at airports. i mean, i've had my own experience with flight delays and cancellations, with hand luggage problems that were just airport purchases. i often had to claim compensation for cancelled flights, and it was not always easy. i therefore welcomed the fact that it was specified which of the circumstances of the cancellation of flights were exceptional and which were such as to be the responsibility of the carrier and to compensate the passengers for the costs. however, i think it is very important that the travelling public is informed about the measures taken in the regulation so that they can apply them in their daily practice.
in writing - the aviation industry has undergone major changes in recent years. this is mainly due to the increase in the number of passengers and flights on the european single market. however, this positive development has been and is still to some extent accompanied by the application of unfair practices and a reduction in the quality of services with negative consequences for passengers. the most prominent example of such behaviour, in my view, is the behaviour of some low-cost companies operating on the european market. although their practices may appear to be fine, so to speak, in accordance with regulations and rules, closer examination and, especially, personal experience, reveal that this is not so. the way in which services are sold is often opaque and purposefully misleading, which complicates the exercise of passenger rights. the inconsistent application of the current regulation by both carriers and national authorities is thus causing confusion not only for passengers but also for the whole industry. i agree with the rapporteur that one of the main problems is that carriers do not automatically recognise the rights of the passenger concerned. the commission notes that the airport services agreement between ryanair and ams was signed on 20 june 2010 and that the agreement between ryanair and ams was signed on 1 july 2010. i therefore regard the main priorities as being to increase transparency, improve access to information and improve enforcement of rights with the help of national authorities.
in writing, the european union and other major greenhouse gas emitters have focused their efforts on reducing emissions to the upper 80-95% limit by 2050. according to the ipcc report, the timeframe for global action is fast approaching its end and inaction by individual states could reverse recent progress towards the mdgs. there is a real threat of a 10 to 20 percent increase in global hunger, which would hit the poorest regions and countries of the planet hard. member states of the european union must reduce their greenhouse gas emissions to at least 50% below 1990 levels by 2030. by 2020, member states must make every effort to increase investment in climate architecture to help less developed countries achieve ambitious goals. the european union should continue to reduce emissions and create new jobs in the environmental industries.
in writing - the syrian conflict has devastated the country and the humanitarian and social situation in syria is catastrophic. violence is escalating, and the number of victims is constantly increasing. monitoring the number of dead and wounded is very difficult due to the lack of access to the area due to the conflict. the regime of president assad has committed widespread human rights violations and groups supporting him have been terrorising the region. according to the office of the united nations high commissioner for refugees, massacres and unlawful killings, arrests, executions of prisoners, and systematic torture are taking place. the conflict in syria has resulted in the flight of 2,63 million citizens to neighbouring countries. one of the largest refugee exodus in modern history is putting pressure on surrounding countries and their economies and destabilising the region. the health system in syria is on the verge of collapse due to the kidnapping, torture and killing of doctors, nurses and paramedics. the regime continues to demonstrate its use of barrel bombs and chemical weapons against civilians. the situation in syria is destabilising the political situation in the region and threatens to spread the conflict to lebanon and iraq.
in writing - the civil war or crisis in syria has been ongoing since 2011. the conflict continues to take a toll on the lives of syrians, with the death toll exceeding 100,000. human rights are violated constantly, whether by government troops or by insurgents. unicef found that more than five and a half million children have been affected by the conflict, of which more than four million are in syria and more than one million are in neighboring countries. as for children, the surrounding countries have nearly 8,000 refugees. un reports prove that chemical weapons have been used against civilians including children. last year's un resolution banning the use, development, acquisition and stockpiling of chemical weapons was adopted unanimously. chemical weapons, however, are only the tip of the iceberg, since the vast majority of deaths have been caused by conventional weapons. the biggest problem with the way out of this crisis is the lack of an alternative to the current dictatorial regime. it is estimated that over a thousand different factions may be fighting against the current regime, with the risk of radicalisation increasing. available information indicates a possible 'force' of up to ten thousand foreign individuals who have joined the many factions of the fragmented opposition in order to advance the agenda of radical islam.
in writing, the new egyptian constitution was approved on 1 december 2013 and a referendum on its adoption was held in mid-january 2014, when it was overwhelmingly approved by valid votes. during the referendum, there were several clashes between supporters and opponents of ousted president morsi. over the past six months, over a thousand protesters have been killed by security forces. egyptian civil society is currently undergoing major political and social changes, with a free and independent press and media coming to the fore. tensions between muslims and coptic christians have escalated since president morsi was ousted from power in the country, and the process of church demolitions continues. former president morsi faces four different criminal cases, three of which relate to his term as president. the egyptian economy is experiencing a period of rising unemployment and a significant increase in poverty in the country. the level and scope of the european union's engagement in egypt depends on the fulfilment of its commitments in the areas of democracy, the rule of law, human rights and gender equality.
in writing. - russia is one of the european union's most important strategic partners. on 28 january 2014, a summit took place, which provided an opportunity to improve relations and discuss regional economic integration, the common neighbourhood and other key foreign policy issues. the european union encourages russia to fulfil its obligations as a member of the world trade organisation and to remove trade barriers such as motor vehicle recycling fees. cooperation between the european union and russia in the field of research and development is at a high level. cooperation in the field of energy is very important for both partners and russia should not use the supply of natural resources as a political tool. in russia, problems with the independence of the judiciary and the deteriorating situation of civil society, political opposition, human rights defenders and the lgbt community continue. no progress has been made in the negotiations on a new agreement to replace the current partnership and cooperation agreement.
in writing - what ? during my visit to moscow last week, i was able to see once again what topics the russians were most interested in in relation to the european union. not much has changed, but rather, to what's left, topics like ukraine and syria have been added. the most important issue in russia, in my opinion, is the issue of security. the two terrorist attacks in late december that shook the country and the international community were compounded by the approaching sochi olympics. the topic of the crisis in ukraine attracted the most attention. the eu-russia summit was similarly reduced from two days of talks to a few hours. some european politicians also stirred up enthusiasm by taking part in pro-european demonstrations in kiev. the demonstrations in ukraine began in november after president yanukovych announced that he would not sign trade and political agreements with the european union. in confronting my russian partners, i held to the values and opinions that i share with my s-d colleagues. first and foremost, it is about recognising the importance of the eu-russia partnership, which is strategically important because of the interconnectedness of the two actors. it is also unacceptable that the creation of political pressure creates instability, as was the case in ukraine and therefore we must not allow the emergence of a politics of blocs competing for spheres of influence, but instead find common interests in the management of a stable neighbourhood.
in writing - what ? the country lacks cooperation and dialogue among the highest ethnic representatives. civil society has been very little involved in efforts to reform the country, overcome existing disputes and improve the lives of the citizens of bosnia and herzegovina. the country must undertake a key constitutional reform in the near future to kick-start the transition to a fully functioning rule of law. the country's accession process to the european union remains unobserved due to dysfunctional institutions, a democratic deficit and the continuing failure of the competent authorities to meet the requirements for the entry into force of the stabilisation and association agreement. the ongoing dispute between the political leaders is affecting, among others, the implementation of the judgment of the european court of human rights, which could lead to the question of the legality of the 2014 elections. the european commission has cancelled all pre-accession aid projects due to the country's ongoing political disputes, and the reallocation of money for socioeconomic development remains in question.
in writing - the non-reopening of accession negotiations with macedonia is increasing public opinion frustration in the country and increasing internal tensions. the european commission will have to take all the necessary steps to ensure that the decision to open accession negotiations is taken during the current greek presidency. the delay in the opening of accession negotiations represents a steadily increasing financial cost for the country and a further deterioration of the economic situation in the region. the authorities in macedonia must accelerate the adoption of reforms and strengthen the rule of law, judicial and public administration reforms, as well as improve inter-ethnic cohesion. the government must continue reforms in public spending and public procurement to improve the quality of public administration.
in writing, macedonia made progress in several areas in 2013. reducing unemployment and increasing gdp, but the country still has difficulties in addressing the problems of the minorities living on its territory, in particular the albanian and roma minorities. however, we believe that the accession negotiations can accelerate the last necessary reforms in the country, which are necessary for its membership. these are mainly reforms in the judiciary, public administration and also human rights issues. we call for better neighbourly relations, in particular with bulgaria and greece. if accession negotiations do not take place, the country risks incurring unexpected costs, which will have a negative impact on regional stability. it could also cause deepening domestic problems and internal tension. accession negotiations should therefore serve as a stimulus for macedonia to accelerate the building of a strong democratic state that upholds european values, with the ultimate outcome being eu membership.
in writing, montenegro made progress towards integration into the european union in 2013. the implementation of concrete measures and effective reforms brings it closer to meeting all the requirements for formal membership in the accession negotiations. judicial reform and the fight against corruption and organised crime are part of the efforts to gradually build the rule of law. we also consider the strengthening of parliamentary powers and cultural and economic cooperation with neighbouring states, including kosovo, to be important steps. however, the country needs to complete electoral reforms and reforms of political party financing to ensure a higher level of voter confidence. we also consider it important to include representatives of civil society in the negotiating structures and to ensure that citizens are informed in good time by the government, which will result in increased transparency during the negotiations. concerns about the persistence of violence against women remain, and we also stress the special role of independent and sustainable public media. despite the shortcomings identified by the european union, montenegro has recently shown initiative to eliminate them as soon as possible and it is therefore realistic that accession negotiations will be resumed swiftly.
for the efd group the political situation in thailand is extremely tense. prime minister shinawatra's proposal to amnestize crimes committed by politicians and government officials after 2004 naturally provoked public anger. opposition leaders have taken the lead in confronting the dissatisfied citizens and have sharpened the atmosphere ahead of the parliamentary elections with radical statements. the state of emergency declared by the prime minister on 21 january has established absolute impunity for government institutions and officials involved in the enforcement of this decision. parliamentary elections are thus held in a situation where the exercisers of governmental power can, in the name of order, influence and change the electoral decisions of citizens in any way with impunity. to consider the parliamentary mandates that came out of these elections as legitimate would be a mockery of the thai people and democracy. however constructive our relationship with the administration of prime minister shinawatra, we must make it clear to her that a parliamentary body created by such violence and manipulation cannot be the basis for the establishment of a respected democratic government. on the other hand, the radical or monarchist opposition that is escalating political conflicts bears a large part of the responsibility for the rising tensions. the solution therefore appears to be coordinated international economic and political pressure on the representatives of the hostile parties to abandon confrontational procedures and, in their own interest and in the interest of the country, to let their citizens decide peacefully and freely on the future government.
it is well known that organised crime and illegal businesses generate huge profits every year, which are subsequently legalised and invested in legal activities. i also consider the concealment of assets and their reinvestment in countries other than those where the crime was committed to be a serious problem, which weakens the ability of states to combat cross-border crime effectively and ultimately reduces tax revenues for national governments and the eu budget. i therefore consider it essential that an effective system for the freezing, administration and confiscation of criminal assets be established in all member states, with the necessary institutional framework and financial and human resources. i consider that the legislation of the eu and its member states governing the confiscation of property derived from criminal activity is underused and underdeveloped. i believe that confiscation of criminal assets is an effective tool in the fight against organised and serious crime and should be given higher priority in the future. i expect that the adoption of minimum rules will be consistent with member states' freezing and confiscation regimes and will strengthen mutual trust and increase the effectiveness of cross-border cooperation. the european union must send a clear signal and take action to combat organised crime on its territory.
thank you, reporter, for your excellent report. the emergence of voluntary humanitarian aid is of enormous importance for europe's perception in the world, but especially for the younger generation of europeans. they get the opportunity to go where their help is needed most and be personally responsible for the outcome. they gain valuable experience, not just in the workplace. but above all, the european union will give thousands of young people the opportunity to see for themselves what is really lacking. europe is obsessed with a mentality of entitlement, too often taking for granted things that are not so. to have food, a place to live, to be able to go to school. but also to rely on solidarity when we get sick, lose our job, or are prevented by old age from taking care of ourselves. thanks to the european voluntary service, we have a chance to change this mentality.
the proposal for a regulation establishing the european voluntary humanitarian aid corps is one of the concrete measures to strengthen the solidarity of european citizens with people in distress and need. it is well known that the union is the world's largest provider of humanitarian aid, but it is of the utmost importance to enable citizens of the european union to participate personally in humanitarian aid. direct aid also carries its own risks due to the exceptional natural or social conditions in the target countries and it is therefore necessary to create a secure framework for its effective and rapid delivery. the talent of volunteers, their skills and, above all, their goodwill must also be recognised by the fact that the conditions of volunteers are also addressed by the legislator in a legislative way, thus strengthening their position in the union's humanitarian aid.
i want to thank the three foreign ministers of france, germany and poland for their diplomatic engagement in kiev that helped bring an end to the violence. just as we mourned the deaths of innocent citizens of ukraine, we were filled with hope by the departure of president yanukovych. the tragic events have reminded us of the enormous importance of the european project. it has many flaws, but it protects us from dictators, from lawlessness, from violence, from chaos. ukraine has a place in the european area of freedom. but he has a difficult few months, perhaps years, ahead of him. the ukrainians must decide whether they want to continue as one state and whether they can respect european values. the eu stands ready to help ukraine, but only a ukraine that is democratic and fair to all its citizens, ukrainians as well as russians, tatars and jews, orthodox as well as catholics, evangelicals and atheists.
let me express my support for mrs honeyball and the text of the report which was adopted in committee. the term prostitution clearly includes trafficking in human beings. one man buys another man for his own appetite and pleasure. whether it is legal or forced prostitution, the principle is the same. treating human beings as commodities should be prohibited. i hope we can agree on that. i am aware that my position will not be accepted by colleagues whose member states have legalized prostitution. even the european court of justice has declared that prostitution is a profession like any other. i will not support the motion for an alternative resolution. i hope that we can get the honeyball report through, and as shadow rapporteur i will do everything to find a text that my group will support.
in writing. the eu must change. it's time to put the job first. today, 27 million europeans are unable to find a job, including almost a quarter of our young people. 120 million europeans live at or below the poverty line. creating jobs for young people is a challenge for the young generation of today and tomorrow, and it is also our long-term commitment. to create jobs, we need an ambitious industrial policy, investment in the social economy and support for small and medium-sized enterprises, which cannot do without access to external resources. we need strict rules in the social field: equal pay for equal work; protection of workers' rights; dignity of work. it's time to revive the economy. pure austerity has hurt our economies and the weakest have paid the price. we need to use innovation, science and research, education and sound industrial policy to create jobs and boost the economy. discoveries from european laboratories and universities will create more jobs for european workers. we need to coordinate economic and fiscal policies in the euro area and anticipate the possible social impacts. public finances must be tightly guarded, the ballast removed and investments made where the citizens benefit most. the fight against tax fraud and tax evasion is a crucial priority for achieving a fair tax system.
in writing. - what ? the economic and financial crisis has significantly undermined the financial stability and economic growth of the member states. the european union has provided external assistance through macroeconomic programmes, which currently cover cyprus, greece, ireland and portugal. the main objective of the aid is to ensure that the member states concerned have sufficient financial resources to implement their fisheries policy projects in the future. the extension of the application of the co-financing rates increased by [nbsp ]10[nbsp ]% is a necessary measure to stimulate the development of projects and investments in the fisheries sector and thus to promote the creation of new jobs in the various regions.
in writing. - rule 136 of the rules of procedure of the european parliament governs the provisions on the residence document. as it stands, it only distinguishes between members present and absent at a sitting on the basis of the member's signature on the attendance record. it does not therefore take account of justified absences authorised by the president and justified by the quaestors. the proposed amendment should be recorded in the minutes of the meeting and identify precisely those members who were excused after the submission of the relevant documents. the reason for the amendment of the rules of procedure and the provisions on the residence document is to limit the impact of residence documents on parliament's activity indicators in justified cases.
in writing. - what ? legislation adopted by the european union in 2009 set binding targets for reducing emissions from new cars. the main objective of the legislation is to improve the fuel consumption of new cars and to reduce co2 emissions to 130[nbsp ]g between 2012-2015 and[nbsp ]95[nbsp ]g/km by 2020 on the european market. car manufacturers are being encouraged to innovate and incorporate new green technologies and packages that reduce consumption and therefore co2 emissions. in the future, incentives should be created to facilitate the recognition of innovative technologies and a new global fuel consumption test cycle.
in writing. - what ? reducing co2 emissions from new cars through modern drive concepts will bring significant financial benefits, primarily for the consumer. the agreement between the european parliament and the council on co2 emissions reductions will also lead to a directly proportional gradual reduction in fuel consumption. on the other hand, it can be said to be a clear step forward in maintaining the competitiveness of the european automotive industry on the world market. the new eu strategy sets a binding target to reduce co2 emissions from 130g/km to 95g/km in the production of new cars by 2020. i believe that the final text agreed by the european parliament and the member states is an adequate compromise that will promote the production of low-emission vehicles. however, we must not forget that the use of new technologies must also take account of environmental protection. innovation not only moves the world, it also serves as a driving force for job creation. it is this aspect, which is also in line with the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy, that i considered the most important in my vote. the automotive industry has great potential for the future of the eu and the increase in employment of our citizens within the european union will bring us closer to achieving our personal and professional goals.
in writing. - what ? i consider the automotive and other motor vehicle industry to be the world's technological leader today, not only because it employs at least 8.5 million people worldwide, but also because of the significant investment in innovation that is taking place in this sector. this is also evidenced by the fact that many car manufacturers are already offering vehicles that are lighter, more fuel efficient, and lower in co2 emissions. despite this, we often find that cars and light commercial vehicles still produce around 15% of co2 emissions, which is not acceptable for the eu. i voted in favour of this report because it contains proposals and[nbsp]procedures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and increase energy efficiency in new passenger cars. i also supported the report because i believe that new technologies and improvements in fuel efficiency can have a positive impact on demand for components, which could ultimately also benefit employment.
the draft trade mark regulation is part of a long-standing package of measures to ensure faster and more efficient access for businesses to trade mark registration, thereby seeking to boost innovation and legal certainty. the modernisation of existing provisions in view of the dual system of trade marks in the eu appears necessary in view of the ambiguities and, in the new provisions, the complexity and time-consuming procedure, which is not in the interest of businesses or consumers. in that regard, i would point out that trade marks help consumers to avoid the counterfeiting of goods, which mainly come from outside the union, and to make an informed decision on the quality of the products when they make their purchases. for these reasons, we support the unification of the trade mark registration system, the introduction of common tools and the harmonisation of the overall trade mark framework.
in writing. - what ? the long-awaited reform of the trade mark system in europe comes at a time when european customs authorities are reporting massive intellectual property infringements, especially in relation to counterfeits. it is therefore clear that the trademark system in europe needs to be modernised. the european union institutions must act swiftly to protect citizens from the negative consequences of counterfeiting. the european union agency for trade marks and designs is preparing to set up a system for the registration of geographical indications in the near future and is preparing to take on the tasks of the upcoming legislation on the protection of trade secrets. the resources raised by the agency in the form of fees should be used to further develop the agency and to ensure a high level of protection of intellectual property rights in europe.
in writing. - what ? the reform of the trademark comes at a time when eu customs authorities are reporting massive breaches of international patent law standards. the current system calls for stricter measures, not only to protect consumers but also the producers of original products themselves, because of the increasing number of counterfeits crossing eu borders. the trade mark system was developed 20 years ago and has had a clear success in consumer protection. as the eu developed, the number of member states increased, and the number of consumers increased in direct proportion, to 500 million. the main purpose of the community trade mark reform should, in my view, be to reduce costs for manufacturers, especially small and medium-sized enterprises, who would be guaranteed protection of their rights within the eu. european producers lose their income because of the illegal counterfeiting of low-quality and often harmful products. i hope that, in cooperation with the council, we will be able to define the process of granting trade marks correctly and to establish rules for the european and national levels of protection that are suitably complementary.
the legal framework for confiscation of proceeds of crime has been discussed at eu level since 2009 in view of the billions of euros in damage caused to society by crime. the fight against crime requires a coherent approach and mutual trust between law enforcement authorities in all member states in order to cooperate effectively across borders. the harmonisation of penalties in this respect strengthens the certainty that offenders will be punished with comparable severity, which has both a preventive and a repressive effect. i consider it necessary that the directive strictly observes the fundamental rights and principles recognised by the european convention for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. it is important to ensure sufficiently that the principle of ne bis in idem is respected where the same act is committed by the same perpetrator and, as regards confiscation even without a final judgment of a court, it should be permissible only in the cases of particularly serious organised crime, which are listed in exhaustive terms.
i consider the confiscation of criminal assets to be a very important tool in the fight against organised crime, which is becoming increasingly widespread in the european union. the activities of organised crime are increasingly carried out outside their home country, often in several member states. in this context, i consider it of the utmost importance to establish harmonisation rules that facilitate the cross-border freezing and confiscation of assets stemming from serious and organised cross-border crime by eu member states. i voted in favour of this report because i am convinced that the adoption of these measures will make it possible to detect cases of corruption and to recover ill-gotten gains even more effectively and consistently. the adoption of the proposed measures will also strengthen public confidence in the criminal justice system, strengthen cross-border cooperation and make the confiscation process more efficient in the successful fight against serious and organised crime.
in writing. - what ? the current economic situation, the financial crisis and the slowdown in economic growth present new opportunities for perpetrators, increase the vulnerability of the economy and pose new challenges for public authorities. organized crime and illegal enterprises generate huge profits every year, which are then legalized and invested in legal activities. the concealment of assets and their reinvestment in member states other than those where the crime was committed undermines the ability of member states to combat cross-border crime effectively and ultimately reduces tax revenues for national governments and the eu budget. an effective system for the freezing, administration and confiscation of criminal assets must be in place in all member states, with the necessary institutional framework and financial and human resources. the legislation of the eu and its member states governing the confiscation of criminal assets is currently underused and underdeveloped. confiscation of criminal assets is an effective tool in the fight against organised and serious crime and should be given higher priority in the future. the adoption of minimum rules will be consistent with member states' freezing and confiscation regimes and will strengthen mutual trust and increase the effectiveness of cross-border cooperation.
in writing. - what ? the critical situation in europe in the form of increasing counterfeiting and infringement of intellectual property rights requires the modernisation of the trade mark system in europe. member states have had the opportunity to learn and use the experience gained to strengthen the substantive law and practice aspects of trade marks. the european business environment is made up of a huge number of companies and therefore the trade mark system needs to be clearly understandable and accessible to all users. individual member states must work to improve the services provided by national offices, which would make national trademark protection systems more attractive to users.
in writing. - what ? the european police office was created in 1995 in response to the rapid growth of organised and violent crime, which remains a significant threat to the security and prosperity of the european union. the regulation aims to establish a european union agency for law enforcement and cooperation and to repeal council decision 2009/371/jha, thereby bringing europol into line with the treaty of lisbon, which brought about the recognition of the european parliament's right to co-decide, and thereby to enable the european parliament and the council, by means of a regulation and by means of the ordinary legislative procedure, to determine the scope and structure of europol. the proposal strengthens, inter alia, democratic governance and the oversight of europol's activities by national parliaments and the european parliament, which will be informed in detail through annual activity reports and the closure of accounts. the new regulation should strengthen and clarify the data protection regime. there should also be no merger between europol and the european police college.
in writing. - what ? the new directive aims to merge and recast the existing directives 2005/71/ec and 2004/114/ec and to add two new categories of paid trainees and au pairs. the european commission intends to create a more attractive environment for migrants from third countries. it will allow students from third countries to work 20 hours a week and will also allow students and researchers who have studied in the european union to stay in the eu for 12 months to find employment or set up their own business. the european commission is working to simplify and harmonise entry conditions so that successful applicants can obtain a long-term visa or residence permit. simplifying the process of accession conditions seeks to address the problem of europe's ageing population and to maintain the competitiveness of the european economy.
massive disasters - such as droughts, floods, landslides, tsunamis, earthquakes, fires, and other hazards - affect millions of people every year. as the scale and frequency of these global threats in the world is increasing every year and is likely to continue, i agree that new solutions and practices should be sought to help save lives and reduce suffering. the european union is the largest provider of humanitarian aid in the world, and together with the financial resources provided by individual countries, it contributes more than half of humanitarian aid. i voted in favour of this report because i believe it is important that the european union continues to provide support and humanitarian aid to those in need, and that not only prevention and the preparedness of the voluntary humanitarian aid corps can be strengthened, but that the long-term consequences of various disasters can also be addressed.
in writing. - what ? solidarity and humanitarian values are a highly developed and important part of the european union's policies. the establishment of the volunteer humanitarian aid corps is intended to contribute to enhancing the european union's capacity to respond to humanitarian crises and thus to declare solidarity with people in need or communities affected by disasters in third countries. the activities of the college should complement the existing structures and organisations of the european union in the field of humanitarian aid. the european union suffers from an acute lack of a uniform mechanism for the selection of volunteers and their lack of training for the provision of humanitarian aid. in the current period of war and conflict, the importance and scale of humanitarian operations have increased and the financial resources invested in humanitarian action need to be increased.
in writing. - there is no doubt that humanitarian aid in third world countries is needed. the european union is also working on a proposal to set up european voluntary humanitarian aid corps during this period. they should enhance the interest of european citizens in humanitarian projects. they would thus complement existing national and international volunteering schemes without duplicating their work. european aid volunteers [should not be deployed to third countries affected by natural disasters or man-made crises. their assistance in situations of armed conflict and internal problems of states to ensure the safety of volunteers is expressly excluded. based on a number of studies as well as personal experience, i have realised that awareness of humanitarian aid among eu citizens needs to be constantly raised. few people know that the european union is the largest donor of humanitarian aid. its share of global humanitarian aid is almost 50[nbsp ]%, but it is often overshadowed by the assistance of other international organisations. the establishment of european voluntary humanitarian aid corps would provide a kind of light at the end of the tunnel for[nbsp]the people who desperately need this aid, and would also help to raise the profile of one of the fundamental values of the european union, which is solidarity. as volunteering is a concrete and visible expression of solidarity, i will certainly support this proposal.
i think the fight against poverty is very important, although it is rather problematic and not easy. i supported the report because i believe that the fund can contribute to alleviating some forms of poverty and social exclusion in europe. although the fund is estimated to be able to help only two million citizens a year and we estimate that around 120 million people across the union are living below the poverty line, i believe that reducing and eradicating poverty and social exclusion must remain a key task for member states. the €3.5 billion allocated to providing and distributing aid and material assistance to the poorest is a good thing. it is a foundation, but everyone has to be involved in the aid, member states, businesses, the voluntary sector, regional authorities, civil society organisations, and let us not forget that poverty reduction must also be a central theme ahead of the upcoming european parliament elections.
in writing. - what ? in 2010, almost 120 million people in the member states were at risk of poverty and social exclusion. the economic and financial crisis that has been ongoing since 2009 has changed the social map of the european union beyond recognition. material deprivation and the most severe forms of poverty have spread throughout society. the financial resources for the fund for european aid to the most deprived have been increased and can only be used to support them. the fund is the successor to the european food aid programme, which has been in operation since 1987, but has a much larger scope and reach. the fund for the most deprived is a sign of european solidarity, despite its low budget. the rate of poverty and social exclusion in the european union is increasing significantly and there is therefore a need to invest in measures to reduce the social gap in society and to protect human dignity and solidarity.
in writing. - what ? in the context of the continuing economic difficulties in the member states, the number of people suffering from severe material deprivation is increasing every year. in 2012, almost 8% of union citizens were living in material deprivation and about one quarter of europeans were at risk of poverty in 2010. unfortunately, the most affected are women and children. the union strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth, europe 2020, aims to reduce by at least 20 million the number of people at risk of poverty and social exclusion by 2020. one way forward is to ensure that, in addition to creating jobs in all regions and strengthening social infrastructure, for example through the structural funds, adequate financial coverage is provided under the fund for european aid to the most deprived. for these reasons, i support the report.
in writing. - what ? i see the establishment of the fund for european aid to the most deprived as a demonstration of our solidarity with the people most affected by the crisis. in 2010, almost a quarter of europeans faced poverty or social exclusion. despite its limited resources, the fund is an adequate instrument to alleviate the most severe forms of poverty, food and material deprivation, which are still to be found in almost all eu member states. the fund is built on the european food aid programme, which was established in 1987. however, its scope is much wider and funding can be used, for example, to support social inclusion measures as well as to provide practical help to homeless people in finding accommodation. the use of the fund must be committed to helping those most affected by poverty, and the application of its funds should be subject to consultation with stakeholders such as food banks, charitable organisations and civil society. i agree with the proposal that the key actors should also be involved in the design, operation and monitoring of the national schemes that will implement the programme. i think it is necessary to point out that the fund for european aid to the most deprived is only a complementary instrument to existing national policies. the responsibility and commitment to fight poverty and contribute to the general well-being of our citizens lies with the member states themselves.

in writing. - what ? violence against women is a major cause of gender inequality. it's a violation of basic human rights. the statistics that up to a quarter of women in europe have experienced physical violence at least once in their adult lives are alarming. violence affects women from infancy to old age. it occurs in school, at work, in peer groups, in public, but also in the privacy of family or partner life. we all know about violence against women, we talk about it, but it's not very effective in eliminating it. i therefore support the rapporteur's view that the commission should organise a european year against violence against women in the coming years, which would allow us to talk more about this problem and involve more organisations, institutions and local authorities in tackling it. an information campaign would also help, so that the women themselves who are victims of violence are not afraid to speak up and, if necessary, seek professional help. in order to combat violence against women, member states must draw up action plans, increase media awareness and adopt specific criminal law provisions and measures.
in writing. - violence against women is unacceptable. but if we want to address the situation of women who are at risk of or have been victims of violence, let's not hide it behind a modern fetish that the word gender has become. the european treaties do not recognize this term. the word 'gender' is not defined in european law, as the commission confirmed in its written reply to my question. by using it in legislation, we increase legal uncertainty. i support the creation of uniform european rules to combat this form of violence, from prevention to protection of victims to prosecution of perpetrators. however, according to the report, the 'reproductive health policy' should become the 'core of the discussion on violence against women', including, for example, the liberalisation of abortion. it is a mockery of women because it places the responsibility for the rapist's act on the victim of the violence. such a distorted view of women is in direct conflict with efforts to prevent violence and to help its victims.

i think today is a historic moment because, as a result of years of negotiations, we are able to take part in the plenary debate on the common european sales law. the report concludes that the common european right of sale can address the currently known internal market problems caused by the different contractual law regimes in the member states. the definition of the scope of the regulation may alleviate concerns regarding the influence of national legal systems. the european system will only be applicable to cross-border contracts and in particular to online contracts, and the common european sales law will be optional, i.e. will remain an optional instrument, according to the ep report. it will make available, within the legal systems of the member states, the uniform contract law rules laid down in the regulation, as a second system. i am confident that, through the work of parliament, traders, european smes, will really benefit from the opportunities offered by the new regulation. this would mean that they would be able to rely on common rules and the same contractual conditions for all their cross-border transactions, thus reducing their costs. the application of the same system is also relevant from the point of view of legal certainty, provided that appropriate consumer protection guarantees and remedies are provided. the legal certainty factor can be enhanced by the development by the commission of european standard contractual clauses. the legal instrument promising potential benefits for consumers and businesses in the internal market has taken shape and been perfected through the work of parliament. this will provide a good basis for further negotiations.
the consolidation and development of a well-functioning internal market are among the union's primary interests and tasks. nevertheless, for years consumers and traders have been waiting for unified rules on the right to buy, which would provide clear legal solutions to everyday business transactions. in particular, the area of distance selling, whether via the internet or other telecommunications technologies, suffers greatly from the lack of clear cross-border rules. i am convinced that the potential of distance selling will develop in direct proportion to the increase in predictability and legal certainty of both parties. consumers in particular appreciate standardised contractual terms which do not vary from one country to another but which, on the contrary, can be readily and in advance in their own language.

in writing. - council decision 2009/831/ec concerns the application of a reduced rate of tax in the autonomous regions of madeira and the azores to locally produced and consumed rum, liqueurs and wine distillates. the reason for adopting this decision was the fragmentation and weak mechanisation of local farms. the 75% tax advantage in question is intended to compensate for the additional costs incurred by operators due to the geographical specificities of madeira and the azores. the portuguese authorities responsible have requested an extension of the tax concession until 31 december 2020 due to the low production volumes and the absence of any breach of the integrity and the legal order of the european union. the validity of the extension of the authorisation is relevant until 1 july 2014, when the new european commission guidelines on regional aid for the period 2014[nbsp ] to 2020 will enter into force.
