in writing. - what ? in the last ten years, we have had to deal with three railway legislative packages. these aimed at gradually opening up the markets of the member states and increasing the competitiveness and interoperability of rail transport. however, the share of rail transport compared to other modes of transport within the eu remains low. the fourth railway package therefore aims to increase the efficiency of rail transport markets, improve the sector's performance and create a single european railway area. the creation of the common market was aimed at reducing the cost of services to consumers, while maintaining a high level of quality of services available to all. liberalisation should therefore not be an end in itself but should be seen as a tool to create a more competitive sector with a high level of services. the opening of the market must therefore be accompanied by strict rules concerning, in particular, passenger rights.
in writing. - what ? rail passenger transport currently accounts for a very small share of total passenger transport in the european union, and investment in this sector could support employment growth as well as environmental protection. the problems in the railway sector should be addressed by strengthening the institution of competitive public procurement. the european commission is aiming to abolish the possibility of public procurement in rail passenger transport. the implementation of all the necessary changes and reorganisation of a complex system while maintaining the quality and safety of the services provided requires sufficient time, and it is therefore necessary to provide member states with sufficient time to implement the necessary changes. in order to maintain a high standard of employment protection, it is important that there is no unjustified redundancy in the event of a change of operator.
in writing. - what ? with increasing air pollution and the resulting increase in greenhouse gas emissions, the importance of rail transport is growing. the european commission estimates that by 2050, rail should provide more than 50%[nbsp ] of passenger and freight transport over short and medium distances. unfortunately, with more than 11 000 different national safety and interoperability regulations, the european rail system is neither efficient nor transparent. the report aims to modernise interoperability rules in response to new needs arising from the opening up of the market and the expected growth of the railway modal split, in particular by defining and clarifying existing rules more precisely, using technical specifications for interoperability, simplifying long and costly authorisations for rolling stock and creating a european register. all the proposals put forward by the european commission are designed to help companies reduce the time taken to market and to improve the position of the european rail sector on world markets.
it is a well-known fact that rail transport is one of the safest modes of transport in the eu. but if an accident occurs, the cost in lives, health, and property is pitifully high. in view of the rapid scientific and technical progress, i consider it necessary to update regularly the existing safety conditions for passengers, railway staff and other people who come into contact with rail transport in their professional or private lives. significant differences in safety standards persist in the european union, which negatively affect the functioning of european railways as a whole and undermine their competitiveness and attractiveness, especially for cross-border passengers. i therefore believe that there is a need to harmonise operating and minimum safety rules and i support the creation of a common regulatory framework for railway safety through harmonisation.
in writing. - the european commission expects rail transport to become increasingly important over the next decade. today, rail travel is the safest form of travel for passengers. accidents in the railway sector are rare, but when they do occur, they have catastrophic consequences for the number of victims, the environment and the reputation of rail transport as the safest form of transport. every railway undertaking entering the market and wishing to be part of the european network must apply for a safety certificate. the acquisition of the certificate in question is linked to a number of conditions that the undertaking must fulfil in order to comply with the relevant safety regulations. the certificate should be in two parts. after the end of the transitional period, the single valid certificate should be issued by the european railway agency following a process of cooperation with the national safety agencies.
in writing. - what ? the efficiency of rail transport is a major priority for the european union. however, railways need to improve their operations and the services they provide in order to catch up with other forms of transport. often, measures are geared to national interests rather than the needs of citizens. the european union agency for railways is responsible for providing technical support and improving the safety of the railway system. the role of the european railway agency should be increased in line with the interoperability and safety directives, while at the same time new tasks should be added to it, including support for standardisation. the agency should be able to delegate certain tasks to national safety authorities. achieving the set objectives will require improving the governance of the agency in terms of avoiding conflicts of interest, strengthening the board and assessing priorities, taking into account available resources.
in writing. - what ? the fourth railway package is also to include the repeal of council regulation (eec) no 1192/69 on common rules for the opening of accounts of railway undertakings, due to its low use, as only 4 member states have used it since 2007. the regulation in question allows compensation to be granted to railway undertakings for liabilities which other undertakings in different sectors are not obliged to bear. these measures are intended to help railway undertakings to cope with the specific working conditions in the railway sector.
in writing. - what ? the growing importance of air transport also brings with it the challenge of not increasing accident rates and the associated fatal consequences for human life and the environment in direct proportion to the increasing volume of passengers carried. in order to increase safety in the air transport sector, a harmonised incident reporting system should be established within the european union, based on spontaneous reporting of incidents by individual airline employees. the new system will be based on a mandatory and voluntary basis, whereby individuals will report and describe incidents. in order to ensure the effectiveness of this system, the employees concerned will be protected from sanctions, except in cases of serious breaches of work discipline and negligence. in view of the challenges facing the aviation sector, the role of the aviation safety agency will be strengthened and new systems for data protection and incident reporting will be introduced. the introduction of new standards also provides for the creation of a culture of fairness and protection of employees from criminal prosecution by employers.
in writing. - what ? the eu's air accident investigation system is of a high standard. however, data collection and analysis activities on such accidents need to be strengthened. while accident investigation consists of an analysis of the causes of an accident, the purpose of the occurrence reporting system is to identify incidents that could have contributed to the disaster if they had not been detected and[nbsp]corrective measures taken. however, the collection of such data is not simple, as the system is based on spontaneous reporting of events by aviation industry employees. however, these employees could be threatened with sanctions by the airlines their employers if they report such incidents. it is therefore necessary to ensure the creation of a genuine and uniform environment of trust, a so-called justice culture. the functioning of such a system requires not only the protection of the reporting person himself but also the protection of any person concerned by the reported event, which will allow for confidence-building. employees who have been punished in the past on the basis of a report will be able to use the appeal mechanism in the future.
road traffic accidents represent incalculable costs and the figures for 2012 are alarming, with some 28 000 people killed and more than 1.5 million injured on the roads of the european union. the european parliament has long been working towards a system that would mitigate the consequences of road accidents, in particular saving lives, and be accessible to every driver. the introduction of the 112 e-call system in all new vehicles by 2015 will certainly contribute to[nbsp]building the necessary infrastructure for immediate operational assistance. i call for it not to be confined to passenger cars but to be extended to other modes of transport in the interests of the protection of the life and health of road users. the protection of personal data is a major issue throughout the system, and both legislatively and technically it must be treated in such a way as to ensure the required level of privacy protection and to take measures to prevent tracking and misuse.
i welcome the proposal for a regulation introducing an on-board emergency call to 112 via the so-called e-call system. in serious traffic accidents, every minute, even every second, until the arrival of the emergency services is a golden opportunity for the injured. the introduction of e-call is a way of informing emergency medical services immediately of an accident and of being able to navigate to the exact scene of the accident in a timely manner, thus helping to prevent unnecessary loss of life on european roads. naturally, for a system to be truly functional and reliable, its minimum attributes must be standardised, interoperable and accessible to all across the eu. i hope that citizens will be able to start using this system in the near future, which will contribute to their safety and road transport.
in writing. - what ? with the development of new technologies at the beginning of the 21st century, the european commission included the creation of a harmonised pan-european ecall service as one of the priorities of the e-safety initiative. the european commission is working to have an ecall system fitted to every new car by 2015 that will dial the single european emergency number in the event of a serious accident. the system allows emergency calls to be made even if injured passengers are unable to speak to the security forces by establishing a connection to the nearest public security reception. the european commission expects that, once installed in cars, the system would reduce road fatalities by up to 50% in rural areas and by 60% in congested areas. the faster arrival of rescue teams has a profound effect on the lives of people who are struggling.
in writing. - what ? although we do our best to reduce the number of accidents and to mitigate the consequences of them, we do not always succeed. the ecall system is an important structure that deals with the safety of road users. i believe that the automatic emergency call to the 112 line, which is implemented through the on-board system, will save many lives. the european commission has been working on ecall since 2005. it originally intended to introduce this system on a voluntary basis, but this approach failed. in view of the number of accidents on european roads, i consider it inappropriate that ecall is currently only present in approximately 7% of vehicles. let's face it, few people do anything voluntarily, even when it's for their own safety. that is why we adopted by a large majority the joint report of the committees on ecall in 2012, on promoting the introduction of the system in all vehicles, regardless of brand. as it is a free public emergency call service, it will benefit all drivers in europe. the ecall system should be available at all times, at least as a back-up. the only aspect that needs to be examined in more detail is, in my view, the protection of personal data. they must not be misused in any way and the owner of the ecall must feel safe in his vehicle in all respects.
in writing. - what ? the health of the citizens of the member states is one of the european union's main priorities. the objective of the european union health programme 2014-2020 is to support and add value to the existing health policies of the member states in order to maintain a high level of health for citizens of the european union and to reduce inequalities between member states by promoting innovation in health, the sustainability of healthcare systems and protection against serious cross-border health threats. the financial envelope of the programme is eur 450 million. the way in which funding is allocated between the different objectives of the programme will depend on the projects submitted.
in writing. - what ? the objective of the connecting europe facility is to define guidelines for the deployment of digital infrastructures and broadband networks. the european parliament considers the completion of the digital single market to be a major priority for the future, which will not be possible without significant investment in high-speed and efficient internet networks. the reduction of the planned high budget to eur 1 billion will significantly disrupt the plans in the sector. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on digital broadband in the member states and in the union as a whole. cross-border access to infrastructure can bring high added value to the european union in the future.
in writing. - what ? the accessibility of public sector bodies' websites is very important for all users, including those with functional limitations. it is a way of quickly accessing important information needed for the daily lives of millions of citizens of the european union. public sector bodies, but also any other bodies providing essential information and services to the public, must take full responsibility for the quality and safety of the services provided. the european union is making a considerable effort to improve the accessibility of its institutions through various programmes. the problem is that member states have different approaches to the availability and level of information and services available through public websites. access to information is just one of the many barriers faced by people with disabilities in their daily lives. the main objective of the european commission is to improve monitoring and to set up national bodies to receive complaints from users, thus improving the existing system.
in writing. - what ? the economic and financial crisis has brought a great decline to the european economy. in order to overcome the crisis and restore confidence, it is important to maintain public investment and banks as the main source of long-term financing in the european union and not to return to excessive risk-taking as in the pre-crisis period. public investment is important in the recovery of the financial system and the european parliament insists on harmonising the evaluation of projects of public interest. without the necessary investments in infrastructure, research, development and innovation, the economy will not be able to recover. the european union cannot rely solely on banks and savings banks, but must find alternative sources of finance to complement the intermediation process offered by banks.
in writing. - the european union seeks to protect its citizens from all forms of discrimination and inequality. the period of economic and financial crisis has hit women the hardest, whose desperate financial situation can eventually lead to prostitution, another special group being young people. these two groups of people are often the targets of exploitation and discrimination on the grounds of gender. forced prostitution is one of the highest forms of physical abuse and sexual exploitation. prostitution and sexual exploitation are often linked to trafficking in women and girls, which involves trafficking in women and girls between member states of the european union, but also involves selling outside the european union. there are several approaches that are applied within member states, but legalization of prostitution has failed to prevent trafficking in human beings and the flourishing of organised crime in this area, following the experience of some member states. in the european union, the so-called nordic model also applies, where the offence is always committed by the person buying the sexual services and therefore does not ultimately criminalise the person engaging in prostitution.
in writing. - what ? the ever-increasing demands of society and growing competition pose major challenges for developing countries in the use and exploitation of natural resources. the extraction of natural resources is a major source of armed conflict in the world and also represents a huge burden on the environment. developing countries that choose to intensify their mining industry are also exposed to secondary factors of this decision, such as forced population displacement, growing social disparities in the region, and escalation of tensions. developing countries often lack the ready-made systems that require the necessary economic, environmental and social reforms, as well as labour law reforms, for multinational corporations to enter the local market.
in writing. a week ago, ukraine and europe were shocked by the brutality of the repression of the demonstrations on the kiev maidan. today, we mourn for the people who sacrificed their lives because they showed their civil courage and stood up for a more democratic and free ukraine. the ukraine we all want. i'd like to pay my respects to their memory. ukraine needs concrete support and assistance and i believe the union can provide it. we've been able to respond to the political developments of the last few days. but change is coming fast, and all the more so if we are to assist in a peaceful political transformation. the union must have a concrete plan for ukraine, based on a reform and democratic agenda. it should not be about gestures, but about clear and targeted aid, both humanitarian and economic. we must send a signal to the people of ukraine that we are ready to help fulfil their desire for a better functioning, prosperous and democratic ukraine. elections in ukraine will be held on 25 may, at the same time as the elections to the european parliament. i hope this day will be symbolic for ukraine's step towards the union.
in writing. colleagues, the situation in ukraine has developed dramatically indeed. it's a real revolution, and as we all know, revolution carries risks. the eu must therefore contribute first and foremost to the stability of the country. what does that mean in practical terms? try to avoid a possible negative scenario. in particular, to help the new representation set a precise timetable for the transition to a fully democratic regime. set the date for early presidential and parliamentary elections. furthermore, to stop the wave of political revenge and retaliation: it is unacceptable that radical forces seek to ban political parties, collective punishment of state officials, the return of the well-known selective justice. there must be no attacks on minorities, ethnic groups, or the right to use the russian language. the standard of human rights protection must not be lowered. finally, the economy: the eu can take dynamic steps to reform the ukrainian economy. and in this process, there should be well-known oligarchs, all of them, who have become rich through privatization and the redistribution of wealth since the 1990s and are now based in financial centers like london. coordinated efforts could also lead to a faster process of signing an agreement with the eu, but only with a new legitimate government.
we consider drones to be a modern technology, being developed by virtually all the developed countries of the world. it's a technology that's usable in civilian space. it can be used, for example, for traffic monitoring, for interventions, for emergencies, for floods or for fires. the technology also has applications in meteorology. the european union does not have a common army, it does not have a common armament, so we cannot influence the individual member states, which are themselves shaping their security policy and their defence concept. but if we want to be active and proactive in this area, we should strive for an international convention that would establish the conditions for the use and use of drones in military conflicts. and that, i think, is the only way we can go.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your support. first of all, i would like to thank the rapporteur for his work, but above all for his openness and willingness to compromise. the report is broad and forward-looking, but there are parts of it that we can't agree with. the liberalization of access to drugs is unacceptable in the christian sense, but the question of euthanasia also raises serious moral problems. the commissioner has repeatedly stressed that the human rights situation in europe is not without problems. there's plenty of room for improvement here. obviously, the problem is the universal accessibility of the rights enshrined in the charter of fundamental rights. for example, is it not clear how to take account of fundamental rights when applying the anti-discrimination directives? it appears that we have not fully exploited the means of applying this existing mechanism to redress, but it is also debatable whether new mechanisms are really needed. it is regrettable that these annual reports do not focus on issues where europe really needs to pay attention to developments, be it access to justice, the right to privacy or the situation and legal status of national minorities. the question of double standards is also a problem: the acceding countries are obliged to respect the rights of national minorities, but the member states are not obliged to do so. thus, in the enlargement of the union, we see that after accession the situation of national minorities, instead of improving, has actually worsened. the so-called copenhagen dilemma must be solved sooner or later. the situation of national minorities cannot depend solely on the vague goodwill of the majority. however, the proposed copenhagen mechanism raises several concerns, including the real danger that human rights could easily become the victim of political battles. we expected more decisive progress from the member states in many areas and i am very pleased that the commissioner mentioned the directive on trafficking in human beings, which unfortunately some member states have not yet adopted.
the european union has established a core set of legislation whose primary objective is to guarantee respect for, and the protection and promotion of, fundamental rights, including through the establishment of the copenhagen criteria, the incorporation of articles 2, 6 and 7 of the treaty on european union, the charter of fundamental rights, the obligation to accede to the european convention on human rights and the corresponding national provisions of the laws of the member states. with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, the charter has transformed its values and principles into concrete and enforceable rights and has become legally binding on the institutions, bodies, offices and agencies of the european union as well as on the member states in the implementation of legislation. the european parliament has repeatedly called for the strengthening of mechanisms to ensure that the union's values as set out in article 2 of the treaty on european union are respected, protected and promoted, and for crisis management in the union and in the member states. and there is now a discussion on the creation of a new mechanism in which the commission, the council and the member states join parliament and the ngos. the commission also announced that it would like to strengthen the rule of law in the european union and could propose the use of a formal notice under article 7 (1) of the treaty on european union. you also mentioned the need to amend the treaties and announced that you would propose amendments to enable the organisation of the debate during the elections, including on the application of article 7, and to seek consensus on these proposals, which should aim to ensure that the european union's fundamental rights policy in the european union is based on clear rules and mechanisms, objective indicators and data, is transparent, fair and, in particular, firmly protects individual rights, democracy and the rule of law. as the rapporteur has already mentioned, many violations of fundamental rights still occur within the european union and in the member states, as is also clear from reports, whether annual or special, by the european commission, the fundamental rights agency, the council of europe and many other organisations. the bodies concerned noted the findings, in particular as regards the situation of violations of the rights of minorities, but also of the media and journalists, or the actions of the security forces, police and secret services. i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr michel, for his very good cooperation and for the very good debate. and in conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to point out that we are three months away from elections for the next legislative term of the european parliament. i believe that the adoption of this report, as approved by the committee, will also be a good signal to our citizens in the member states in tomorrow's vote that it is the european parliament that will always seek to ensure that their fundamental rights are respected.
the demagogy of the so-called abortionists is endless. the charter of fundamental rights, in its section on human dignity, says that everyone has the right to life, and it mentions reproduction only when it prohibits the cloning of human beings. the report on human rights in the european union states that reproductive rights, including abortion, are an essential element of human dignity. in doing so, the european court of justice confirmed that the unborn human fetus also has dignity and integrity. the charter of fundamental rights is meant to protect this dignity. defending abortion by reference to the charter is not just an arbitrary misinterpretation. i personally reject this demagogy. the report also proposes to increase the powers of the european commission and the fundamental rights agency to intervene in the internal affairs of the member states. this would be contrary to the principle of subsidiarity, which is one of the main pillars of the european project. it is to subsidiarity that we owe the delicate balance of power and responsibility. violation of this would lead to a loss of legitimacy and would strengthen the rise of radical nationalism in the member states. therefore, we should categorically reject such proposals.
this year's resolution again draws attention to a number of problems in the protection of fundamental rights. it is important that we talk about the shortcomings within the union and that we can correct the errors that are revealed as soon as possible. it doesn't matter how, by what means, we try to improve the situation. in assessing respect for fundamental rights, the union should follow the principles and values enshrined in the treaties, stressing that our community is a community of values and that this is at the heart of integration. all members of the community of values are expected to fulfil their obligations to guarantee fundamental rights and freedoms. this applies to the eu institutions as well as to the member states. the evaluation of member states should focus on the appropriate use of mechanisms that already exist. the examination and assessment of member states in terms of the rule of law is purely objective and applies to all member states and can be carried out on the basis of uniform criteria, both now and in the future. the aim of all the mechanisms at the lower level should be to guarantee the rule of law and respect for fundamental rights. respect for the rights of persons belonging to minorities is also listed in article 2 of the treaty on european union. in this area, europe's national, ethnic, linguistic and cultural diversity will not be fully realised until adequate, comprehensive eu legislation is in place to promote the protection of the fundamental rights of historical and linguistic minorities. guarantees formulated at union level can make it easier to eliminate discrimination against national minorities within member states. although the report was produced in relation to the year 2012, it has been a year since the eu declared the year of eu citizens, signalling that eu citizenship has also provided opportunities. eu citizens will only be able to enjoy their fundamental rights and the benefits of free movement if the principle of equal treatment is fully applied to them in all member states.
the common european area requires the establishment of a well-defined, effective cooperation between the security and judicial services in the prosecution of migrant offenders. the 2002 framework decision on the european arrest warrant provides for mutual recognition of acts on the basis of mutual trust. the european commission's reports on the implementation of the framework decision identify a number of problems complicating the functioning of this system. it will therefore be necessary for the commission to prepare a set of legislative measures addressing the identified shortcomings of the practical application of the european arrest warrant. in doing so, it should also draw on analyses of unenforced european arrest warrants, which indicate where the current mechanisms are fundamentally ineffective and inefficient, in addition to the findings contained in its reports.
i would like to remind you in this discussion that it was venezuelan president hugo chávez who said that -- and i quote -- "the way to a new, better world is not capitalism, that way is socialism". unfortunately, it was the president of the european council and the president of the commission who, on the occasion of chávez's death, highlighted the social development of venezuela. after 14 years of socialist rule, venezuela's economy is collapsing and society is facing waves of social unrest that the government is brutally suppressing or at least tolerating escalating violence. this is the true face of socialism. freedom of speech, thought and assembly are suppressed. we know this from before the berlin wall came down.
the protests in venezuela prove that a regime built on authoritarian principles cannot last forever. violence against its own citizens, their enslavement, imprisonment and torture, violations of human rights and civil liberties, corruption and pervasive violence are not the hallmarks of a permanently prosperous state. venezuela is changing. it is being changed by people who see no other option but to take to the streets and stand up to a senseless regime. the number of casualties is increasing, and the chances of keeping the country at peace are diminishing. this violence must end. those responsible must be brought to justice and those wrongfully imprisoned must be released. the development in maduro's venezuela has hit rock bottom economically, socially, but also morally and humanly. you have to bounce back from it. venezuela needs new democratic elections. not another experiment.

in writing. - what ? the single european sales law is an important piece of legislation for the citizens of the european union, as demonstrated by the years of preparation. european firms will be able to trade much more easily in other member states thanks to the provisions of the common rules for cross-border sales which will be used as an alternative to the national rules already in place. the common european sales law is a set of european rules for cross-border trade, based on the principle of voluntariness. the main objective of this regulation is to put the business environment and the internal market back in motion by removing trade barriers arising from national contract law. european consumers and traders will be able to exploit the full potential of the internal market. consumers can benefit from the high standard of consumer protection system offered by this new law.
the statistics on the effects of smoking are alarming and cannot be ignored. smoking kills around 700 000 people a year in the eu, and up to 70% of smokers start smoking before reaching adulthood. an even more alarming number is the number of children under 15 who smoke. it is therefore time to tighten tobacco guidelines and ban cigarette flavourings and other additives such as vitamins altogether, and to introduce stronger health warnings on tobacco products to discourage young people from smoking. while tobacco companies will certainly continue to push for the relaxation of eu directives, we must be firm in this, with people's health and lives in mind. i supported the report because it also introduces a number of improvements on traceability and security aimed at combating illicit trade in tobacco products.
in writing. - what ? the european union has a longstanding commitment to combating the negative effects of smoking and the harmful consequences of the use of tobacco products. despite the enormous efforts of the european union and the ban on smoking in public places, the consumption of tobacco products is increasing, especially among young people. tobacco companies are moving forward with sophistication, offering people a number of visual and other enhancements to current products, resulting in 37[nbsp ]% of tobacco product use by children aged 11 to 15 years. the european commission considers prevention of smoking, especially among adolescents, to be one of its main objectives for the future. the attractiveness of tobacco and tobacco products plays a very important role in their consumption and subsequent addiction, and the european commission is therefore continuing to increase the proportion of health warnings on the packaging of individual tobacco products.
in writing. - what ? our main objective in drafting the directive was to discourage young people from smoking. young people and teenagers alike are used to seeing something fashionable in cigarettes and smoking and want to be as close as possible to their peers. in the past few years, it has become evident that tobacco companies have come to realize this fact. cigarette packs with attractive designs and colors have flooded the market. but the tobacco campaign did not stop at the packaging. the various flavours that have misled smokers about the amount of harmful substances contained in cigarettes should be properly regulated by the new tobacco products directive. i am very pleased that, after several months of work on this directive, the european parliament, the council and the commission have agreed on a compromise wording for the final text. i think that the development of a so-called blacklist of additives with carcinogenic and toxic effects is a great step towards banning their use in cigarette manufacturing. on the other hand, additives which are necessary in the production process itself, such as sugar, should be allowed. a compromise without some concessions on our part would not be called a compromise. i therefore support, for example, the amendment of the original proposal for a total ban on slim cigarettes. they will continue to be available on the market, but in no case in a package resembling lipstick or a perfume box.
in writing. - what ? prostitution and exploitation of women and girls are global in nature. no eu member state can honestly claim to be free of this problem. i regret to say that more and more women and girls are being trafficked. girls and women in dire financial situations are the most likely target group of this notorious organised crime. despite the continued practice of the oldest craft in human history, we can see that there are countries where, after the introduction of apparently successful legislation, the high percentage of women practising this profession has declined significantly. i therefore think it is logical to take inspiration from countries such as sweden and norway, which have pioneered a new approach to prostitution and introduced the already well-known nordic model of combating the exploitation of women. i stand by the fact that advocating for the legalization of prostitution is not the right thing to do. prostitution and the trade in white meat are closely linked. the only way to improve the living conditions of women is therefore to combat both phenomena. we cannot tolerate the practices of people who treat women's bodies as commodities. every human being has the right to dignity and the ones who should be punished are those who take advantage of the poor situation of women, not women who are forced into prostitution for the most part.
for more than 20 years, the solvit internet network has been ensuring the free movement of goods, services and capital for the people of the eu. although the single market has succeeded in expanding trade within the eu and providing consumers with more favourable goods and services, we often see crossborder disputes between citizens or businesses arising from the incorrect or inaccurate application of the single market rules. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that the present times demand a more efficient and better functioning of the solvit internet network. while the report notes that the solvit network is developing in a positive direction, i believe it is essential that the european union and the member states take measures to ensure that individuals and companies are not only better informed about their rights in the single market and the possibilities of assistance in solving their cross-border problems, but also have access to the solvit network for all equally.
in writing. - what ? the solvit network involves all the administrative systems of all the member states of the european union. the main task of the solvit internet network is to find solutions to problems arising from the incorrect application of the internal market rules of the european union by member states as quickly as possible and without legal proceedings. the european commission has presented a programme to improve the functioning of the solvit network and a your europe action plan to improve the accessibility and quality of information on european union rights in the single market and thus to improve citizens' awareness. during its 10 years of existence, solvit has proven to be a successful project with tangible results. as a further step in the development of the solvit network, the solvit centres in the member states should be empowered to refer findings of non-compliance with single market legislation to the european commission. to realise the full potential of the single market in the future, the solvit network needs to constantly evolve and improve its system of operation.
in writing. - the free solvit system is a public service to eu citizens. it helps them to solve their legal problem concerning rights and freedoms in the internal market quickly and efficiently. among all the advantages, i would highlight above all the informal nature of the procedure and the almost 90% success rate in addressing non-compliance with eu internal market legislation. solvit has provided citizens with a valuable tool to enforce their rights in situations where a member state has incorrectly transposed european legislation into national law. this is most often the case in the area of workers' rights, social security and pension rights. unfortunately, public awareness of solvit remains low. one of the tasks of solvit should also be to increase its visibility among citizens. by removing the various inconsistencies in eu and member state law, we will achieve a better functioning of the internal market for the benefit of eu citizens. for these reasons, i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - what ? one of the primary objectives of the european integration process was to create an area of freedom, security and justice. the adoption of this directive is a step in the right direction, given the state of play of judicial cooperation in terms of guaranteeing security and protection of fundamental rights. the introduction of the european investigation order will increase the use of such a tool by competent authorities in practice. the european union needs to establish a comprehensive system to replace all existing instruments in this area, including the framework decision on the european evidence order, covering as far as possible all types of evidence, including time limits for execution and minimising grounds for refusal. during the negotiations, it was possible to reach agreement on a number of contentious issues, in particular the conditions under which a european investigation order may be issued and the manner in which it is recognised by the executing member state.
in writing. - what ? the annexes to regulation (ec) no 539/2001 specify the countries whose nationals must be in possession of visas when crossing the external borders and those whose nationals are exempt from that requirement. the lists in question must be updated, amended and reviewed at regular intervals and individually on the basis of various factors such as illegal immigration, public policy and security, and external relations with the european union. the european commission is proposing to lift the visa requirement for moldovan citizens. the european commission initiative builds on the progress made by moldova in adopting reforms in the institution building process. the visa liberalisation will bring tangible positive results for moldovan citizens when travelling to the schengen area and will facilitate and intensify people-to-people contacts.
in writing. - what ? the european commission's proposal on visa requirements aims to deepen relations between the european union and the countries concerned. the european commission proposes to lift the visa requirement for citizens of island states in the caribbean and the pacific. the measures provided for in this decision are in accordance with the opinion of the standing committee on plants, animals, food and feed, the european commission has dealt responsibly and with due consideration with the assessment of third countries, which shows that the countries in question do not pose a threat to the european union in terms of illegal immigration, public policy and security. visa liberalisation is of great importance for the citizens of the pacific states, as it significantly reduces their costs of travelling to the schengen area.

in writing. - what ? a large part of the population in iraq continues to be affected by the serious economic and social problems that persist in the country as a result of serious political, security and economic problems. there is an increased violation of press and media freedom by the government and extremist groups. the measures taken by the iraqi government and the recent violent suppression of sunni protests have contributed to the escalation of religious violence in the country. the iraqi government will have to take the necessary measures to ensure the security of all iraqis and to stop the bloodshed and the escalating religious conflict and to promote religious dialogue between sunni and shiite clerics.
in writing. - what ? there are still many violations of fundamental rights within the european union, as evidenced by reports from the european commission, the council of europe and many other documents. concerns were noted and expressed by the actors concerned, in particular as regards the situation of roma, migrants, asylum seekers, refugees, minorities, lesbian, gay, bisexual and transgender persons, the media and journalists, the conduct of the security forces, police and secret services, the investigations necessary to bring to justice and punish those responsible for human rights violations, the involvement of states in cases of torture and ill-treatment in third countries, the use of evidence so obtained, the conditions of detention and ill-treatment of detainees. following the 2011 report, this is the second annual report on the situation of fundamental rights in the european union since the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. the report is divided into two parts, the first of which is institutional, while the second consists of several chapters dealing with specific rights under the charter of fundamental rights. these chapters include minority rights, anti-discrimination, roma issues, lgbt rights and data protection. the institutional part emphasises that fundamental rights are part of the primary law of the european union and as such should be protected through the various instruments currently provided for in the treaties.
in writing. - what ? the european arrest warrant, when put into practice, has acted as a perfect example of mutual recognition in the field of justice and police control. the conditions for its application are rather strict and it should only be applied in the case of serious crime. the european arrest warrant acts as a very useful tool in the pursuit of justice across borders and acts as a deterrent to criminals committing offences. however, this instrument also has shortcomings such as the proportionality check on the basis of all relevant facts and[nbsp]circumstances in order to prevent the issuing of a european arrest warrant in relation to less serious offences or the unreadiness of a specific case for a trial, as well as the right to an effective remedy in the form of an appeal against the issuing of an arrest warrant and the right to compensation in the event of an erroneous arrest warrant. in future, the offences covered by the warrant should also be clearly defined.
in writing. - what ? the application of the european arrest warrant in the area of freedom, security and justice has considerably simplified and, in particular, accelerated the surrender of persons in criminal proceedings. moreover, its very introduction has a preventive effect on potential offenders, since it increases the likelihood that the offender will be apprehended in any member state and subsequently tried and punished. however, in the rule of law, it is necessary to ensure, in the interests of justice, respect for fundamental human and procedural rights, as well as the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality deriving from eu law. since criminal law significantly interferes with the rights of individuals, there is a need to respond urgently to the shortcomings identified so far in the european arrest warrant. these include, in particular, the lack of guarantees for the rights of suspects and accused persons, the absence or imprecise definition of certain basic concepts, and the provision of effective judicial oversight of overall enforcement. i therefore support the request that the commission submit legislative proposals to address the shortcomings identified so far.
in writing. - what ? organized crime is now present in many countries and is far from legal standards, which is why the european arrest warrant needs to be revised. on the one hand, the procedural rights of suspects and accused persons are not respected, the rights of detention are not respected, on the other hand, there is a need to speed up and simplify the procedure for bringing suspects and accused persons to justice. the european arrest warrant should provide a more effective mechanism to ensure that persons seeking to escape justice do not take advantage of open borders. the speed and[nbsp ]simplification of the process of bringing persons to justice will certainly also contribute to confidence in the judiciary and criminal prosecution bodies in the individual member states.
in writing. - what ? the ongoing reform of the common fisheries policy is, inter alia, improving the position and role of women in fisheries, as well as their working, economic and cultural conditions in the fisheries sector. women undoubtedly play an important role in the fishing industry, but there is little mention of equal treatment for men and women. women's work in the fisheries sector often complements men's work, for example in an economic context where they are generally not paid a fixed wage or receive social benefits. the new european fisheries policy should aim to achieve the objectives of equality and historical and social recognition of women's participation in the fisheries sector.
i supported the report because i believe that a european framework is needed to ensure a level playing field for rights holders, consumers, manufacturers and importers of equipment, and service providers throughout the union. i understand that private copying fees are a very important source of income for the cultural sector, that authors of literary and artistic works, singers, musicians and actors claim their rights in accordance with copyright legislation. but i cannot agree to the continued acceptance of the diversity of national systems for charging for private copying. i expect the commission to put pressure on the member states to agree on a common definition of the private copying levy. i also expect that there will be a significant improvement in the awareness of citizens and a simplification of the procedures for setting fees.
in writing. - what ? the european union has a rich history and culture that forms the basis of european identity even in the digital age. the european union is working towards fair remuneration for creators and artists, but the digital market has not yet fulfilled its main objectives in the cultural sector. private digital copying has seen a great expansion in the 21st century. the current system does not take account of the dynamic development of the sector. the directive adopted in the field of reproduction of sound, visual and audiovisual works is outdated and does not reflect the dynamic developments in the field and the needs of consumers and creators. a more effective and up-to-date approach to the problem needs to be developed. the new legislation should balance the legitimate interests of both creators and consumers, and also take into account the wide differences that currently exist at national legislative level. member states should use a proportion of the funding received from private copying revenues to support creative activity in all its forms.
for the efd group. in july 2012, the pilot phase of the european union and european investment bank project bond initiative was launched with the publication of regulation no 670.[nbsp ] the european investment bank has been the initiator of the joint activity use of project bonds to finance strategic trans-european transport, energy and telecommunications projects. the project bonds were also intended to finance innovation in meaningful technologies enhancing the union's competitiveness. the first issue of eu project bonds of eur 1.4 billion was intended to support the castor project, the construction of a large offshore gas storage facility in spain. however, in view of the low performance of this project, it appears necessary for the eib to provide more detailed information on its preparation, in particular in relation to the carrying out of geological studies and seismic risk analyses. according to the information available, the eib has so far approved project bond support mainly for energy and transport projects. however, in order to increase the eu's competitiveness, the information technology sector and the area of research, development and innovation should be supported in particular. i therefore think that the eib should also pay more attention to the usefulness and, in particular, the innovation potential of the projects supported by the bonds.
for the efd group. a large part of the report presented is devoted to social inequalities and discrimination against women caused by eu measures and policies. last week, a report comparing the level of pay between men and women in my country confirmed the fact that women are paid about 20% less than men for the same work. despite the union's stated efforts to eliminate wage discrimination against women, nothing changes in real life in this area. however, in addition to the above wage discrimination against men, women in eastern europe are even more discriminated against in their wages compared to women working for the same employers in the old member states of the union. in my country too, there are multinational chains such as tesco, billa, carrefour, lidl and so on. however, women working in the cashiers, warehouses or plants of these employers in the eu's common economic area receive only half the pay of their colleagues in germany, austria, belgium or the netherlands for the same work in the same companies. however, the price of a litre of milk, baby bread, electricity or hot water is the same as it is practically throughout the union thanks to the common economic area. so the same prices of goods as in the rich eu countries.
i would like to highlight one point in the report, the question of the wage gap, as the commissioner did, which is particularly topical given that the commission adopted recommendations on this last week. despite the long-term improvement in the trend, as the commissioner mentioned, the latest statistics available - many of which are still preliminary - are based on 2012 and show that the wage gap has increased by 0.2% over the last two years, i.e. compared to 2010, in terms of the eu average or the euro area average. for member states, the wage gap widened in 16 countries during this period. the 2012 parliament resolution on this issue emphasised that existing european law in this area is of low impact. the trend is not linear, it varies from situation to situation, and as we can see, regression, negative orientation is one of its characteristics. the commission's recommendations adopted last week are in line with parliament's decision, point in the right direction and are therefore welcome. what leaves something to be desired, however, is the nature of the material. since recommendations are not legally binding, they are not expected to be more effective than binding legislation. and millions of women are suffering because the legislation itself is not effective.
in writing. the economic and financial crisis has changed the face of the european union. it has brought high unemployment and destabilised the social system in many member states. the most vulnerable members of any developed society are children and women. women are at a higher risk of job loss than men. women face reductions in the real value of their pensions and real wages and discrimination in pay. the european union is working towards equality between women and men through the strategy for equality between men and women 2010 2015. i consider the completion of the so-called barcelona goals in the areas of childcare, women's representation in the highest decision-making bodies of companies and violence against women to be a very significant step forward. it is very important to reflect in a broader context on the impact of the major economic and[nbsp]social reforms that have taken place in recent years in the member states and their impact on the social status of women and the decline in the quality of their professional lives. i think it is very important that we minimise the impact of the reforms that have been carried out, because many young girls and women have found themselves in a situation where they have difficulty accessing the labour market.
