torture and other forms of cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment constitute the worst form of violation of human rights, as they directly interfere with the physical and mental integrity of a person. despite an absolute ban, torture persists in many countries of the world, affecting millions of victims, including children. human rights have become an integral part of the eu's foreign policy and must therefore be taken into account in a coherent manner in the conduct of the union's other activities. declaratory sentences for torture are not enough. the union must actively engage in the establishment of effective mechanisms aimed at preventing and[nbsp]repressing torture and rehabilitation. as regards the issue of forced pregnancy, i would like to touch on this, and i call on the union to adopt a very sensitive and cautious approach, since the situation of the mother cannot be dealt with independently of her unborn child. i recall that, in accordance with its obligations under the charter, the union must not promote activities which lead to a violation of the fundamental right to life, which is inherent in both mother and child.
the european union's lax legislation creates a wide space for uncontrolled money movements. the events of the last days in ukraine, for example, have shown us that ukrainian oligarchs have been hiding their dirty money in dutch trusts. the current diverse national value-added tax mechanisms, which allow businesses to generate huge profits through interstate carousel trade, also provide a large space for theft from the state. therefore, the establishment of a central publicly available register of legal entities engaged in trade, as well as the disclosure of the owners of these companies, is a very important step that needs to be implemented as soon as possible. from this point of view, i think the solution proposed is a good step forward.
in writing. - what ? regulation (ec) no 1365/2006 lays down common rules for the production of statistics on inland waterway transport. the main objective of the report is to harmonise the definition of delegated and implementing powers conferred on the european commission in view of the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, and thus to ensure an improvement in the reporting obligation. the european commission is aware of the need to make the major changes in the inland waterway transport sector that are needed to make the sector's full contribution to the europe 2020 strategy. the necessary reforms are also important in view of the growing importance of inland waterway transport not only for the transport of goods but also for the transport of passengers in cities through which watercourses flow. data from water infrastructure statistics are needed to improve the situation and implement the necessary reforms. the review provides a unique opportunity to obtain the information in question.
in writing. - what ? the economic and financial crisis has also brought about, inter alia, the need to improve the quality of statistics transmitted by the member states of the european union on macroeconomic imbalances, as well as the prevention and correction of excessive imbalances within the european union. the early identification of macroeconomic imbalances is a very important factor in preventing economic difficulties. the logic of the macroeconomic imbalance procedure framework is to limit the sanctions applied to countries whose currency is the euro and to allocate the fines collected to the european stability mechanism.
in writing. - the nagoya protocol was adopted at the international summit on biological diversity held on 29 october 2010 in japan. the main objective of this convention is to ensure fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from the utilization of genetic resources and thereby contribute to the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity. key elements of the protocol include a commitment by the parties to[nbsp]sharing the benefits arising from the exploitation and subsequent use of genetic resources, as well as a transparent and effective system of supervision and control in conjunction with judicial cooperation. the nagoya protocol provides greater legal certainty and transparency to the parties for providers as well as users of genetic resources. it is therefore necessary for the european union and all its member states to ratify it as soon as possible. the european union is a dominant player in the use of genetic resources and a sustainable approach to this area is very important, both from the point of view of research centres and the industries that use them.
in writing. - what ? the economic and financial crisis has also affected the world trade sector to a considerable extent. the globalisation adjustment fund was established to provide necessary and indispensable support to workers affected by major structural changes in the world trade sector. on 28 january 2014, the european commission approved the mobilisation of the fund for the benefit of spanish workers made redundant in 198 enterprises. eur 840 000 from the european globalisation adjustment fund is to be used for the reintegration of 560 staff. the spanish textile industry is currently facing changing market conditions due to global competition, in particular from china. this appropriation is intended to cover the cost of the purchase, hire or leasing of equipment and installations, including the purchase, hire or leasing of furniture, appliances and equipment, and the purchase of furniture, furniture and appliances, as well as the purchase, hire or leasing of furniture, appliances and equipment, and the purchase of furniture, appliances and equipment, including the purchase, hire or leasing of furniture, appliances and equipment, and the purchase of furniture, appliances and equipment.
both committees dealing with the proposed regulations on the production of plant reproductive material rejected it. the committee on agriculture adopted the negative opinion by an overwhelming majority. the environment committee even unanimously rejected the commission's proposal. it is clear that the commission has failed to convince and allay the concerns of citizens and small farmers about the negative impact of this proposal on the agricultural sector, in particular on those producers who strive to grow quality and healthy food and who also care about biodiversity and environmental protection in europe. i share these concerns and have therefore supported parliament's rejection.
in writing. - the commission's proposal for a regulation on the production and placing on the market of plant reproductive material does not meet the expectations of meps, farmers and many citizens. in many respects, it fails to meet its stated objectives, does not take into account the specificities of the member states, the needs of producers and consumers, and does not ensure sufficient environmental protection. i do not agree with the commission's approach of replacing the current directives with a directly applicable regulation, which will, moreover, be complemented by a large number of delegated acts. delegated acts, as we all know, give the commission extensive powers and therefore member states would lose the possibility to choose their own way of achieving the objective set, as is hitherto possible in the context of the current directives. for the reasons i have given, i voted against the adoption of this proposal for a regulation on seeds.
in writing. - the regulation in question lays down the procedures which must be put in place in order to successfully implement the trade aspects of the stabilisation and association agreement between the european union and the republic of serbia. the amendments proposed by the european commission and the council aim to ensure consistency with the omnibus i and ii packages, thereby bringing the rules in the field of trade policy into line with the regime of the new system of delegated and implementing acts introduced by the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon.
the financial crisis has exposed the shortcomings of the existing supervision of financial institutions. it also reminded us that the european economy is already so interconnected that the failure of one article immediately translates into the whole financial market. i am not an expert on banking, insurance or the stock market, but i believe that the proposed mechanism for assessing systemic risks in this sector will prevent a future crisis scenario that has affected millions of businesses and households. i therefore supported the motion.
in writing. - what ? the fight against money laundering and terrorism is a priority for the european union and requires billions of euros from the budget every year. the european union aims in the near future to make the fight against money laundering and terrorist financing more effective and tougher by increasing the transparency of all types of crossborder and[nbsp]domestic transfers of funds. it is essential that the competent national authorities designated for the enforcement of money laundering law have easier access to basic information on the principal transferring funds. one way of combating money laundering is to require payment service providers to record information on the payer and the payee when making transfers. among other things, strengthening the position of financial service providers is also part of the new and updated recommendations of the financial action task force. every year, huge damage is done to the economy due to the lack of protection of financial flows from being misused by criminals and terrorists. if the european union is to succeed, it will have to put more emphasis in the future on the clarity and consistency of rules in individual member states, thus protecting european solidarity and the economy.
in the fight against money laundering, it is important to know who is the real owner of a business or a business transaction. only in this way can the legalization of proceeds of crime be prevented. businesses must be able to verify the true owners so that they can be held accountable for activities that could launder money tainted by crime. it is therefore necessary to improve the functioning of business registers in the member states to include beneficial ownership information. the interconnection of national registers is also important in view of the european internal market. i also supported the proposal given the negative experience in slovakia, where for years the owners of some companies suspected of money laundering have not been traced.
in writing. - what ? the misuse of the financial system for money laundering purposes causes huge losses to the european union's economy every year. the european union needs to make the operation of business registers more efficient and better, as identifying the beneficial owner of a business or a business transaction is crucial in the effort to prevent the financing of terrorism and the use of the financial system for money laundering. improving the functioning of trade registers should provide the necessary beneficial ownership information to help the competent authorities and[nbsp]the undertakings themselves to verify the beneficiaries of the profits from commercial transactions. the interconnection of national registers is of great importance for the exploitation of the necessary information in the conduct of cross-border business and for the interconnection of the internal market, which has not yet reached its full potential. the fight against money laundering should not forget about data protection and the fundamental rights of citizens of the european union.
in writing. - i welcome the adoption of the directive on the prevention of the use of the financial system for the purpose of money laundering and, like all decent eu citizens, i welcome the fact that the union has started to tighten the requirements for banks, financial institutions, auditors, accountants, tax and legal advisers in the area of suspicious financial transfers within the eu and from abroad. the damage caused to the economies of states and consequently to their citizens is a reason to introduce greater transparency of operations and transactions, as according to available un data, the amount of money laundering is around eur 330[nbsp ]billion[nbsp ]a year and represents about 3[nbsp ]% of global gdp. i agree[nbsp ]with the urgent need to establish a coordinated european approach by member states and to adopt effective preventive and repressive measures to combat this serious crime. the perpetrators and their accomplices must not escape their just punishment. i voted in favour of the draft money laundering directive and i believe that the money laundering directive will be finally adopted in the very near future in accordance with the ordinary legislative procedure in cooperation with the council.
in writing. - what ? the scale of money laundering is enormous. the international monetary fund estimates that money laundering amounts to 5[nbsp]% of global gdp. therefore, any measure is welcome and should be encouraged. the adopted directive will certainly help the functioning of business registers in the member states, which will include information on beneficial ownership and help to identify the real profits of companies, especially in cross-border business transactions. funds derived from illegal activities are mostly used for terrorism, leading to loss of revenue for governments and impeding competition. they also adversely affect the smooth functioning of markets and disrupt development. the fight against money laundering must be a priority for the eu as a whole and for each member state, so that the conversion of proceeds from criminal activities into legitimate means through the financial system is not possible, or that such activities can be detected, eliminated and punished.
in writing. - the european union, through the european investment bank, seeks to support various investment projects outside the territory of the member states in order to meet the objectives of the european union's external policy. the new legislation will set out the overall scope and general conditions for the financing of the european investment bank under the european union guarantee for the years 2014 to 2020. the total ceiling for financing operations was increased to eur 30 million following the intervention of the european parliament, while additional funding was secured through backflows from previous european investment bank activities. under the new approach, the european union will significantly reduce its investments in the balkans, the eastern partnership countries and north africa. the european union must show zero tolerance towards activities carried out for illegal purposes, including money laundering, terrorist financing and tax fraud, which harm the financial interests of the european investment bank.
in writing. - the european union is a dominant player in the use of genetic resources and a sustainable approach to this area is very important, both from the point of view of research centres and the industries that use them. the regulation covers the use and protection of genetic resources available to states and over which they exercise sovereign rights and the application of traditional knowledge that will be accessible after the entry into force of the nagoya protocol. the rules that have been created serve to protect and preserve biodiversity. the main objective of the proposed regulation is to implement an international agreement into european union law. it is important to achieve full compliance with the nagoya protocol and this requires the strengthening of certain mechanisms. a clear legislative framework will bring benefits and benefits to all users, in particular to public and economic actors, as it will provide them with permanent access to genetic resources, but also legal certainty in their management.
in writing. - what ? studies examining the relationship between accidents and technical defects in cars have not reached a clear conclusion as to the impact of more frequent inspections on road safety. the rules on their frequency are therefore not justified. the obligation to carry out an annual vehicle maintenance check would lead to a disproportionate increase in financial costs for ordinary users. the average age of cars in slovakia is still more than 11 years. people would feel it really strongly. in these economically difficult times, it is therefore imperative that such changes be postponed.
in writing. - the european institutions are working to increase road safety and reduce the high accident rate through a package of vehicle health measures and to enforce new rules on vehicle health checks. before a manufacturer can place a new motor vehicle on the market, the vehicle in question will have to meet the relevant type-approval or individual approval requirements. these requirements shall ensure a sufficient level of safety and environmental standards. once these conditions have been met, each member state will have to register the vehicle in question without exception, and the certificate issued will serve as an official authorisation for the use of the vehicle on public roads within the european union. the new proposal aims to define in more detail the place of registration of vehicles and to introduce the institution of withdrawal or total cancellation of the registration certificate. through the deregistration institute, the european commission wants to reduce the number of vehicles that endanger road safety and traffic flow.
in writing. - what ? the deterioration of road safety on the roads in the european union is a serious problem that needs to be addressed urgently. the current directives in force no longer sufficiently cover the sector and its dynamic development. the establishment of new regulations and rules on the admissibility of new vehicles to the market is a step forward, but road safety itself depends on the capabilities and behaviour of road users and it is therefore important to deepen the education, training and enforcement of road users' rules. the role of the responsible authorities should therefore be to create conditions for road users which take account of human error and misconduct on the road.
in writing. - the european commission proposes to amend regulation no 91/2003 in order to simplify the existing rules. the changes concern in particular the concept of simplified reporting, which has placed an undue burden on the member states of the european union and on respondents, but has not produced the necessary results and significantly improved the quality of data on rail passenger and freight transport. the european commission must ensure that there is no double collection of statistical data, as for example with accident data collected by the european railway agency. the existing statistics should be extended to include new data classes in the future.
in writing. - what ? it is well known that the widely recognised benefits of electronic procurement have led several member states to require the submission of electronic invoices for public procurement in the whole or in certain parts of the public sector. electronic invoicing and support for electronic procurement are tools that need to be modernised and used as a step-by-step approach to paperless public administration. the digital sector is currently advancing at a rapid pace and is threatened by market fragmentation due to the high number of national e-invoicing regulations. the different technical solutions of the member states continue to exacerbate the interoperability problem. the large number of different requirements, standards and information networks are currently too complex for businesses and lead to legal uncertainty, as businesses cannot rely on the fact that an invoice will be accepted in another member state.
in writing. - what ? the european investment bank's main task is to support the strategic short- and long-term objectives of the european union, and its shareholders are the 28 member states of the european union. the european investment bank is the largest multilateral borrower and lender, providing finance and expertise for investment projects that help the european union achieve its stated objectives. the european parliament shall examine each year the banking activities of the european investment bank on the basis of the available financial and statistical reports. however, the audit shows that the volume of loans signed is below existing possibilities and that in some member states there are no projects that meet the criteria for financing through the european investment bank. the european investment bank should in future review its own resources policy and actively seek out projects that could be supported, thus promoting projects and sustainable development in the member states of the european union. the european investment bank's cooperation with intermediaries with negative records is also in question.
the report by mrs zuber, the portuguese member of parliament, on equality between women and men in the european union for 2012, which is being debated today, is another in a series of initiatives against life, the family and motherhood, and i could not therefore support it. this report understands women's equality as equality and ignores the life choices of many women mothers. to say that a woman is inferior and discriminated against when she chooses to prioritize childcare over her career is an insult to every woman. of course, in a just society, women and men must be guaranteed equal opportunities that reflect their individual capabilities, and this cannot be achieved by quotas or by mandatory gender training of children in schools. and a separate chapter is the demand to ensure women's right to terminate a pregnancy, another of the attempts to make abortion a human right.
in writing. - what ? the economic and financial crisis has changed the face of the european union. it has brought high unemployment and destabilised the social system in many member states. the most vulnerable members of any developed society are children and women. women are at a higher risk of job loss than men. women face reductions in the real value of their pensions and real wages and discrimination in pay. the european union is working towards gender equality through the gender equality strategy 2010-2015. complementing the barcelona objectives on childcare, women's representation in the highest decision-making bodies of companies and violence against women is a very important step forward. it is important to reflect in a broader context on the impact of the major economic and social reforms undertaken in recent years in the member states and their impact on the social status of women and the decline in the quality of their professional lives. minimising the effects of reforms will be one of the main tasks for the european institutions in the future.
in writing. - since 2012, the number of unemployed people in the eu has been rising steadily, affecting women more than men, often the victims of the economic situation. uncertain employment, reduced wages and job losses often put them at a social disadvantage, and the livelihood of their families is threatened. the entry of young women into the labour market is also uncertain. skilled and intellectual women try to find jobs abroad in order to make a better living. the migration of young people leads to the ageing of a region, to economic decline. monitoring the 'youth guarantee' programme would ensure more effective and flexible progress in the member states in terms of the access of young people, including young women, to the labour market. within the family, women usually have the responsibility of caring for children as well as for the elderly. unfortunately, the social benefits that come with it don't provide a fraction of the livelihood. affordable care solutions that improve women's employability are needed. unfortunately, women's underrepresentation is still a problem in some areas of employment and in political representation. i agree that women's representation is important in these areas and that political parties have a key role to play in encouraging women's active and passive participation in elections. i voted in favour of the report because of the above.
in writing. - what ? the 2014 gender equality report highlights the many problems caused by the existence of gender inequalities. it also seeks to highlight the effects of austerity measures and the economic crisis and their negative impact on equality between women and men. i believe that a report of such political significance deserves a wider public debate. by rejecting the report, we have only confirmed the efforts of some member states to divert attention from the consequences of austerity measures imposed on women during the crisis by several eu governments. on the other hand, i really don't understand how anyone can vote against a report on promoting equality, combating violence against women, improving business conditions, just because it mentions promoting women's reproductive and sexual rights. the report does, however, list problems that have a real and daily impact on the lives of the female population living in the european area. the demonization of the news known as estrela, lunáček and this time zuber is incomprehensible from my point of view. but there is one thing we cannot deny. every human being, without distinction, has the right to a life of dignity and to the pursuit of happiness. for these reasons, i disagree with the final vote and the associated negative attitudes towards women and their overall position in society.
in writing. - the european union recognises the great importance of the openness of its institutions to public scrutiny by citizens and ngos. transparency is a very important factor for a modern and democratically functioning society. public access to documents is a fundamental right of its citizens. however, some institutions continue to hide behind various exceptions, which do not give a positive image of their functioning in the eyes of the citizens of the european union. the european commission must establish clear and understandable criteria for the classification of documents at european union level. just as the european union requires its citizens to properly apply and comply with current legislation in force, so too should its institutions, in particular regulation no [nbsp ]1049/2001 on public access to documents.
in writing. - what ? public affairs in a democratic and rule of law state should be conducted transparently under the supervision of citizens in order to prevent abuse of power, corruption, inaction of public institutions and other undesirable practices associated with unjustified secrecy. the public has a right to truthful information about the activities of public authorities, since without sufficient and timely information it is not possible to make the right choice of its representatives or to make informed decisions aimed at improving the overall functioning of public authorities. institutions and bodies should not forget that they are not created for their own sake, but for the citizens, because their legitimacy derives from them. for the reasons set out above, i supported the european parliament's report which critically assessed the current state of public access to documents in the years 2011-2013 and which calls for effective correction of the errors found.
madam president, i rise to congratulate the work of the committee on petitions. i would have liked this report to pay due tribute to the great success of the citizens' initiative 'one of us', on the implementation of union law and the protection of the human embryo. in writing. - what ? it is essential for the future of the european union that democratic principles continue to develop and that citizens' participation in decision-making is increased, and the european union has not become detached from its citizens. the enhancement of transparency and accountability will ensure full respect for european union law. it is vital that the european union regains the confidence of its citizens, which has been eroded by the outbreak of the economic and financial crisis. it is therefore understandable that this turbulent period, coupled with a weakening of confidence in institutions and the banking sector, also brought with it a relatively high number of petitions submitted by european citizens. in 2013, a very small number of petitions were considered, which is regrettable, and the european union should give them much more importance. objectively, the number of petitions more than doubled compared to 2012, and in 2013 the committee was also responsible for organising the election of the new ombudsman and carried out more activities than in the past. in 2013, the committee on petitions met 11 times and many petitioners and representatives of member states' authorities attended its meetings to provide their explanations in relation to the allegations in question.
in writing. - what ? the right of appeal and its application is an important right of eu citizens to draw the attention of the union institutions and the member states' authorities to specific problems arising from the implementation of european legislation. as the only directly elected institution in the eu legal system, the european parliament, through its committee on petitions, provides a transparent mechanism through which citizens can obtain an out-of-court remedy for their complaints, subject to the basic conditions laid down. in this context, i would like to thank all citizens who take an active interest in public affairs in the union and draw the attention of legislators, through their initiatives and through petitions, to the legal deficiencies in everyday practice. this feedback from citizens is of immense importance in creating better legislation for the benefit of the people. as regards the handling of petitions, the number of which is growing significantly year on year, amounting to almost 3[nbsp ]000 in 2013 alone, its speed depends on the nature and complexity of the case. i would stress that the european parliament's objective is to deliver within a reasonable time. in conclusion, i would like to express my satisfaction that petitions concerning disabled people are being dealt with as a matter of priority.
in writing. - i am convinced that every citizen of the union now has a sufficient set of rights, which not only benefit the 500 million europeans who can enjoy them, but also the eu economy as a whole. i supported the report because i believe that the right to petition is one of the [nbsp ]forms of direct democracy which allows citizens to express their views and to have a say in matters of public or other common interest which concern all eu citizens. although i cannot be entirely satisfied with the number of petitions filed and not dealt with in 2013, i am sure that the eu has taken the right path in terms of the right to petition, which can also help to rebuild trust in the union, which has also been weakened by the impact of the financial and economic crisis. in the context of strengthening the rights of european citizens, i consider it particularly important that petitions sent are properly and timely examined and that opinions are not sent by the competent authorities only when the petition authority grants a favourable opinion, but also in cases where it does not grant a favourable opinion, but that its opinion on the legality and appropriateness of its action is duly and[nbsp ]convincingly justified to the petitioner. i expect the committee on petitions to review the whole process of dealing with petitions and to give greater weight to inadmissible petitions in its work.
in writing. - what ? the european union is the second largest importer and producer of fruit and vegetables and the world's largest producer of flowers, onions and nuts. it is therefore important to clearly support the horticultural sector for the future, which represents 18%[nbsp ] of the european union's agricultural production. the european union must improve its fight against unfair trading practices and overcome the growing number of non-tariff barriers that make exports more difficult, if not impossible. in the future, it will be very important to encourage growers to join producer organisations, thereby supporting the bargaining power of the sector and contributing to increased investment that will help innovation, research and climate improvement. the ever-increasing demands and conveniences of consumers are significantly reducing profitability and increasing costs for businesses operating in the sector. the european commission must reduce the regulatory burden on businesses in the horticulture sector in the near future.

in writing. - what ? the european union's established guidelines and their implementation in the fight against torture and other ill-treatment appear to be insufficient once again. a new initiative by member states and the establishment of rehabilitation centres for victims of torture is needed, as the world is increasingly confronted with the torture of children and child pornography. it is regrettable that efforts to enforce fundamental human, social, cultural and economic rights are often met with harsh repression, with the inevitable consequence of the loss of life of the poorest members of society. violence against women and children in developing countries has a significant impact on their social and economic inclusion and creates social divisions in society. european parliament delegations must step up their efforts and visit prisons and other places of detention more frequently where there is a justified suspicion of torture or ill-treatment of the accused. the european union, in cooperation with the united nations, plays a crucial role in the fight against torture worldwide and the principle of zero tolerance for torture and the promotion and protection of fundamental human rights must therefore be strengthened. the european commission must give greater support to regional organisations and mechanisms for the prevention of torture.
in writing. - what ? despite the fact that international law prohibits torture, we regret that in many countries torture is still tolerated, and even more disturbingly, in some countries torture is even part of the legal system. i personally consider torture in any form to be a crime and i am strongly in favour of the absolute and unequivocal prohibition of torture and cruel, inhuman and degrading treatment in the [nbsp ] union. i am also sorry that the implementation of the eu guidelines on torture is still insufficient and that we still have many cases of violations of human rights and fundamental freedoms, which take a heavy toll on millions of individuals and families. as europe is home to more than 400 000 people who have had a personal experience of torture and the impunity of the perpetrators persists, i expect the eu and its member states to take measures to ensure that victims are thoroughly investigated in their cases of torture and that perpetrators are brought to justice. in this context, i consider it of particular importance that the eu and its member states, in the context of torture and ill-treatment, pay increased attention to new and emerging forms of violence perpetrated against the most vulnerable groups, in particular women and children, via the internet.
in an age of electronicisation of both the public and private sectors, it is very difficult to ensure the protection of natural persons with regard to the processing of their personal data. the free movement of persons, goods and services, as one of the benefits and rights of eu citizens, creates major difficulties for the protection of personal data of individuals, also due to the heterogeneous legislation in the member states. in my country, slovakia, we have stricter legislation than the european one, which protects the personal data of people, employees, patients, clients, etc. very effectively, but business and employers object because the cost of ensuring the protection of personal data has increased. it is therefore essential that personal data is only provided to the extent strictly necessary, that the rules on the protection of personal data are harmonised across the eu and that misuse of personal data is sanctioned.
a few months ago, a case broke out that made america synonymous with secret wiretapping, spying and unfair practices against its own allies. the incident also triggered a debate on data security and protection in europe. by the way, it was about time. there are some serious aspects to the debate. there are two principles that i think we should follow. first, the collection and processing of personal data must be of a sound nature, not unlimited and not without rules. second, trust but verify should apply to our partners as well. however, i do not think that the agreements with the usa, and in particular the ttip negotiations, should be called into question and used as a tool in this case. whatever our views on the partnership with the us and whatever our views on the importance of data sharing, these issues need to be separated. so let's focus on identifying our own problems as quickly as possible and being able to respond appropriately to them. we now have the opportunity to start the discussion and prepare modern legislation, including a digital habeas corpus, which will guarantee an additional level of security in the handling of data and will guarantee increased protection of personal data for our citizens.
, in writing. the european union has made a strong commitment to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 80-95% by 2050 compared to 1990 levels. emissions of all greenhouse gases are declining, but fluorinated gases have increased by 60%. products containing these and similar types of gases can have a lifespan of up to 50 years, and therefore we need further legislation to prevent further growth of these types of gases in the atmosphere. various sustainable alternatives are available almost today, and almost all new fluoride-based equipment can be replaced by other options. we therefore need to push for the phasing out of hfcs from the market. the current phase-out schedule should be tightened to ensure a feasible and cost-effective transition to sustainable alternatives. further research and development can not only provide new impetus but also ensure sustainable growth and innovation. experience with backfilling shows that the measure can achieve significant hfc emission reductions in a cost-effective manner in a short time. in order to ensure the most efficient recovery, recovery schemes should be established in all member states to ensure the recycling of such gases. in order to improve the level of compliance and enforcement, a maximum level of leakage loss should be set for each type of equipment and precautions should be taken to prevent leakage before it occurs.
rapporteur. - mr president, first of all i would like to thank all the shadows and colleagues with whom i worked on this directive. thanks to them the text was enriched. i also appreciate the efforts of the lithuanian presidency to reach a good agreement. free movement of citizens is one of the core values of the european union. almost 60 years later, however, we still have to admit that there is no smooth free movement of workers in the union. according to commission statistics, 97[nbsp ]% of eu citizens do not move for work, while the other three freedoms are much more widely applicable, having in mind the free movement of capital, goods and services. what is the problem with the free movement of workers? why does the regulation in force not solve the situation? the problem is that in different member states, workers coming from other member states are still discriminated against and are facing unjustified obstacles. recognising the problems in this field, the commission decided to propose this piece of legislation in order to facilitate the free movement of union citizens by enforcing the existing regulation. we have to keep in mind that this directive deals only with european citizens. in the proposal, the commission uses the expression ‘migrant’ workers, which can be misinterpreted by extending it to third-country nationals. avoiding this, we have agreed with the council to use the term ‘union workers’ so that it is crystal clear that the directive is applicable only to union citizens. furthermore, this directive deals with discrimination based on nationality and residence and does not include other forms of discrimination. in addition, the scope of the directive is extended, in the same way as that of the regulation, to frontier workers (those who live in cross-border regions and only commute to work). it is also applied to seasonal workers. another new, core element in the directive is that it calls for closer and more intensive cooperation between national authorities both at national and european level. in order not to put an extra burden on the member states, they do not have to establish new bodies whose task is to coordinate or facilitate the free moment of workers. in addition, the member states can decide that more than one body will be in a charge of this issue. however, we found it essential to include in the text the idea that, if more than one body is responsible for free movement, there must be cooperation and coordination and it has to be clear who is in charge of coordination. finally, the essential element of this directive is citizens’ access to free, easily-understandable, comprehensive and up-to-date information and assistance wherever and whenever they need it. the european union is the biggest and most prosperous market of goods, services and capital. if we want it to be prosperous to its fullest extent, we have to ensure that all union workers have access to this huge european labour market and that every union worker is treated equally, no matter which member state he or she comes from.
the rapporteur. - i'd like to thank my colleagues who are still here at this late hour. a few comments on what was said in the debate: it is possible to quantify that the value created by those working in other member states far exceeds the expenditure that individual member states contribute to social benefits through benefits. i think it is important to clarify this again, and i thank commissioner andor for clarifying it. it is surprising on the one hand, not surprising on the other, but again, you find yourself wondering why, when the other three freedoms - the free flow of capital, the free flow of goods, the free flow of services - are not questioned by colleagues. and there are side effects to that. life is not black and white. likewise, free movement has side effects. and i was more concerned about the tens of thousands of children whose parents left them because they went to work in another member state. perhaps it is because some member states are questioning whether they are entitled to family allowances or whether they can study in their schools. for me, these abandoned children are a much bigger problem than the few tens of percent that each member state spends more on welfare. unfortunately, it's always easy to make a stranger into a scapegoat. but i think it's extremely dangerous, and i'm sure that sooner or later, colleagues will become aware of this. thank you very much. perhaps one last comment: we have received some amendments which neglect the principle of free movement. i'm asking my colleagues, after difficult negotiations, we have reached such a compromise. this compromise, this was the best compromise available. i urge you to support this compromise and reject the amendments.
russia's invasion of crimea is a relic of the soviet mentality. ukraine and crimea are a european matter and we bear responsibility for them. the ukrainian sovereignty has been violated and an aggressive russian invasion of crimea is underway. the so-called referendum is a desperate attempt to cement this situation. we must use all the political and diplomatic means at our disposal and use our own means, all of them, boldly and fearlessly. we need to really show that we are on ukraine's side and that we can prevent further escalation of tensions. it is also necessary to commend the government of yatsenyuk, which has taken a reasonable position and does not react to provocations. they should also be clearly assured that we fully support a democratic and free ukraine.
in writing. the current situation in ukraine would have seemed like the script for a new hollywood thriller just a few months ago. the action is picking up moment by moment, what seems unimaginable to us now may be a harsh reality tomorrow. ukraine is a country characterized by the term 'democratic transition', but after the latest news, i will look at this term forever differently. all this is happening at the doorstep of the european union. ukrainians have not even recovered from one revolution and now have another, not to mention the civil unrest that has resembled a civil war in the last days of the maidan. the golden nail of the program, of course, remains the sharpening of the dispute over russia, which under the pretext of protecting the russian population living in the southern part of ukraine allowed itself to occupy the crimean peninsula. protests continue to sweep the country, both anti-russian and pro-russian. anti-russian protesters in the southeast are demanding independence, so the partition of ukraine is also on the line. the eu is also actively involved in the fight against terrorism and the fight against organised crime. the only thing left to do is to hope for an early and diplomatic solution to the conflict, which can be helped by a firm but above all united eu position.
in writing. colleagues, what is happening in ukraine today is an attempt to change the geopolitical balance of power in europe. and not only that: the principles of post-war reorganization. it is not acceptable that minorities are again used and abused to change the borders between states. these methods led to the second world war and we must not accept them today at any cost. yes, it is our fault that we did not provide russia with security guarantees in the 1990s in the face of nato's reckless expansion. today, russia seeks to secure its own security by creating spheres of influence and annexing various territories. it's imperial expansion, and it's by exploiting its own minorities in neighboring countries. in the case of ukraine, it brought us to the brink of a military confrontation. but europe must not show weakness here, on the contrary, it must show strength. the weakness of the eu will not lead to de-escalation of the conflict, as some think, but to a feeling of russian strength and attempts to further expansion. we must make it clear within the eu that no minority policy in neighbouring countries can lead to a change of borders and that the eu will not support such a move anywhere in europe, but must be determined to prevent it by all means. otherwise, we risk war chaos throughout europe.

in writing. - pakistan has been marked by natural disasters that are damaging its economy and slowing its development, as evidenced by the drop in its human development index listing. social disparities in the country are widening due to the persistent dangers of floods and earthquakes. the country continues to have a problem of uneven feudal land ownership, with a small number of people owning nearly 40%[nbsp] of the land and others having difficult access to irrigation and other production facilities, which in rural areas fuels violence and spreads social instability. progressive reform in the areas of human rights and media freedom is of great importance for the development of pakistan. in the future, major reform of the education system and improvement in the status of women will also be needed, as society has not seen any improvement in the fight against domestic violence and crime. the european union expressed concern at pakistan's blasphemy laws, which often lead to the death penalty and are abused to fight political opponents. the pakistani authorities must take the necessary steps to comply with international commitments leading to improved working conditions and safety standards.

in writing. - what ? the new data protection regulation replaces the directive and is necessary because of the lack of transparency in implementation and the enforced differences between member states, which have become too substantive and confusing for individuals, but also for businesses and executive bodies. the data protection regulation in question lays down rules concerning the protection of natural persons with regard to the processing of data and rules concerning the free movement of personal data. the european union aims to protect the fundamental rights and freedoms of natural persons to the greatest extent possible. the updating of the regulation improves the level of protection and legal certainty in respect of the observance of the rights of individuals. the main improvements of the new regulation include more precise and clearer definitions that better stand the test of time, clear principles for data processing, a strong definition of consent, reduction of the administrative burden for small and medium-sized enterprises, harmonised enforcement of rules across the european union that guarantee a level playing field for all citizens and the business community.
in writing. - what ? until now, the european union has had a directive on the protection of personal data, adopted in 1995. in view of the rapidly evolving electronic world and the transfer of data to third countries, i agree with a thorough reform of the european union's rules. i believe people have the right to protect and control the spread of information about their personal data. on the other hand, the unification of rules in the eu will also benefit companies with crossborder operations. the stricter rules for data exchange between countries provide for penalties for companies that violate the regulation of up to 5[nbsp ]% of the revenue from total sales. the processing of personal data revealing racial or ethnic origin, political opinions, religious or philosophical beliefs, sexual orientation or gender identity, etc., must be prohibited. i fully support the new rules which make it clear that classifying individuals on the basis of their personal characteristics is unacceptable. i am very pleased that after years of hard work, we can today freely declare that we take the protection of the fundamental rights of our citizens seriously. i think that the final text of the directive, as well as the regulation, clearly reflects the main problems of our time. it is now up to the governments in the member states themselves to adopt both legislative texts, thus confirming their commitment to protecting the personal data of their citizens.

in writing. - what ? children are the weakest and most vulnerable members of society and deserve to be protected and brought up in favourable conditions without restrictions on their fundamental rights and freedoms. yet, in many lands around the world, children are called into armed conflict, resulting in their separation from their families. such separation has a profound and negative impact on the physical and mental development of children, as they are often subjected to sexual abuse and forced to kill at a young age. since 1996, more than 2 million children have lost their lives in armed conflicts around the world. many countries have now signed action plans whose main objective is to end the recruitment of minors into armed forces, which results in the return of millions of children to their natural environment and the prosecution of the perpetrators. the primary role in child protection must be played by the states concerned themselves, but the role of armed non-state actors, such as the geneva call, is also important.
in writing. - what ? the various forms of protection of natural persons are among the main priorities of the european union's policies. the institutions of the european union seek to protect the personal data of natural persons while ensuring a high level of public security and ensuring the exchange of information between competent authorities within the eu. the scope of the new directive is therefore not limited to cross-border data processing but covers all processing activities carried out by competent authorities. the main objective is to repeal the old and malfunctioning framework decision 2008/977/jha and to bring about a higher level of protection of personal data and a greater enforceability of rights and legitimate interests. rapid technological developments in the field of personal data protection bring new challenges and technology allows public authorities to use personal data on an unprecedented scale. in the near future, the competent authorities must decide how to use and exploit personal data and prevent misuse in different areas.
in writing. - what ? the level of protection of personal data in the eu has become significantly outdated in the light of new scientific and technological advances. many eu citizens are fearful and frustrated at losing control over their data, which can be easily and quickly exploited, especially in the area of information technology use. the protection of personal data must therefore be adequately ensured by the competent european data protection authorities, which will ensure that such information is not misused. i agree with the rapporteur that the processing of personal data must be legitimate, transparent, proportionate and limited in time and that the purpose of the collection must be clearly defined in advance. the infringement of legal obligations by operators must be detected and punished by fines which are sufficiently dissuasive also for international businesses and other companies. i therefore voted in favour of adopting a more modern legal framework which will ensure a higher level of protection of personal data. however, i believe that the proposal could have been more ambitious in many respects.

in writing. - the single internal market within the eu must also allow travellers to enjoy uniform protection and clear, unambiguous rights, regardless of[nbsp ]the member state in which they live, work or purchase a package. when purchasing a package of travel services, whether in person or through a broker or via the internet, the european consumer must have all relevant information at his disposal before concluding the contract of sale. every year we see cases where a consumer, through the insolvency of the retailer or the organiser of the package, is unable to provide the consumer with the adequate service he has purchased. full harmonisation of legislation is therefore necessary in the interests of consumers. i also welcome the obligation to provide adequate assistance to passengers who find themselves in difficulty during the journey or holiday, even if the organiser is not to blame and the financial assistance is at the expense of the traveller.

in writing. - what ? this directive aims to improve the enforceability of regulation 492/2011 on the free movement of workers within the european union. the main objective is to remove existing obstacles to the mobility of workers within the european union and to the enforcement of their rights under eu law. many workers in the territory of the member states face discrimination on grounds of nationality and are not provided with basic information in the field of job search. the european union must provide fair and transparent conditions for all european citizens who decide to move to another member state to pursue a profession. the social partners must act more decisively in the protection of mobile workers and in the prevention of unequal treatment.
in writing. - as a consequence of the economic crisis, many people are looking for work in other countries, in other eu member states, far from their home country. the directive on the facilitation of the free movement of workers is intended to complement the existing legal framework on this subject. the free movement of workers is one of the fundamental freedoms of eu citizens and a cornerstone of the internal market. the directive aims to address the problems of permanent workers, seasonal workers and crossborder workers. the situation of labour-scarce regions may improve. the rights contained in the regulations are not always and adequately exercised in practice by the member states of the union vis-à-vis the citizens of other member states, and eu citizens enjoying the right of free movement are therefore often subject to discrimination on grounds of nationality. i hope that the directive will put an end to such discriminatory procedures in the member states. member states have a duty to inform workers of their rights. precise definitions are needed to make it clear which jobs can be filled by foreigners. improving cooperation and mutual trust between member states, including through a well-functioning network of contacts, would help to ensure the right to free movement. i believe that simplifying the administrative procedures that affect citizens living and working in the union would also make their daily lives easier. that is why i voted in favour of this proposal.
in writing. - what ? although only three per cent of all eu citizens live in a member state other than their country of origin, i consider the free movement of workers within eu member states to be one of the eu's most significant achievements. we declared 2013 the european year of citizens, with the aim of ensuring the effective enforcement of eu citizens' rights, including free movement. i regret to say that i have personally encountered many times in recent times that citizens who were interested in travelling or who had travelled to another country for work encountered a number of problems and obstacles that prevented them from working in another member state. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that it not only identifies and points out these shortcomings, but also comes up with concrete proposals and measures that can significantly help to eliminate these persistent problems. as i see labour mobility as one of the alternatives that gives workers the opportunity to improve their living and working conditions, i expect the eu and the individual member states to comply more strictly with the provisions of the founding treaties and to increase awareness of eu rules.
in writing. - what ? copernicus is the successor to gmes, which provides important environmental information through earth observation using satellites and other advanced technology, and tracks natural or man-made disasters such as forest fires. this system was used, for example, in the typhoon in the philippines. this programme can help to organise urban and agricultural planning better and more efficiently, saving around eur 30 billion and creating 50 000 jobs in the period 2015-2030. a new operational phase of satellite positioning and earth observation will be launched through the copernicus programme. it is questionable why the budget for such a major project as copernicus has been reduced by[nbsp ]35[nbsp ]% and why the european commission is resisting the expansion of the european economy and major projects that could lift europe out of[nbsp ]the economic difficulties it is in.
the eastern partnership is the eu's ambition for the future at this point. i have no problem supporting this ambition in the spirit of the report submitted. but i'm skeptical about its practical implementation. the eu's strength is its economy, which is still recovering from the financial crisis. i do not see the will for grand gestures or bold policy here today, because the citizens of the member states are more focused than ever on solving internal problems. but there is one thing we must be consistent about. the eu's stance on the violation of fundamental human rights in the eastern partnership countries must never be overlooked or underestimated.
in writing. - the eastern partnership is a young project which has recently been seriously threatened by third parties and it is important that the european union shows openness and commitment to this european project. a european perspective could help to kick-start the necessary reforms of all stakeholders and strengthen their commitment to respecting common principles such as democracy and the rule of law. the citizens of the eastern partnership countries need a clear signal from the european union in the form of support in the area of financial assistance, as well as visa facilitation. the european union must make more flexible and efficient use of macroeconomic instruments and simplify trade regimes. recent events in some eastern partnership countries have highlighted the fragility of the political, economic and social integration process and the need to improve cooperation.
in writing. - what ? recent suspicions of surveillance of european citizens and institutions by the united states of america have raised major concerns about security and privacy rights. intelligence services can learn more about citizens than ever before through cutting-edge technology. the mass surveillance process has not been the subject of wide public debate or democratic decision-making until now. however, the situation that has arisen since the revelations by edward snowden is alarming and calls for strong action to protect society and the right to privacy. it is questionable whether most citizens would want to trade privacy for a greater sense of security. it is the duty of the legislature to adapt existing legislation and legal instruments to minimise the damage caused by violations of fundamental human rights. however, member states do not respond in the same way to the problem, demonstrating the uneven level of controls and balances of eu powers in these matters and the need for greater cooperation between the parliamentary bodies responsible for supervision in the individual member states.
in writing. - what ? the revelations by former national security agency analyst edward snowden indicate a shift in the attitude of the us security forces towards the right to privacy of citizens, regardless of their nationality. this attitude shows that the world has changed since september 11, 2001. the high demands placed on security, combined with developments in communication technologies, allow agencies like the nsa to know more about citizens than ever before. these factors have resulted in mass surveillance practices that are the exact opposite of the targeted surveillance originally used. targeted surveillance has been used in the fight against terrorism as appropriate and within the framework of appropriate measures. on the one hand, it is entirely logical and beneficial to seek the most effective use of ict for protection and, above all, prevention against crime. however, i am of the opinion that the provision of information requires an informed public debate because of the scale of the problems that such information causes in the context of interference with the fundamental rights of citizens. the problem, however, is that this debate is not conducted uniformly across the eu. many member states do not allow for public debate and media attention varies between countries, with germany perhaps the most prominent. however, i believe that a fundamental right such as the right to privacy should not be neglected.
in writing. - the european commission is working to raise awareness of citizens' rights in the member states through europe direct centres and various portals. however, member states and[nbsp]their politicians must also make a greater contribution to raising citizens' awareness. with the european parliament elections in 2014 taking place in many countries with low interest and participation of eligible voters, there is a need for citizens to become increasingly involved in the democratic processes of the european union. the responsible authorities must promote active citizenship in all its manifestations and forms. it is necessary to develop a european civil dialogue that promotes participatory citizenship. it is clear that the importance of the european institutions and their decisions will have an increasing impact on the daily lives of the citizens of the member states in the future, and it is therefore important that member states give more space in their school curricula to political education in the field of european union affairs.
in writing. - the european public prosecutor's office should be responsible for prosecuting a range of offences committed against the financial interests of the european union. it is essential that the european public prosecutor's office, if it is established, has an efficient and simple structure that guarantees independence and experience, and that professionalism is combined with speed of decision-making and accuracy of investigation with knowledge of the specific conditions in the individual member states where the offences in question were committed. the establishment of the european public prosecutor's office represents a step forward in the process of cooperation between individual member states in the field of criminal justice and, in cooperation with eurojust, olaf and europol, can complement an effectively functioning system of cooperation in criminal matters.

in writing. - what ? accession negotiations with turkey were officially launched in 2005, when turkey committed itself to democratic and modernisation reforms, after which the country's possible accession to the eu could be considered. the fair but strict conditions of the copenhagen criteria and alignment with the european union are therefore a prerequisite for the further process. however, the commission's 2013 progress report on turkey reiterates that turkey has not implemented the provisions of the association agreement and the additional protocol to the agreement for eight years. i stress that any country aspiring to join must unconditionally ensure the functioning of the principles of democracy and the rule of law based on respect for fundamental human rights. this implies, in particular, the reform of the judiciary, the elimination of corruption and[nbsp]discrimination, the increase of transparency in the exercise of public authority and respect for plurality.
the 25th session of the un human rights council is symbolic. this year also marks the 25th anniversary of the fall of communism and the iron curtain. many countries that suppressed human rights for decades are now members of the eu, and many of those who fought for human rights are now eu leaders or sitting in this parliament. the eu has experienced a mini-revolution in human rights a few years ago and insists on consistency and coordination among the eu's most important actors. i would like to address two specific priorities during the 25th council in geneva. the first is the role of ngos in human rights issues. it should be emphasised and the eu should actively support their engagement. ngos can play a decisive and positive role in non-democratic regimes. the second is the rights of the child. we're raising awareness of them, and it's even our top priority. since it's a broad term, i want to draw attention to the issue of child soldiers in this area. their growing numbers in armed conflicts are alarming and call for our increased activity. syria, mali and the central african republic are among the new conflicts where child soldiers are used on a daily basis. brute interference with their rights, manipulation and indoctrination by extremists, use of schools as military facilities. that's just a poor estimate of what all the kids in these zones are going through. finally, impunity and the violation of children's rights is another area that requires special eu attention, and where else to draw attention to it but in the united nations.
for the efd group. although the asian business centres operating in thailand, china and india differ in many ways in terms of their organisation, work structure and the range of activities they manage, two aspects of their functioning are common. their work is co-financed by the european union and[nbsp]an independent evaluation of the effectiveness of their work has found a weaker performance. in view of the continuing economic stagnation, the union cannot afford to maintain, at the expense of the taxpayers, inefficient structures which are unable to provide the necessary service for european business entities entering the asian market. i therefore believe that the european commission should examine the efficiency of the use of european funds used to cover the activities of individual business centres and, drawing on the experience of the best-performing analogue workshops, reform the existing practices in the organisation of the work of these business centres.
madam president, my political group and i have always been supportive of policy measures that are aimed at enhancing the competitiveness of eu companies beyond the eu market. european business centres in asia are one of the means aiming to help the eu stay competitive in an ever-changing global market. therefore, they must function and operate in the most efficient and effective manner possible. unfortunately, recent evaluations have shown that these initiatives have not been performing to the standards that we expected. these data raise several questions. we are concerned about whether demonstrated initiatives offer sufficient added value for european companies; whether the resources were allocated effectively; whether there has been enough transparency in these initiatives and whether they are already able to continue to function partly on their own revenue. i am aware that setting up new business centres is a long-term project and that they have to overcome initial challenges. business centres in india, china and thailand already achieve measurable success, and i believe they have a potential to reinforce common eu interests. however, we must hold the commission accountable to ensure that the business centres are efficient in aiding european businesses. we must also ensure that there are appropriate tools and information at the disposal of our businesses so that they can not only enter the asian market but thrive in it.
