in writing. - what ? the european union's priority now and in the future must be to create an area where people can move, live and work freely, while having confidence that their fundamental rights and freedoms will be fully respected. the economic and financial crisis has hit the people of the european union hard and changed the lives of many regions and cities. some regions in europe are ageing significantly and need an influx of young and talented workers from other member states with the skills and competence to fill the increasing gap in the labour market. if the european union is to remain globally competitive, it must develop a comprehensive immigration policy and take a tougher stance against the threats of organised crime. an integrated approach to migration and asylum policy can bring significant added value to the european union in the future and serve as another example of solidarity and responsibility sharing. the establishment of a common framework for migration and asylum policies will increase the efficiency of spending and improve budgetary synergies. a clear strategic multiannual financial framework, based on a dialogue between the european commission and the member states, will allow for better targeting of assistance to individual member states. any action financed by the fund shall be consistent with the european union's existing instruments in the field of external assistance and with humanitarian principles, which shall be clearly and precisely defined.
in writing. - the hong kong international convention for the safe and environmentally sound recycling of ships was adopted on 15 may 2009 under the auspices of the international maritime organisation. the convention covers the construction, operation and preparation of ships in preparation for their sustainable recycling, without compromising ship safety and operational efficiency. for the convention to enter into force, it needs to be ratified by significant recycling countries. so far, 5 countries have signed it and norway has acceded to it. the parties expect the convention to enter into force by 2020. the european parliament and the council adopted a new regulation on ship recycling in november 2013. its objective is to ensure the environmentally sound recycling of ships, in particular their dismantling. it is important to allow the member states of the european union to ratify or accede to the hong kong convention in order to ensure safer and more environmentally sound recycling of ships from third countries.
in writing. - the troika is only a technical body and its democratic legitimacy should be enhanced in view of the european taxpayers who have shown considerable solidarity with the countries most affected by the economic and financial crisis. at the outbreak of the crisis, 16 of the 17 euro area countries were not complying with the maastricht criteria. it is important that member states comply with european legislation and pursue the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy in order to promote jobs and growth in the euro area. the fulfilment of the consolidation objectives shall form the basis for the allocation of funds by the european union. the national responsible authorities must fully assume their responsibility and legal obligations for confirming the legality of financial transactions and the effectiveness of supervisory and control systems. the european parliament should be more involved in the whole process and the esm, which serves as a source of financial assistance to the member states, should be directly accountable to the european parliament.
in writing. - the committee on economic and monetary affairs drew up an inquiry report on the role and activities of the troika in the four countries affected by the economic adjustment programmes, namely greece, ireland, portugal and cyprus. the conclusions reached by the inquiry are quite clear to the committee. the troika has failed in its mission and its policy has failed. the methods used by this system are not in line with the standard of democracy that the member states deserve. the social dimension of the eu was grossly neglected and the troika acted like a bankers' club. europe must show that it cares not only for the financial health of its member states but also for the lives of its citizens. the troika's actions have led to anger and resentment in the countries under investigation, and therefore all stakeholders, in particular the social partners and the ep, will need to be taken into account when planning the new revitalization of the economies. it is therefore necessary that, following the dissolution of the troika, a european monetary fund should be established, operating under the auspices of the european stability mechanism and[nbsp ]within the framework of the community principle.
in writing. - what ? the activities and tasks of the troika have had an impact on employment and social aspects in the countries concerned. a closer analysis of the social and economic situation in the countries concerned shows the devastating effects of programmes and measures, including cuts in social benefits and other areas such as education, health and social services. in the member states, public debt continues to rise, unemployment and insecurity are rising, wages are falling and working-age poverty is increasing. the conditions to be met by member states for granting loans seriously undermine the social objectives of the european union and the main objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. crisis management mechanisms need to be established that are transparent and that better integrate the european institutions, namely the european parliament. the european commission should draw up a study of the actual effects of economic and social measures on employment and present measures and incentives to improve the employment and social situation in the countries concerned. in the future, there is a need to increase spending on health, health care and education, which should be seen as investments and not as expenditure.
in writing. - what ? the economic and financial crisis had a particularly strong impact on four european economies greece, ireland, portugal and cyprus. the report criticises the troika's work on the process of creating the recovery programme as well as its results. it notes in particular the deepening of social problems and the marked increase in unemployment in these countries. such a sharp deterioration in social conditions, often leading to extreme poverty and social exclusion, affects the stability and health of the whole society. it is therefore clear that corrective measures are needed to improve the social situation, especially of the most vulnerable groups of the population. however, for these measures to be effective, it is also necessary to design an on-going control system. unfortunately, this proposal is not in the report, and i could not in good conscience support it.
in writing. - the internal security fund is one of the two funds established in the field of internal affairs under the multiannual financial framework for the years 2014 to 2020. the european commission has proposed to split the new internal security fund into two parts. it will deal with police cooperation and crisis management on the one hand and external borders and visas on the other. the external visa financial support facility is intended to provide an integrated approach to issues arising from migratory pressures and the security of the external borders and to support cooperation with third countries in preventing illegal migration to the european union and facilitating legal travel. it is important that the european union ensures the security of its external borders. member states will need to improve the training of border guards and increase investment in the area of smart borders.
in writing. - what ? the european union has made it a priority to ensure a high level of security of networks and information flows within its territory. the main objective of the draft directive is to strengthen the network and information security of critical infrastructure operators on the market. many security incidents cross national borders and can thus threaten consumer confidence and the proper functioning of the internal market. member states have very different levels of preparedness to manage fragmented procedures across the european union, which results in different levels of protection for consumers and businesses and weakens the overall level of network and information security within the european union. in the modern world, technological and it challenges in the field of threats and attacks are growing, posing a major challenge to the national economy, security and the private sector. building critical infrastructure is vital to sustain and preserve economic and social activities in the fields of energy, transport, banking, infrastructure, financial markets and health. the proposal targets public administrations and market players to implement appropriate technical and organisational measures and to effectively manage and mitigate risks to network and information security.
in writing. - what ? the substantive modernisation of directive 1999/5/ec is necessary due to the extreme growth of mobile devices and wireless applications, which brings with it an increased risk of signal interference between products. it is important to use the radio spectrum efficiently. the revised directive fully harmonises the placing of products on the market, with member states not being allowed to introduce further restrictions which at national level govern the same requirements. the directive increases and updates the essential requirements for health and safety protection, electromagnetic compatibility and prevention of harmful interference. however, it will be important in the context of the implementation of the directive not to increase the administrative burden disproportionately.

in writing. - i cannot agree to the eu's active participation in the commission on the status of women, which also deals with the sexual and[nbsp]reproductive rights of women and girls. the establishment and implementation of sexual and reproductive health and rights policies, which today also include the sensitive issue of abortion, are the responsibility of the member states. there is no consensus on abortion in europe today, and laws governing the practice of abortion vary widely from country to country. the eu therefore has no mandate to speak for all member states and to do so would violate the fundamental principle of subsidiarity enshrined in the treaties.

in writing. the eu's greatest strengths are the single market, the free movement of people, goods and services. roaming is now a thing of the past, and the abolition of roaming charges is therefore good news for consumers. the opposition of the large multinational telecommunications companies was not justified, since many operators are the same in many member states. the promotion of net neutrality is also good news for electronic communications users. all data will have to be processed as quickly as possible, and neither operators nor governments will be able to limit the speed of data transmission. electronic service users will also welcome better consumer rights to prohibit opaque contracts on font size and data range, which will allow for easier comparability of individual contracts.
i am pleased that the rapporteurs have tabled an amendment which removes the reference to the so-called copenhagen commission from the original text of the report. this idea was bad enough when it appeared in a recent report by colleague michel. such a special committee not only lacks the support of the treaties, but its action would be an interference with the powers of the member states and a breach of the principle of subsidiarity. however, i regret that even after the adoption of this amendment, the requirement to extend the fundamental rights agency's powers will remain in paragraph 16. an agency that detects violations of eu citizens' fundamental rights by posting an anonymous questionnaire on the internet really doesn't need new powers. the so-called copenhagen dilemma that such proposals seek to solve does not exist. if a member state fails to fulfil its obligations, the european commission takes action, followed by the european court of justice.

in writing. - what ? the european parliament seeks to improve the level of environmental protection and public health through innovation in the automotive industry, while at the same time increasing road safety in the european union. one such measure is the regulation on the noise emission of motor vehicles. the purpose of the regulation is to establish a set of limit values applicable to vehicles used for the carriage of passengers and goods in three stages. the first phase will be applicable from 01.01.2017. the introduction of the limit value sets should result in a reduction of the limit value for passenger cars to 68 db. manufacturers will be required to provide information on the noise level of cars at the place of their sale. it is necessary to address the growing number of electric cars whose operation on the roads endangers blind and otherwise vulnerable persons. the solution to this problem is to install sound devices in the cars concerned to alert the affected persons to the proximity of the vehicle.
in writing. - what ? the protection of the public health of the inhabitants of the european union requires stricter regulation of the permissible noise levels in the context of the increasing number of motor vehicles. as a doctor, i know that prolonged exposure to loud noise can have a very negative effect on a person's mental and physical health. scientists have established a direct link between noise and hypertension, sleep disturbances, and other disorders. very high noise levels can even lead to progressive hearing loss and shift the threshold of hearing, which is already common among children and young people. it is therefore necessary to ensure that healthy human hearing is protected against excessive, intrusive or annoying noise. for the reasons set out above, i consider the proposal for a regulation as a clear step in the right direction and i vote in favour of its adoption.
in writing. - parliament and the council have agreed on a set of limit values which apply to both passenger and freight vehicles. for example, the limit values for passenger cars should be gradually reduced to 68 db. but it's not just about noise reduction, i really welcome the introduction of mandatory audible warning for different types of electric cars. it is well known that such vehicles pose a great risk to both the blind and pedestrians whose attention is impaired by other factors. noise caused by motor vehicles or other types of vehicles is one of the most widespread environmental problems in the eu. noise can cause anxiety, sleep disturbances, mental problems, as well as cardiovascular disease. almost 70 million people in the eu are exposed to excessive levels of noise caused by road traffic. more than 44%[nbsp ] of the population of larger cities is exposed to excessive noise levels during sleep. it is therefore necessary to set the rules in such a way as to protect the health of the population to the fullest extent possible.
in writing. - what ? the increase in motor vehicle traffic on our roads is enormous and it is therefore very important that noise limit values are gradually reduced. for example, for passenger cars, the limit value would be reduced to 68 decibels. the values would gradually decrease not only for passenger vehicles but also for goods vehicles. i also consider the issue of the surface and quality of the road surfaces and the production of tyres, which can also affect the noise of motor vehicles, to be important. the regulation adopted is welcomed by citizens of all member states, as the road network is located close to our towns and cities and noise from motor vehicles has a negative impact on people's daily lives. that is why i supported the report. i also agree to provide protection for blind and other vulnerable persons by fitting appropriate vehicle signalling systems to electric vehicles.
in writing. - the european union is very sensitive to the current turbulent period in international trade, characterised by a high number of retaliatory measures. it is important to strengthen the enforcement of the european union's trade rights and to reassure partners that the conditions set and agreed in trade agreements will be respected. the new horizontal legislative framework is by no means intended to be protective, but is intended to serve as a last resort against entities that break the rules. in the past, there have been only a few cases where the european union has been forced to retaliate. the mere existence of the regulation in question should have a sufficiently deterrent effect and prevent possible breaches of trade agreements.
in writing. - what ? the main objective of the present proposal is to bring council regulation no 3491/90 of 26 november 1990 into line with the system in force after the adoption of the treaty of lisbon. since the adoption of the regulation, the transitional restrictions on sugar and rice have been increased each year until they were fully lifted in 2009. the 1990 regulation has therefore lost its effect on the import regime between bangladesh and the european union. the european commission proposal also mentions the export tax that the exporting country should levy. the decision to levy an export tax should be left to[nbsp]local political legislators, not the european institutions. the european commission should also bear in mind that the regulation in question must be compatible with the objectives of economic development, poverty eradication and, last but not least, decent working conditions.
in writing. - what ? the protection of public health is one of the european union's main priorities, which seeks to promote and protect the health of citizens living in the territory of the member states through various measures and innovations. such measures include a review of the current regulatory framework for medical devices. the european commission proposes in a new proposal to divide medical devices into four categories according to the degree of risk to patients. it also raises doubts and many serious shortcomings in the transparency, rapid approval and marketing of medical devices despite the lack of testing in patients, which ultimately put patients at great risk. there are currently too many devices that are labelled as single-use devices, even though they could be refurbished. in times of economic and financial crisis, it is very important that medical devices are used as efficiently as possible. it is also important to reflect on the functioning of the eudamed database, which has been mandatory since may 2011, as the way it works has been criticised many times in the past. the european commission must propose improvements, in particular in the area of transparency of information.
in writing. - what ? the purpose of medical devices is to seek to eliminate disease, to alleviate pain and suffering of patients. however, a number of different products are appearing on the market for medical devices, and doctors need to be able to make appropriate choices, even though not all of them meet the same criteria. the current lack of clarity for doctors and patients is being addressed by a draft regulation on medical devices that strengthens certification procedures. i have personally supported all the regulations that enhance patient safety and increase the traceability of medical devices from manufacturer to patient. this is particularly important for high-risk devices that are implanted directly into the human body, such as hip joints.
in writing. - what ? the health and[nbsp ]safety of diagnostic equipment or devices are among the eu's priorities, including in vitro diagnostic devices. these medical devices are intended for use in out-of-body diagnostics, i.e. they can be carried out in the laboratory or in the vicinity of the patient, for example at bedside. this includes a wide range of tests, such as blood sugar tests, hiv tests and dna tests. in vitro diagnostic medical devices are products which are freely available on the common market. more than one million different products are covered by medical devices. there are currently more than 25 000 companies active in the field of medical technology in europe. in 2012 alone, the eu registered 10[nbsp ] thousand patent applications. there are many issues where the european commission proposes to regulate in vitro diagnostic medical devices in the same way as other medical devices. there are no national borders for these products on the european market. the european union is therefore obliged to ensure the greatest possible security.
in writing. - what ? i support legislation that ensures that the quality and safety of medical devices are tightened up, because this is important not only for patients but also for healthcare professionals and carers and all those who come into contact with them. with hundreds of thousands of different types of medical devices on the eu market, only strict legislation will ensure their safety. since may 2011, the use of the european database on medical devices (eudamed) has been mandatory, but its use is often criticised. in the future, it should be ensured that the public and healthcare professionals have adequate access to those parts of the eudamed electronic system that provide important information on medical devices that may present risks to health and safety. i also consider it important to label medical devices, whether they are single or multiple use, and to recycle devices.
in writing. - in vitro medical devices are appropriate for use in diagnostics outside the human body. without expert diagnosis, neither prevention of disease nor proper treatment can be guaranteed. the directive in its current form does not guarantee the influx of low-quality in vitro diagnostic products onto the market. this may lead to erroneous test results and thus endanger public health and the lives of citizens. it is essential that the european union ensures the highest possible safety for its citizens, as in vitro diagnostic medical devices are products that are freely available on the free market and for which there are no national borders. the european commission proposes substantial improvements in the strengthening of the member states' oversight of notified bodies, as it is very important to strengthen market surveillance. the measures the european commission plans to introduce include, for example, unannounced inspections in businesses or the creation of a network of european reference laboratories that would play an important role in the control of risk medical devices. the european union should not seek to restrict patients and their access to dna testing, but should provide and ensure professional and expert advice to inform about the consequences before the test is carried out.
in writing. - what ? the use of in vitro medical devices has many specificities, clinical and health implications, as in vitro devices are used for example in pregnancy detection, dna testing or hiv detection. the reinforcement of safety guarantees also concerns guarantees against the failure of such devices, which may have far-reaching negative consequences in specific cases. for example, in the case of unreliable hiv detection, there is a risk of contamination of other patients if blood transfusions or other contacts should occur. in the field of genetic testing, i support strengthening ethical principles in the use of such devices and, in particular, the introduction of mandatory informed consent of the patient before such testing. in the interest of promoting the safety of in vitro medical devices in the european union, i voted in favour of the proposal.

in writing. - what ? in order to monitor and analyse in detail the progress and quality of life of people in the world, the european union has integrated environmental accounting into its programme since 2003. the european commission therefore adopted a regulation in 2011 introducing the first three modules, namely air emissions accounts, environmental taxes and material flow accounts. these new accounts should contribute to the development of more comprehensive indicators to assist public debate and the development of new policies. however, some member states are not yet ready for the data collection in question and therefore request a year's delay. the european commission and national authorities should make the widest possible use of environmental accounts data in the future, which will help to better define the european union's priorities, such as green growth and more efficient use of resources.
in writing. - what ? in recent years, confidence in the ability of the european institutions to deal effectively and expertly with the daily problems of civil society has been significantly reduced. this is why a legislative initiative has been launched, which is the result of awareness-raising work involving, among others, the confederation of european civil society organisations. increased activity by all actors involved should bring about a marked increase in public awareness of the european union's external action in developing countries. the european union is aware that the 100 million people in the member states who suffer material and financial deprivation cannot be forgotten in the discussion of the poor people of the rest of the world. the year 2015 will be a very good opportunity to assess the challenges of development cooperation, but also cohesion policies for development. the year 2015 will offer the european institutions a clear picture of the acceptability of the european union's development policy.
in writing. - what ? the flegt scheme was introduced in 2005 and is primarily aimed at systematically addressing the problem of illegal logging and related trade. only timber products that have been legally produced and in accordance with the national legislation of the partner countries may be exported to the european union. the aim of this proposal is not to amend or replace the current flegt licensing system but to adapt its provisions to the new regime of delegated and implementing acts which are part of the lisbon treaty. the new proposal confers on the european commission the power to adopt delegated acts in respect of the examination of products of the wood species listed in the three annexes to council regulation (ec) no 338/97 on the protection of wild plant species. the new article on amendments adopted after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon will require two amendments reflecting the results of the trilogue on the omnibus i and ii consolidated acts.
in writing. - the stockholm programme was presented in 2010 and sets out the european union's priorities for the area of justice, freedom and security for the period 2010[nbsp ][nbsp ]2014. legitimacy of legislation has been increased through the increased use of the ordinary legislative procedure, which gives the european parliament, as the only democratically elected institution, a greater degree of influence. in the future, cooperation between national parliaments and the european parliament is expected to improve, as the stronger involvement of the european peoples in the democratic process contributes significantly to policy-making and legislation at european level. the european citizens' initiative gives the citizens of the european union the same powers in proposing policies as the ep and the council, which they appropriately use, and in the future it can help to identify issues that should be addressed at european union level. the european union should continue to seek to protect and intensify its efforts in this area, including in the light of the ongoing investigation into the mass electronic surveillance of european union citizens. cooperation between the ep, the council and the ec is needed to maintain an area of freedom, security and justice.
in writing. - i welcome the proposal for a regulation on a single european electronic communications market and believe that the new principles on which this market will be based will enhance competition, dynamism, efficiency and innovation. in recent years, it has been difficult to talk about a genuine single telecoms market, given the unjustifiably high charges for telephone services, messaging and mobile internet use in a member state other than one's own. this was particularly the case for ordinary consumers in the exercise of their freedom of movement within the internal market. i therefore support the abolition of roaming charges within the european union, which should enter into force on 15 december 2015. as regards the blocking of internet access by providers in order to prevent or discourage the use of competing applications, i voted in favour of rules prohibiting such unfair anti-competitive behaviour.
in writing. - what ? the creation of a single european electronic communications market is a response to the current rapidly evolving market for[nbsp]telecommunications technologies. the single market in electronic communications will bring certainty to consumers as well as to firms active in the sector and will trigger investment, growth and job creation. moreover, the new regulation will facilitate innovative uses of wireless broadband, ensure broadcast protection and[nbsp ]stop interference in the event of multiple devices being used in close proximity. the savings from the scope of the single telecoms market will also make it possible to strengthen the electronic communications sector, making europe a strong global competitive player. zero roaming for calls within the eu is a concrete example that will have a positive impact on the costs of european mobile network users. i see another positive step in the introduction of the so-called fair use clause, which aims to prevent abuse of the use of retail roaming services. despite the successful outcome of the vote, it is a pity that all retail roaming charges for calls from mobile phones, sms and data within the eu will not be abolished until december 2015. today we gave the green light to roaming in the european parliament at first reading and now it is the member states who are next. i therefore support a maximum eurotariff for price ceilings, which should continue to serve as a protective limit for charges for consumption exceeding the real limits until the expiry of the regulation.
in writing. - what ? the european union is currently facing many security challenges and a growing struggle for energy and resource security. it is essential that the european union continues to work with strategic partners and to protect the security and prosperity of its citizens. the european union will become increasingly important in the future and it is therefore important that it provides certainty and added value in dealing with complex situations and creates synergies between different sectors and institutions. various institutional and procedural problems hamper the coherence of the european union's external action in crisis areas, undermining its credibility as a global actor and provider of security. in the area of financial cohesion, the european parliament will exercise full democratic control, thereby guaranteeing the efficient and economical use of the limited resources of the european union to achieve results. in line with the objectives of the lisbon treaty, the european union must continue to strengthen its foreign policy and become more involved in the pursuit of world peace.
in writing. - what ? the protection of the safety and health of the public is a priority for the european union. it is therefore the task of legislators to update and strengthen food safety provisions as often as necessary. the main objective of this regulation is to improve the clarity and applicability of the legislation on the identification and traceability of bovine animals, thereby improving the situation and making beef safer for the final consumer. in recent years, several food scandals have emerged, raising consumer dissatisfaction with the quality and safety of food. electronic identification of livestock will allow more accurate and reliable information to be obtained and will improve food safety.
we are talking about putting in place an infrastructure for alternative fuels. this directive is intended to move europe towards a position of less dependence on oil. we see our incredible dependence on ukraine, on russia, and that's why this is a step in the right direction. in this directive we want to achieve an ever-increasing use of electricity, to use hydrogen, but also liquefied natural gas. for example, it is good that all key maritime ports are equipped with such lng terminals by 2025 and then all ports by 2030. i think it's also important from a strategic point of view that these alternative fuels develop. and the second thing is actually reducing co2 and improving the environment, and a science and research base that would be dedicated to that could be an opportunity for jobs in europe again.
what we are discussing is actually the permissible dimensions of certain vehicles in national and international road transport and the maximum permissible weight in international road transport. i want to pause on the fact that on the one hand, this would be a step towards transportation modernization, but on the other hand, we know that these mega trucks, these huge trucks, which we could also call monster trucks, have a huge weight. and as our colleague from the czech republic said, we are not prepared, for example, with the bridges in the slovak republic or in the czech republic, to let these mega trucks drive on our roads. if some countries want to go down this road and make more aerodynamic, more comfortable, but above all heavier and longer trucks, please, that is their national jurisdiction, but i would not go here harmonizing and that is valid for all european countries.
the noise-related operating limitation for airports, with noise levels within a balanced approach, is very good news that we are actually discussing and accepting. i am glad that the european union is respecting all the rules that apply in the international civil aviation organisation, and in fact we are trying to help citizens who are bothered by the increased noise levels, for example in relation to night flights, which should be more restricted. it is good that the european union, together with the council, has not come to the conclusion that we must harmonize everything and that there must be one single regulation, and that we are leaving a great deal of freedom to the member states within the framework of subsidiarity, while respecting the health risks and reducing the noise level for citizens.

for the efd group the involvement of interest groups and lobbyists in the european union's policy-making is steadily increasing. according to current studies, some 1700 lobbyists are currently trying to influence the decision-making of the european institutions. the most important group are the financial sector lobbyists, who spend more than €120 million a year on their activities in brussels. the number of lobbyists from the financial sector exceeds the number of lobbyists from the field of civil society or trade union organisations by a ratio of 5:1. the disparity between these groups is even more pronounced in terms of the financial resources at their disposal. the financial lobby provides its representatives with 30 times more resources to promote its interests than the possibilities of civil society organisations or trade unions. from this quantification it is clear that the material possibilities of interest groups or lobby groups can seriously distort our democratic environment. we therefore need to monitor the activities of lobbyists in the european institutions as closely as possible and to widen the range of information and persons to be recorded in the transparency register. only precise rules defining the scope for proper communication between the representatives of the european union and interest groups can contribute to eliminating the risk of distortion of the democratic environment in the union.
mr president malina hedvig, the hungarian student in slovakia, has been harassed and investigated for seven years. the last time the prosecutor sought an expert opinion on the girl's mental state was seven years ago, from an orthopedic specialist. in 2006, the girl was beaten on the way to the exam, on her blouse was written: "hungarians across the danube, slovakia without parasites!" the perpetrators were never punished, the investigation was closed after two weeks, but the prime minister considered it important to clarify that he would not let a student ruin slovakia's good reputation. this is obviously a message to the hungarians in slovakia. the sequel is shocking. the girl was charged with perjury by the intelligence community, all after the previous government apologized to malina hedvig for harassing her. the prosecution charged him with perjury and perjury on april 2nd of this year. the penalty is up to five years in prison. i ask the commission to uphold the rule of law
