for the efd group in my country, the banking sector is currently relatively healthy and stable. however, it may run into serious problems in the course of a few years as a result of the banking union measures. after the dubious privatization of state-owned financial institutions, the banking sector in our country is now dominated by subsidiaries of foreign banks. while subsidiaries are separate entities not threatened by the insolvency of the parent, after their transformation into branches under the proposed amendments, they become a vehicle for draining liquidity from the country of operation to the state in which their head office is located. national banking supervision cannot prevent the transformation of a subsidiary into a branch and cannot then block liquidity transfers from the branch to the foreign parent. commissioner, i do not want to suggest that the creation of a banking union is a hidden mechanism for the theft of resources from healthy stable subsidiaries by unhealthy mothers, or a project to drain liquidity from prosperous countries of the union to other states. however, i would point out that the proposed legislation creates a wide scope for financial manipulation with serious societal implications.
in 2006, the european socialists put forward proposals to reduce the likelihood and severity of future systemic crises. the right wing of the european parliament, together with the eurosceptics, rejected us with the words: "the market will solve everything". he didn't. that's why banks that fail have to be bailed out by people who didn't cause the problems. nice law of the market, when the profits are shared by the bank owners and the losses are shared by everyone. when the richest people keep their money in tax havens and everyone else pays for their greed. the aim of resolution must be to protect depositors, to ensure that essential financial services can continue and to ensure the stability of the financial system. the moral hazard should be reduced by ensuring that failing institutions have only a minimal reliance on public financial support. the shareholders and creditors of failing banks and investment companies must bear these losses. it will also give them an incentive to monitor their banks more closely.
in writing. we are all consumers of the internal market and thus weaker players in the market that need to be protected. balanced regulation protects the health and safety of consumers from commercial excesses and provides a framework for the legitimate operation of businesses. the proposal for a regulation will help to ensure that the rules are applied uniformly in all member states. in the committee on legal affairs, we have produced an opinion that contributes to ensuring a high level of consumer protection in the eu. we considered it particularly important to protect the interests of consumer groups at risk when using products, such as children, the elderly and persons with disabilities. it is the unavoidable duty of manufacturers and importers to guarantee the safety of their products. in this context, they should ensure that products bear an indication of the country of origin and the ingredients. in the past, the identification of products from other member states has required more care, including language barriers. linguistic diversity may have arrived in this area most rapidly, crossing borders. the need for improvement does not stop there: each product must be marked with easily visible and easily understandable identification features.
in writing - consumer protection is effective only if consumer rights can also be enforced. raising consumer awareness of their rights therefore plays a key role in achieving a high level of consumer protection. in the case of supplier services, more attention needs to be paid to the protection of their rights, as these are services that are essential for people's lives and their social and cultural integration into european society. these are mainly the four major sectors that have been liberalised in recent years and largely integrated into the internal market: energy, telecommunications, transport and postal services. in the case of these services, consumers do not decide whether to use them, but only from which provider they order them. this often creates an unequal relationship between the provider on the one hand and the consumer on the other.
in writing - the introduction of a deposit protection scheme, as well as a further package of measures to protect consumers from bank misconduct within the eu, is a cornerstone in building an effective banking union. throughout the 13 years of the euro area's existence, it has been demonstrated that only a common approach will sustain the euro area during a possible banking crisis. i also support the new deposit protection scheme because it sets out rules that, in the event of the failure of any bank in an eu member state, will ensure that deposits up to €100,000 are protected by the banks themselves, without the burden falling on taxpayers and people saving for a better future. this guarantee ensures that consumers' deposits remain safe and that they pay for their own mistakes in the management of the bank. the new instrument we have approved today introduces preventive measures that will enable the euro area to recognise and address an impending problem in the banking sector before it can develop into a difficult and consumer-constraining situation. i am glad that the previously unregulated banking sector and the irresponsible behavior of some banks can now be called a thing of the past. however, in my view, it is equally necessary to ensure that the frustration of the less-performing banks is not reflected in the level of fees charged to their clients.
in writing. - to accelerate europe's dependency on oil and decarbonisation, common technical specifications for alternative fuels infrastructure should be defined. this will not only stimulate economic growth, but also strengthen the competitiveness of european industry and reduce greenhouse gas emissions from transport. our long-term objectives must include decarbonising the transport sector, where more than 90% of the energy used comes from oil, of which more than 84% is imported oil. the development of the alternative fuels market is currently facing technological and commercial deficiencies. in many cases, the lack of common basic technical specifications hinders interoperability and the lack of appropriate infrastructure. different technological solutions applied in different parts of europe have led to fragmentation of the internal market. a sound policy framework with binding targets is therefore essential for the successful deployment of infrastructure capable of meeting the energy needs of all modes of transport. to facilitate the market integration of new technologies, adequate research and development funds should be made available and the european market should continue to play a strong leading role in such initiatives. that is why i voted in favour of this proposal.
written - the directive setting maximum weights and dimensions for vehicles carrying oversized cargo should specifically target vehicles with a length of 25 metres and a weight of 60 tonnes. although these vehicles are currently only allowed in scandinavia, the netherlands and certain parts of germany, in the light of a study carried out under the auspices of the european commission, we must be aware of the potential risk of their use on european roads. these trucks pose a threat not only to road users but also to the environment, given the amount of co2 emissions they emit when transporting oversized cargo. i very much agree with the rapporteur's view that freight transport is a railway activity. however, if this option cannot be provided due to certain transport obstacles, carriers should consider combined transport. of course, i fully understand the concerns of the hauliers about making their work more difficult, but human life, as well as our safety on the roads, must remain our priority. our efforts should be directed towards the preparation of legislation aimed at reducing emissions, for example by improving the aerodynamic shape of vehicle cabs. preventing accidents, improving road safety and minimising road risks are and will always be my top priorities when adopting legislation concerning road users, to which we all belong.
in writing - i supported the draft directive on payment accounts mainly because it strengthens the position of bank customers. the most important change i see is greater transparency of fees and conditions for payment accounts, which will allow customers to compare more easily. the transition to competition offering better conditions should also be facilitated. lastly, the directive also introduces the right to open a basic payment account. this means that no bank will be able to refuse to open a basic account to someone who is legally resident in the eu. this is particularly important for increasing the mobility of eu citizens.
in writing - what ? the number of cases brought before the general court has been steadily increasing as the court's powers have been gradually extended. this prolongs the proceedings and increases the number of pending cases. such a situation is contrary to the requirements of the charter of fundamental rights of the eu and in particular to article 6 of the european convention for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms, which governs the right to a fair trial. the high burden on the general court resulting from the excessive volume and complexity of the actions brought needs to be addressed urgently, as the nature of many disputes requires a rapid decision and delays in reaching a decision constitute a form of denial of justice. i agree that the necessary solution is to increase the number of judges in the general court. as regards the appointment of new judges, i am of the opinion that geographical balance must be maintained in order to preserve the multicultural diversity on which the eu is founded. in conclusion, i call on all the institutions concerned to act promptly, as the strengthening of judicial posts is urgent.
in writing - as a doctor and a human being, i welcome the proposal to introduce an eu-wide interoperable ecall system which is automatically activated after a serious accident has been recorded and automatically calls the emergency number 112, thus bringing the emergency services to the exact scene of the accident. in traffic accidents, the minute is life and death. it is therefore vital that the emergency services have accurate navigation to the accident site. the introduction of ecall can be expected to improve road safety and, above all, to reduce the number of accident victims. for the reasons set out above, i am voting in favour of the report and i hope that ecall will soon be operational in all member states.
in writing - the demographic ageing of the population, which is a pan-european phenomenon, cannot be reversed overnight. i therefore supported the proposal for the active and assisted living programme, which aims to promote the development of new information and communication technologies to ensure a decent ageing for a growing number of older people. but i also believe that such solutions are simply putting out a fire that is spreading with a glass of water. real sustainable solutions are based on promoting the natural family across all policies. only then can the aging process be reversed.
written - the research and development programme for small and medium-sized enterprises carrying out research is a welcome response to the increasing number of applications from enterprises which could not be met favourably in the previous period due to budget constraints. i support the provisions aimed at aligning horizon 2020 research and innovation with the programme in question in order to avoid overlapping requirements and unnecessary administrative burden for smes, which should focus their activities more on creative market functioning. i believe that these measures will lead to an increase in innovative solutions and the competitiveness of smes, which are the backbone of the european economy and major providers of employment in all regions.
in writing - smes are the backbone of the european economy. they account for up to 98%[nbsp ] of all businesses in the eu and employ up to two thirds of the workforce. the specific sub-group consists of innovative small and medium-sized enterprises focusing on research and development. this focus carries with it an increased risk of return on investment and therefore this type of enterprise often has difficulty in raising the necessary capital. the eurostars joint programme was created in 2008 to address this problem. the draft decision proposed allows this programme to continue under the new multiannual financial framework and i therefore supported it.
in writing, the european and developing countries clinical trials partnership programme is intended to contribute to the reduction of poverty-related diseases and neglected infectious diseases, which may also spread further to european countries. the commission and the member states make a significant financial contribution to research into diseases and, through their activities, accelerate the clinical development of effective, safe and accessible medical interventions and medicines adapted to the specificities of developing countries. this meritorious activity must meet all the requirements for the protection of human rights. i therefore consider it necessary to strengthen the role of ethics committees and to establish sufficient safeguards to ensure respect for human rights in clinical trials. i believe it is absolutely essential that informed consent be obtained from patients before any intervention in a clinical trial and that it be genuinely informed and voluntary. projects funded by horizon 2020 shall not be in breach of the eu charter of fundamental rights and other international instruments by which the union is bound.
in writing, article 11 (2) teu clearly states that the eu institutions shall maintain an open, transparent and regular dialogue with representative associations and civil society. such direct dialogue is essential for the proper functioning of democracy and therefore requires the establishment of a reliable mechanism to govern its conduct. i believe that all eu institutions must guarantee transparency at all stages of the law-making process at union level. given the large number of businesses, organisations and individuals who seek to influence the eu's policy-making and implementation processes on a daily basis, i believe that there is a need to introduce stricter and more ambitious transparency criteria. the current voluntary transparency register does not appear to be very effective. i therefore support the introduction of a mandatory register which would set equal rules for all. in this respect, i call on the commission to define an appropriate legal basis.
in writing - i supported the changes to the existing interinstitutional agreement on the transparency register, as i regarded them as a further step towards transparency in the adoption of european legislation. it is important for citizens to know who is trying to influence eu legislation and on whose behalf. these are legitimate activities, but they must be conducted according to certain rules in order to exclude corruption and clientelism. these rules must apply equally to the european parliament and to the commission.
in writing. - the multiannual financial framework sets annual ceilings for all eu expenditure and for eu expenditure by policy area. the main objective is to achieve a high degree of predictability and regularity of expenditure, while respecting the ceilings of the union's own resources. on 2 december 2013, the regulation on the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020 and the interinstitutional agreement were formally adopted by the council. parliament defined five main points: the sufficient level of resources, flexibility, post-election adjustment, own resources and budgetary unity. the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020 sets the overall ceiling for commitment appropriations at eur 960 billion and the overall ceiling for payment appropriations at eur 908 billion. these figures represent 1,00% and 0,95% of the union's gni respectively, according to the 2012 spring forecast of the union's gni. in the last round of negotiations, a further specific flexibility was introduced to tackle youth unemployment and strengthen research, allowing for the advance of expenditure in 2014/15 in areas such as youth employment, research, erasmus and smes. this is a very important element and outcome of the negotiations which can improve the current situation by reducing youth unemployment and stimulating entrepreneurship and development in europe. that's why i supported the report.
in writing, the agreement on the multiannual financial framework (mff) for the years 2014 to 2020 was the result of long and complicated negotiations that lasted almost two and a half years. but the result is not satisfactory. the total amount of the new multiannual budget represents a reduction of commitments of 3,5% and payments of 3,7% compared to the 2007-2013 financial framework, despite the enlargement of the union to 28 member states and the strengthening of the eu's powers under the lisbon treaty. given that the budget aims to strengthen the economy and its competitiveness, to invest in the development of the regions and to strengthen european solidarity, i consider the budget cuts to be counterproductive. the budget must reflect democratically determined political objectives and respect the commitments the eu has made to its citizens, businesses and other partners. i therefore welcome this report, which assesses the progress of the mff negotiations, and i call on all the institutions to draw conclusions from this report for future negotiations, in which there is a need to respect more the position of parliament as the only directly elected institution.
dear officer, dear colleagues! the importance of supplementary pension schemes is beyond doubt given the increasing importance of self-employment. the basic pension schemes are still largely linked to the 'pay as you go' system, the sustainability of which is in doubt due to demographic developments. complementary employment schemes vary widely from one member state to another and in some member states they are virtually non-existent. the development and harmonisation of european standards for pension schemes is a particularly complex and demanding task, also because of the very different legal environment. the portability of entitlements across member states' borders, some important definitions such as the standardisation of the age limit, the uniform treatment of access to latent retirement rights are therefore very important steps forward. at european level, several important steps have been taken recently to preserve and strengthen the single labour market. this legal provision is one of those which help to remove the obstacles to the free movement of labour. the complexity of the issue is further demonstrated by the fact that the draft law sets a deadline of four years for transposition into national legal systems. finally, i would like to thank the rapporteur, ria oomen-ruijten, for her many years of thorough and demanding work on the interoperability of social systems, including pension systems.
in writing. on 28 june 2014, europe and the world will commemorate the 100th anniversary of the assassination of archduke franz ferdinand of habsburg, heir to the throne of the austro-hungarian monarchy. this act became the pretext for the outbreak of what came to be called the "world" war. there is not a single town or village in europe that does not have a monument to the local people who gave their lives in that war. the treaty of versailles, however, did not bring peace and prosperity to europe. on the contrary, it created the conditions for the rise of national socialism, which unleashed another, even more terrible war. the treaty of versailles reminds us that to end conflict between nations, it is not enough to conclude a ceasefire and establish a truce. reconciliation is needed. today's european union is the fruit of a genuine reconciliation of the peoples of europe. it is the fruit of forgiveness, not of forgetfulness. her future - and ours - depends on our ability to remember and forgive each other.
there is a consensus in the room: russia is pursuing an imperial and aggressive policy. it must be stopped by direct and strict sanctions, by a rapid and radical change in energy policy, but, colleagues, the european union, let us realise this, has also become a gravitational force attracting the countries of the balkans, eastern europe, the european caucasus. russian imperialism shows that the eastern partnership must be secured and guaranteed by the strength of the european union. the european union is also obliged to ensure the security of the eastern partnership members. this requires the rapid transformation of the european union from a soft power into a real force capable of protecting the security and interests of even non-nato states that are interested in embarking on the european path and moving closer to the european union, and eventually becoming members of the european union. this is something we all need to realize.
in writing. - eu citizens working in another member state will be able to exercise supplementary pension rights abroad. a supplementary occupational pension is made up of contributions from the employer and the employee and can represent a significant contribution to the state pension and personal savings. these rights have so far been waived if they have worked in another member state for more than three years. the new directive, which i voted for, will thus remove another obstacle to the free movement of workers between member states. the entry into force of the directive will also reduce the minimum period of participation in the supplementary pension scheme from five years to three years, after which the worker will acquire supplementary occupational pension rights.
in writing - food and feed law has a significant and direct impact on the daily lives and health of eu citizens and as such deserves particular attention from legislators setting the rules for practice. with rapidly changing conditions on the agricultural products market, the need has arisen to face new challenges arising from complex production and trade relationships in a globalised world. in particular, i welcome the fact that legislation on product quality and safety controls has been revised and modernised. we cannot allow the food fraud scandals we have seen with horse meat or contaminated organic vegetables to be repeated. in this respect, i support the introduction of sufficiently high fines for undertakings, which should not only be punitive in a particular case of infringement, but also deterrent and preventive towards all producers.
writing - i consider the food industry to be one of the most important and dynamic sectors in the eu, employing over 48 million people. although the food and feed safety sector has achieved many positive results in recent years, i believe that the new conditions and risks presented by the global market force us to create a sufficiently flexible and safe control system within the food chain. i supported the report, although i did not agree with some of the amendments, because i believe that the proposals put forward can not only simplify the current legal framework, but can also guarantee the uniform application of the rules relating to the agri-food chain throughout the eu and the smooth functioning of the internal market. i also welcome proposals and measures to improve the functioning of administrative assistance and the mutual exchange of information on risk products on the market between member states, which will enable cross-border breaches of the rules relating to the food chain to be traced and measures subsequently taken to achieve the highest possible level of food and feed safety and thus of health and consumer protection.
written - the report on the draft animal health regulation brings long-awaited provisions on the prevention and control of animal diseases that can be transmitted between animals and to humans. the need for a directly effective eu regulation has become urgent in view of the many worrying developments related to the rapid spread of diseases from one member state to another resulting from an open internal market. in order to protect public health and food safety, a coordinated common approach is necessary. i support stricter measures to control the sources of infection and disease surveillance. of course, the best way to fight diseases remains to prevent and reduce overall the incidence of diseases that have devastating consequences not only for the animals themselves, but also for farmers, agriculture and ultimately consumers. for these reasons, i voted in favour of the regulation.
in writing - what ? in the field of consumer product safety, the current directive has not proved to be very successful, as it has been transposed and applied by member states in a very different way. in order to enhance consumer protection and to make the overall product safety legislation in the eu more transparent, i consider the proposed regulation to be necessary to ensure legal certainty and to create a level playing field in the internal market for all businesses and consumers. i fully support the requirement of mandatory indication of the country of origin. consumers have the right to truthful information on the origin of products in order to make an informed decision before purchasing them. as regards measures against dangerous products, i am in favour of the introduction of sufficiently high dissuasive penalties, which take account of the seriousness of the infringement, its duration, the size of the company or the possibility of recurrence.
in writing - we all know the "made in china" labels. the consumer knows what the goods are, knows the country of origin and knows what quality to expect from the product at first glance. today we are witnessing the approval of the 'made in' label for goods produced and sold within the european union's single market. however, this measure is only one of a series of proposals aimed at strengthening the safety and monitoring of consumer goods in the european union. i strongly believe that european consumers have the right to know what they are consuming and therefore fully support the strengthening of safety requirements for product quality. this will prevent the influx of dangerous products onto the european market, while introducing sanctions against companies that do not respect quality rules. we are therefore putting the finger on the pulse of producers and distributors of, say, antibiotic-treated poultry meat. in today's first reading, we supported, with our position on the commission's proposal on consumer product safety, provisions which would give national authorities responsible for consumer protection wider powers in the area of product safety supervision, of course within the limits of the regulation. i believe that by defining terms such as non-compliant product or by making national controls mandatory, consumers will avoid products that pose a health risk and will have a clearer idea of the contents of their shopping cart.
in writing - what ? small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) are the backbone of our economy and currently account for almost 85% of new jobs created, with 20.7 million of them creating more than 67% of private sector jobs in the eu. i therefore find the situation concerning the administrative burden on smes and in particular the fact that the personnel costs for compliance with regulatory obligations are often ten times higher for smes than for large enterprises, worrying. such conditions have a very negative impact on job creation and employment security, reduce the competitiveness of smes and discourage entrepreneurship. in the legislative process, we must therefore ensure that the regulatory burden on smes is minimised and that the legislation is adapted to their needs. in my experience in the regions, access to european funds for smes needs to be facilitated. i therefore welcome the programme for the competitiveness of enterprises and smes and the sme instrument created under horizon 2020. i also consider the launch of the erasmus programme for young entrepreneurs to be a very positive step towards developing entrepreneurial potential.
in writing. - what ? on the basis of several initiatives, the posting of workers in the context of the provision of services directive has been found to be inadequate and does not address the problems of social dumping. the principle of equal pay for equal work at the same place must be clearly established in european union law. it is for the member states to monitor its implementation and to combat any fraud and circumvention on their territory. since the posting of workers is a cross-border activity, the competent authorities of several states must be involved in its supervision. i therefore consider it essential to improve crossborder cooperation and to introduce effective measures to combat social dumping. exported workers are often exploited, lack sufficient information about their rights in the host country, and cross-border enforcement of their rights is unnecessarily time-consuming and costly. for the reasons stated above, i had hoped that the european parliament's report on the enforcement directive would clearly set out a system of common obligations for subcontractors in all sectors and would advocate the unambiguous protection of posted workers by adopting the most favourable rules scheme for workers in the host country.
in writing - what ? despite the fact that there are currently a number of tools available to combat trafficking in cultural goods, their effectiveness is low. the european union's 1993 directive failed to tackle this third most widespread form of trafficking in terms of revenue. the directive laid down the conditions for the return of cultural goods, which are also considered national treasures, unlawfully removed from the territory of a state after the abolition of the internal borders of the european union. we must be aware that the opening up of the internal market has, on the one hand, given individuals the four fundamental freedoms, but on the other hand, has made the process of returning cultural objects designated as national cultural treasures more difficult. however, in many member states the very concept of national cultural heritage is not defined. for this reason, i am aware of the complexity of the procedure itself for returning the items in question to their country of origin. i hope that the new electronic tool for eu administrative cooperation, also known as the internal market information system, will also help countries to obtain information on exported or previously stolen cultural goods. i support the revision of the current directive because i believe that only by introducing clear rules and cooperation between member states will we be able to tackle the illegal trade in cultural goods, which has become increasingly prevalent in recent years.
in writing - the available statistics provide startling information about the careless handling of paychecks. the average european uses around 200 plastic bags a year, with almost 90% of them ending up in waste after a single use. such practices are not only harmful to the environment, due to the large amounts of waste and pollutants that they release into the environment, including water, but also highly inefficient in terms of resource use. as the recyclability of plastic carrier bags is very low, it is necessary to encourage operators to avoid waste and to favour more environmentally friendly substitutes. charging for the use of bags also appears to be an effective measure against excessive consumption of bags. in my vote i supported the provisions to protect human health from the hazardous substances that are often used in the production of plastic bags. these are substances that are carcinogenic, mutagenic or harmful to reproduction and substances that are endocrine disruptors.
in writing - the current use of single-use plastics is convenient, but also very inefficient and environmentally harmful. only 6.6 percent of plastic bags are recycled, and they can last hundreds of years in the environment. that's why i advocate the use of reusable bags made from eco-friendly materials. it is not a matter of debate that almost no european leaves a shop without a plastic bag. statistics say that in five minutes, one million new plastic bags are used in the european union. but most of them, when they've served their purpose, go straight to the trash. however, given the burden that such waste places on the environment and our seas, it seems logical to me to establish basic rules for reducing its consumption at european level. despite the efforts of some fast food chains to obtain an exemption for the use of plastic bags for delivery services, i am pleased that we have managed to vote for the same rules for all retailers. we have not introduced anything with this directive today that was not already there. we all know from personal experience that even a minimal charge for plastic bags will make most of us not throw away a reusable bag when we next buy, and at the same time, we will be more environmentally friendly.
in writing - i supported the proposal to reduce the consumption of lightweight plastics, because i consider their huge single use to be a source of threat to the quality of the environment, but also to the aesthetics of the space in which we live. under the adopted directive, it is for the member states to use economic instruments such as taxes and levies, as well as trade restrictions and prohibitions, to achieve this objective. plastic bags are an environmental burden and should not be provided free of charge. the exception is the packaging of loose foodstuffs such as meat, fish or cheese. as a result of these measures, by 2019, disposable 'gig bags' and microten bags should be replaced by recycled paper bags or by bags that are biodegradable and compostable.
in writing - i voted in favour of the proposal for funding of european political parties and foundations, because it is the result of a broad political agreement which takes account of the need to preserve political pluralism and at the same time allows for a rigorous and strict control of the way the budgetary resources are used. the model adopted does not discriminate between any party and allows for the preservation and further development of diversity of opinion in european politics.
in writing - fraudulent conduct prejudicial to the financial interests of the union causes losses to the union budget and thus also constitutes a detriment to the taxpayers who contribute to the budget. i consider it necessary to ensure that, in the european area of freedom, security and justice, offenders are punished fairly, regardless of the member state in which they are located. experience has shown that the relocation of offenders has depended on the favourable legal systems of the member states. the means of civil and administrative law are not sufficient to ensure the effective protection of the eu's financial interests, in particular the fight against fraud, active and passive corruption and embezzlement, and i therefore agree to the introduction of criminal law protection. i also support the introduction of minimum levels of sanctions in the directive.
the european union is an association of sovereign nation states which have voluntarily decided to cooperate and to delegate some of their competences to the jointly constituted bodies and institutions of the union. member states shall, at their discretion and in accordance with their needs, delegate to the institutions of the union the tasks they require to be carried out, even with a proportionate financial envelope for the material provision of those tasks. as long as the european institutions respect the mandate thus defined, they have no reason to claim additional authorisations to raise own resources. i therefore fully agree with the council's position, which rejected the commission's proposals for new own resources based on value added tax and financial transaction tax. the european institutions are to carry out only those coordination and servicing activities which have been requested by the member states. they have no right to build behind the backs of the member states a new power centre in brussels with a sprawling, growing administration supported by their own sources of revenue. the citizens of the european union, as far as i know, have not yet agreed to any transformation of the union into a state entity. therefore, the extension of the european institutions' claims to new own funds is totally unjustified.
in writing - the relationship between the european parliament and national parliaments is directly governed by the provisions of the treaty on european union, in particular article 5 on the delegation of powers and subsidiarity, article 10 (1) and (2) on representative democracy and the representation of eu citizens, and in particular article 12 on the role of national parliaments. five years have passed since the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, but relations between the european parliament and national parliaments have not yet fully developed. for example, the so-called 'yellow card' has only been used twice so far. strengthening inter-parliamentary relations would be appropriate in order to strengthen democratic legitimacy and overall improve the quality of legislation adopted at eu level, which many citizens perceive as too distant. in my vote i supported the report presented, which analyses the relationship between national parliaments and the european parliament, and i hope that it will draw the right conclusions leading to a deepening of relations between them after the forthcoming european elections, with a view to deepening the idea of european integration.
in writing - freedom of movement and the ability to find a job in any eu member state is one of the main pillars of a common europe. i therefore supported the proposal to extend cooperation between european public employment services. i also agree with the committee of the regions that good and best practice often emerges at local and regional level. effective public employment services can make a significant contribution to tackling the pressing problem of high youth unemployment. this is where a higher level of cooperation with the social partners and other public, private and non-profit stakeholders is important.
in writing - the solidarity fund is a concrete expression of european solidarity towards member states affected by natural disasters. however, in the light of past experience of its operation in 23 member states, this instrument required some modernisation. i have already pointed out in the past the need for a more rapid administrative procedure in assessing the conditions for granting funds, which have so far proved to be rather lengthy for the affected areas. i trust that the affected states will also appreciate the possibility of drawing a certain advance before the final amount of the financial contribution is calculated. i am pleased that today's vote has adopted provisions which will shorten administrative time limits in favour of the affected countries. i also regard the possibility of using support for smaller regional natural disasters as a positive sign of flexibility. for the reasons set out above, i voted in favour of the report on the eu solidarity fund.
in writing - solidarity is one of the most important european values. i supported the report because we in slovakia have had experience with floods and have seen that if aid for natural disasters is to be effective, it must be fast. the reform of the eu solidarity fund will allow member states to draw up to 10%[nbsp ] of the estimated amount of aid immediately. the criteria for approving aid for smaller regional disasters will also be simplified.
in writing - i have supported the commission's proposal to grant macro-financial assistance to the republic of tunisia. the commission notes that the aid is granted to the tunisian authorities in accordance with the provisions of the agreement on the european economic area ('the agreement'). it is also intended to facilitate the adoption of structural reforms to improve the macroeconomic situation of the country. the payment of this assistance should be strictly in accordance with the budgetary rules and should be conditional on the implementation of the reform measures agreed in the memorandum of understanding between the commission and tunisia. it should be borne in mind that it is in the eu's interest that the countries belonging to the mediterranean region are economically and politically stable. only in this way can the great waves of illegal migrants to europe be prevented.
our task is not only to support different religions, but above all freedom of religion and[nbsp]worldview. secularism does not mean atheism or that society should be secular. conversely, the state must be secular, and that is to ensure freedom for all faiths and beliefs. it is the duty of the state to ensure this freedom and, by virtue of civil society, also the privacy of citizens, which is their religion. the state must be secular solely to ensure freedom of worldview, and not to favor any faith. that's the whole point of tolerance. the role of the state as a guarantor of freedom of thought must be an instrument of the european union's foreign policy. defending freedom for all religions, but also for conversion. it seems obvious, but it's not obvious even to us in europe. attempts to enshrine exclusively christian values in the constitution , as in the [hungarian] constitution , are a warning to us.
for the efd group with the increasing amount of waste produced by our society, the amount of hazardous waste that is illegally exported to third countries is also increasing. according to available studies and analyses, organised criminal groups are linked to the waste management sector, generating substantial profits from the illegal transfer of waste. the ever-growing illegal black market in waste is mainly due to the poorly organised structure of waste management mechanisms, which allows fraudsters to get rich on the significantly lower costs of waste treatment, especially in developing countries. we therefore need to radically improve our approach to waste disposal organisation, improve waste movement records and strengthen control mechanisms so as to significantly reduce the space and illegal waste management practices as recommended in the report presented. ladies and gentlemen, as today is the last day of the seventh parliamentary term of the european parliament and the last time we meet in this composition, let me wish you all the best of luck and success in your future personal and professional lives. thank you for your attention.
in writing - what ? in order to ensure the proper functioning of competition in the internal market, it is appropriate to establish an effective system of enforcement by means of administrative, civil and, where appropriate, criminal law. in many countries, private law actions for damages have proven to be an effective means of ensuring better compliance with competition rules, alongside public law enforcement. the two mechanisms are complementary insofar as their interaction is coherently adjusted. at eu level, we have been negotiating the introduction of common rules for actions for damages for infringements of competition rules for about a decade. i welcome the proposal for a directive because i am convinced that it will open the way for many consumers and businesses, especially small and medium-sized ones, who have been harmed, to exercise their right to compensation for the damage caused by infringements of competition law.
in writing - i supported the draft regulation on new psychoactive substances because they pose a health, social and safety risk. in several member states, including slovakia, sales and consumption have increased sharply in recent years, especially among young people. the rapid emergence and spread of these substances has so far been met with a mixed response from member states, which has complicated the functioning of the single market. the new regulation addresses this problem. substances suspected of posing an immediate risk to public health will be temporarily withdrawn from the consumer market until a risk assessment by the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction is available. when the risk assessment is completed, measures shall be taken which are proportionate to the risks associated with the substances concerned. these measures will be graded according to whether the risk is assessed as low, moderate or severe.

in writing - i supported the joint resolution of parliament on the situation in eastern ukraine. it is important that the european union responds to current events in the neighbouring partner country with determination and unanimity. the adoption of economic sanctions against the russian federation and tougher sanctions against its individual representatives should be a first step. in order to avoid further escalation of tensions, concrete measures against russian companies and their subsidiaries, in particular in the energy sector, as well as against russian investments and assets in the eu, should also be considered.

in writing italy holds the eu presidency of the council of the european union since 1 july 2014, replacing greece. the italian presidency comes shortly after elections marked by record low turnout and a significant strengthening of positions for extremist, far-right and left-wing parties. this result was to be expected, as the european institutions failed to respond quickly enough to the economic and financial crisis, and we failed to convince the citizens of the member states that we could help them with their everyday problems. as a result, citizens' trust in the eu institutions is logically declining. i believe that the deepening of the monetary union as well as a deeper focus on the 2020 strategy should be among the main priorities of the presidency. it is important that competition policy is reviewed, because european businesses need to lend a helping hand. the problem of youth employment persists in europe and it will therefore be important to ensure that the youth guarantee, which is to bring in so many needed jobs, is properly implemented. the ukrainian crisis and the eu's energy independence problems should not be forgotten. it is therefore clear that the italian presidency is facing a demanding six months. the european union is facing new challenges and problems that need to be addressed urgently. together we must show the european citizens that we are able to help them with their everyday problems and that we stand by them.
italy presented a very ambitious plan for its presidency. i appreciate that it has followed up the greek presidency in many areas and will focus on growth, unemployment and migration. the southern states cannot remain isolated in legal and illegal migration, but this issue must be addressed on a pan-european basis. there is a need to strengthen cooperation between countries of origin and transit, but also to combat illegal immigration through a more coherent legal migration policy. in setting the priorities, italy also very clearly identified the problem of smes, namely access to credit and financing. i also welcome the promotion theme of expo 2015 in milan, 'food for the planet, energy for life', and the emphasis on food policy, food labelling and food chain management and nutrition. i am convinced that italy, in cooperation with the new parliament, will manage its role as president very well.
mr president, i am a new member from slovakia and represent the hungarian community, which makes up 10% of the total population of my country. in the two reformist governments of slovakia i was deputy prime minister and was responsible for the successful integration of my country into the european family, which would have been totally unacceptable without the support of my voters. in 2008, when the ratification process of the lisbon treaty was blocked in slovakia, it was again my party, the hungarian minority party, which was in opposition at the time, that helped to adopt this crucial document. for these reasons, i think i can rightly ask that this parliament devote more attention to historical european minorities. i believe that if this parliament deals with these questions with the necessary understanding our common europe will be stronger and more credible to its citizens.

the vilnius process has made the european union a real geopolitical player with a great deal of appeal. the paradox is that the eu itself did not expect this. but it was russia that figured it out faster. the eu's appeal has broken russia's geopolitical sphere of influence game. russian foreign policy has attempted to operate within the borders of the former soviet union through a system of spheres of influence. the vilnius process has broken this plan and seriously paralysed it. it is from this angle that we must look at the situation in ukraine, but also in moldova and georgia. in all three cases, we may be faced with a russian attempt to resolve the situation by military force. the european union must prepare for this, diplomatically as well as militarily. this also requires a completely new approach to eu policy towards russia. this is a new challenge for us members, for afet, but also for the commission.
ukraine is a major test for the eu and its foreign policy. we need a stable and democratic ukraine, but also a partner with whom we share values. that is why it was important to sign the association agreement. but the eu should go further. ukraine needs our support on the international stage and it is in our interest that the conflict in eastern ukraine be resolved and not deepened further. the eu must be active in this respect in all international fora. relations with ukraine, but also with other eastern partners, are partly tied to relations with russia. we have to be united here. i regret that europe has not yet been able to move forward as a united front on energy security. our disunity gives russia an opportunity to use energy as a political weapon. such a course is dangerous for the whole of europe in the long run. unless we are united, we will pass this test weakened.

in writing. the events in ukraine in recent months have once again brought to our attention the fact that the settlement of national and ethnic issues is a key factor both within and outside europe. i welcome the debate in parliament on the situation in ukraine and call on the eu institutions to do everything possible to bring about an early settlement to the conflict. stabilisation and democratisation of ukraine cannot be achieved without the support of the european union. the basis for european cooperation and peaceful coexistence is mutual respect for the sovereignty of nations and solidarity with one another. this message should be conveyed by the european union to its non-eu negotiating partners. i am speaking as a hungarian politician from slovakia, as a person who knows the problems of minorities. i would like to mention the fact that there is a significant hungarian community in transcarpathia, ukraine. the possibility of asserting their rights, along with those of other national and linguistic minorities living there, must be part of future democratic processes. this can be achieved by the european union working as quickly as possible to achieve the recognition and practical application of fundamental human rights in ukraine, including linguistic and minority rights.
the transatlantic relationship is currently on a very thin ice. the ttip is one of our biggest international challenges. parliament and the commission have a new mandate, and we have a unique opportunity to move forward. our job is to approach the u.s. as a partner and an ally. it will be important to negotiate an agreement that reflects the benefits to both parties. the negotiations must be transparent and clearly communicated to our citizens. the european parliament will have the final say in the adoption of this agreement. but this agreement must respect our demands and will not be finalized at any cost. we will stand for a balanced and fair free trade agreement. when it comes to protecting europeans' personal data or the safety of our food, we must think of our citizens, from whom we have a mandate, and therefore protect their interests.
, in writing. ladies and gentlemen, colleagues, the debate cannot be about whether trade makes sense. because there was trade, any company could develop. that was true hundreds of years ago, it is true today, and it will be true in the future. when we fully implement the us-eu agreement, the added value for the european economy under the more optimistic scenario of the european commission's estimates will be €120 billion per year, which equals 0.5% of the european union's gdp. this will bring in €545 per year for each household in europe. this growth will be helped in particular by the removal of tariff and non-tariff barriers to goods and services. even under the worst-case scenario, the eu's gdp would increase by more than eur 68 billion per year and the united states' gdp by almost eur 50 billion. this will bring more than 1.5 million new skilled jobs to the eu and the us combined. this will bring new opportunities for trade and services and improve opportunities for new investment for european smes. ladies and gentlemen, the treaty of lisbon gave the european parliament the power to ratify such treaties. i hope we will take this opportunity to send a positive signal to national parliaments.
i was the shadow rapporteur on the proposal for an amendment to the directive on the safety and health of pregnant workers and workers who have recently given birth or are breastfeeding. the original 2008 text was limited to improving the working conditions of pregnant and breastfeeding women in paid employment. but in this parliament the text has begun to change, and in a substantial way. the requirement for gender equality introduced by the commission there led to the extension of the scope of the paternity leave, adoption and other requirements directives. parliament finally adopted a position which went far beyond the original framework of the amendment, even though it was clear that member states would never support such a text. for example, the income of women during maternity leave is defined differently in each member state and may come from different sources: insurance, the employer or the state itself. the amount of this income also varies and finding a workable european compromise is virtually impossible. the significant extension of this directive in the european parliament has meant that it has ultimately failed to raise the existing european standard of protection of pregnant and breastfeeding women in the labour market. i would therefore support the commission's decision to withdraw and reconsider the proposal. let this be a lesson to us in the future that the documents we receive should remain true to their title. i am in favour of introducing a mandatory six-week maternity leave in the european union, as recommended by the world health organisation. during this period, the woman should receive a full salary, and for the next 12 to 14 weeks, member states should, within the limits of their economies, provide financial support for all women who choose to continue caring for the child. the principle of subsidiarity should be strictly respected.
in writing. i find it very unfortunate that the commission is considering stopping the legislative process on the introduction of extended maternity leave, which it plans to abolish under the refit regulatory fitness and performance programme. this practice is regressive and endangers women's rights and gender equality. it is very sad that even in the 21st century, women are punished for childbirth by their employers instead of being given the right conditions. i am definitely in favour of extending maternity leave from the current 14 weeks to 20 and i also think that employers should guarantee mothers a return to their previous jobs. i do not understand the commission's approach, because the vast majority of large multinationals are not against this increase in maternity leave. i strongly reject the further breakdown of social rights due to the economic and financial crisis, because in slovenia alone, for example, the amount that mothers used to receive during parental leave has been reduced by 90% in recent years. i firmly believe that the european commission will not succumb to the growing conservative, religious and far-right lobby and will continue to work to reduce the gap between men and women and to protect women's rights as it has committed to do. the fact that mothers cannot work full-time during parental leave does not mean that they cannot be a benefit to the employer.
the unemployment of young people in slovakia has been alarming in recent years, not only because of the financial impact on society. young people without work do not acquire work experience and habits, they lose self-confidence, job prospects and many also lose the feeling that their studies were meaningful. the number of registered unemployed under 29 years of age accounts for more than one third of all registered unemployed, with young people with disabilities or from socially disadvantaged backgrounds being the most vulnerable group. therefore, it is necessary to build an inclusive model of education so that no group of children is excluded from a quality educational process, to promote more the development of students' key competences, initiative and creativity, as well as lifelong learning so that school leavers can be employers, not just employees. labour laws need to be simplified and the tax burden on labour reduced, and red tape in setting up and operating a business or firm reduced. initiatives in these areas would improve young people's chances of employment much more than spending millions of euros on temporary subsidised jobs, which do not solve the problem but only delay it.
the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the application of the 'blue card' procedure. yes, mr lucke, it is the lack of competitiveness that is causing these high unemployment rates in the south. now many of my colleagues are trying to solve this lack of competitiveness by investing more and more, but, frankly, there is more and more debt to be paid. my question to you is, where do you think this is going to end? today the european union is approaching a debt ratio of about 100%. what's enough? what's the optimal? we heard there's good debt. is 150% enough for you? what do you think?
slovakia also has one of the highest youth unemployment rates, with up to a third of the unemployed in the country. this situation can be changed directly or indirectly. the eu should continue to support the erasmus and erasmus plus exchange programmes, which will lead to a more intelligent and efficient workforce, which in turn will attract employers. the free movement of labour and services can only work if people speak foreign languages at a communicative level, and they must therefore be guided to do so. the european union should also pay more attention to disrupting the rigid structures of family rule and corruption, which will allow young and competent people to get into relevant positions that are currently unattainable for them. but we also need to change the current paradigm in how we motivate young people, not just teach them 'study something, work in a company and you'll be driving all your life', but encourage them to be creative in small businesses. member state governments must create opportunities for small businesses to emerge. but once upon a time, the father taught the son, he gave the son an example. today, we have in the eu the breakdown of the healthy model of a functioning family of man, woman and children, and above all the authority of the father, which affects the young individual and destabilises him, and therefore he cannot find a relevant job. in school, it was the master and the teacher, so i think schools must lead from theoretical knowledge to professional practice, so that young people go to work in crafts, because craftsmen are lacking here.
youth unemployment is caused by a number of factors in our society. on the one hand, it is the reduction of jobs as a consequence of the effects of the financial, economic and, unfortunately, social crisis. it is the extension of retirement. today, the retirement age is already so high that many jobs are blocked. it's the structure of education in the individual member states, and it's also, of course, a consequence of the overall situation and how production is transferred outside the member states of the european union, because businesses and firms want to generate ever higher and higher profits at the expense of the jobs they should be providing to employees in our member states of the european union. the youth guarantee is one of the good tools, but it is a short-term tool, and i think that in order to create more jobs not only for the young generation but in general, it is important to change the overall social structure in the member states of the european union and to start looking at how to harmonize social policy within the european union.
in writing. the commission's new president will be the first to take the initiative in the area of employment, growth and investment. we are in a critical situation, the eu is fighting against massive youth unemployment. we all agree that we must pay attention to youth employment. i think one of the main recipes for success is rooted in promoting entrepreneurship. let's create opportunities for the young. the entrepreneurial environment creates more jobs where young people can apply themselves and where they can use their experience and ideas. let us remove the administrative obstacles that unnecessarily impede the development of this sector. the second important element is the emphasis on training and support for those fields of study which give young people a chance of finding employment. we must be prepared to link the academic world with the practical world! we should encourage as many programmes as possible where young people can gain their first work experience. i would also like to recall that one of the cornerstones of the eu's functioning is the free movement of persons. mobility in the labour market is indispensable for young people in particular. in this respect, the creation of eures is a necessity.
in writing. we have been tackling youth unemployment in europe for several years, but it does not seem to be very successful. the young people themselves showed their discontent in may this year by not taking part in the european elections. they have simply given up, they have lost hope that europe can solve their problem of finding a job and secure their future. we are quite successful in offering young people the opportunity to study in any member state, but we can no longer guarantee them employment. we now have member states where youth unemployment is 4%, but also countries where it is above 30%. therefore, measures and guarantees for young people must also take into account national requirements, guarantee that education is linked to the requirements of the labour market. this will only work if all stakeholders, public authorities, schools and employers, realise the need to work together. i support any good resolution that we pass in the ep, but the greater initiative must also come from the member states themselves.

if you ask the ceos of small and medium-sized businesses, hospitals, but also mayors and mayors, they will tell you that if debtors do not pay them on time, they can go bankrupt or go into receivership. that is why we adopted a directive last term that companies and institutions must pay their liabilities within 30 days. if we're going to require financial discipline from others, we need to set an example ourselves. today we are once again convinced that we lack the resources to repay our debts. we're threatened by structural deficits and deformities with consequences. how can the council be made to honour these commitments, but also how can budgetary problems be solved systematically? the final solution is to complete the system of own resources to which the european union has committed itself. i expect the next commission, led by mr juncker, to help us do that.

in writing. when eu security is at stake, foreign policy takes precedence over trade policy. i think everyone would agree that if someone violates the constitution or international rules, we have to stand up for democracy. today we are taking steps to ensure that security for the eu. but we should know whether these steps lead to the goal and what we will do with them. if we've imposed sanctions on russia, we need to re-calculate all the implications and expect retaliation. if sanctions or retaliation means that eu countries lose markets, jobs and someone else takes over the space that is being freed, that is not a deliberate course of action. as regards the impact of retaliatory measures by russia, which has banned the import of certain foodstuffs, we must immediately: - prevent the destabilisation of the eu that the resulting surplus of foodstuffs may cause; - help to find new markets and outlets; - provide concrete compensation for the affected farmers, who now number over one million.
in writing. i consider the adoption of an association agreement with ukraine to be the best way to contribute to a peaceful settlement of the military conflict in ukraine. i see this act as a first step towards stabilising the situation and at the same time confirming the well-known principle that democratic states seek to resolve conflicts primarily through peaceful means, while totalitarian ones resort to violence and aggression. the association agreement will not only bring about the mutual opening of markets and the liberalisation of customs duties, but it is also a significant expression of solidarity of the whole of democratic europe with the struggling ukraine. while russian aggression in crimea and eastern ukraine has brought chaos, destruction, loss of life and economic decline, the association agreement will strengthen ukraine's economy, make its elites behave more responsibly and ensure greater freedoms for its people. anchoring ukraine in the european economic and political space will prevent the war that is going on there today from turning into a frozen conflict like we see in transnistria, abkhazia and south ossetia. i don't think ukraine is on the verge of disintegration, as some politicians and mps say. its citizens have been demonstrating for several months that they want a united, strong and, above all, democratic ukraine. it is our duty to do everything we can to help them achieve this goal. moreover, it is also in the clear interest of the european union and our citizens that we have stable and reliable neighbours.
in writing. today's ratification of the association agreement is a symbolic and historically important step in eu-ukraine relations. it's a step in the right direction. the eu has a longstanding clear policy towards ukraine based on respect for its free and sovereign decisions. the association agreement is the result of a long process of negotiations confirming this policy. this was confirmed today by both the european parliament and the verkhovna rada of ukraine. i believe that this agreement will bring economic benefits to ukraine and will be an incentive for further reforms. the arguments against ratifying this agreement are misleading and inaccurate and are often the result of russian propaganda. the european union is interested in ukraine as a partner, both in trade and in politics. today's ratification sends a signal that we are committed to this goal. the tense situation that exists in ukraine is not the result of this agreement, but of russia's aggressive policy towards its neighbors. i firmly believe that such a policy will not last.

in writing. - what ? i voted for the association agreement with ukraine for several reasons. ukraine is geographically divided into two parts by the dnieper river. historically, the left side of the river was called the "left bank" and the right side the "right bank". one part always gravitated more towards russia and the other towards europe. this was also evident in 2004 during the famous orange revolution. statistics show that 78% of the population of ukraine considers themselves ethnic ukrainians and up to 17% proudly identify as russians. in late 2004, the ukrainians experienced their orange revolution. ukrainians have experienced totalitarianism, corrupt politicians and oligarchs. i see the association agreement as a start, but a step in the right direction for ukraine. but the country still has a long way to go before it can hold a free referendum. i believe that ukrainians have the right to freely choose where they want to belong. i stress free. no pressure, no war. ukraine is not a pie to be sliced, it's our partner. i'm glad we sealed the signing of the association agreement today. its main contribution is, in addition to political partnership, the pursuit of freedom of expression, visa-free travel, an atmosphere of democracy and mutual free access to the market. i ask people to pray for this country, which is being tested by bloody conflict.

for the ppe group. the debate in plenary in strasbourg at this late hour should stimulate us to a good debate, but above all to tangible results. today we're dealing with a man, a patient who is in dire need of his medication. access to life-saving medicines is the topic of today's debate. the fate of millions of patients, whether in slovakia, finland, ireland, italy, anywhere, is close to my heart, not only because as a doctor i have seen people die, and i know from my former practice in life-saving medicine in the hospital ward how important it is to have quality medicine available, not just in the intensive care unit, but in all the wards of the hospital, and especially for a patient with a serious illness to have real, real access to medicine. to medicines that are and will be affordable, in his social situation and income level, and will be of quality and fully available. there is still inequality in access to the necessary medicines, partly due to the different levels of healthcare funding in different member states, which then translates into the price of medicines, into the different prices, and the constant increase in pharmaceutical expenditure, and partly because new technologies are financially demanding. on the other hand, pharmaceutical companies that develop new drugs should not abuse their exclusive position and then drive up the price of the drugs to unbearable levels. it's unethical and immoral. between 2000 and 2009, public expenditure on medicines in eu countries increased by an average of up to 76%. this expenditure on medicines is growing faster than the gdp of the member states. and at the same time, we have to take into account the ageing population in the member states, in other words more pensioners and the ever-distant price of newly developed medicines. in this context, it is necessary to systematically allow the same molecule in the generics sector to be made available to patients as soon as possible and at a disproportionately lower cost.
granted, developing new life-saving drugs is very costly. however, it is not justifiable for a patient to be denied the care he needs for financial reasons. every patient in europe has the right to quality treatment and access to medicines. the principle of equitable distribution of resources must be applied without compromise. it is unacceptable that such medicines should be available only to the privileged and that the socially weaker should be excluded from such treatment. then there are situations where the door opens to corruption and clientelism, which is what we want to fight against in europe. if we're talking about life-saving drugs, we should also be talking about situations where the possibilities of actively and fundamentally influencing the prognosis of the disease are exhausted. this is where palliative care should come in. in contrast to conventional treatments, it offers a higher quality of life even in cases of advanced and terminal illness. this is care that ensures quality and comprehensive care for the seriously ill or those at the end of life, with full respect for human dignity and rights. i suppose most politicians are familiar with the saying, "civilization is only as advanced as it is concerned with its weakest and most helpless members". i think it is the responsibility of politicians to live up to that statement in our work. it is shocking that our advanced civilization, instead of making it easier for the elderly and the terminally ill to receive special care, is increasingly striving to eliminate them. in the past, they were confined to institutions; now they are being offered a low-cost solution, namely, euthanasia. it's a paradox. europe's development and prosperity allow us to have a real quality and dignified life for everyone, but we still debate and justify how to get rid of unprofitable people. the latest horrifying example is belgium, where they extended euthanasia to children and the mentally ill. i can't help but think of ancient sparta, where they killed unsuitable children, or nazi eugenics, where they eliminated the unsuitable. where does such a view lead? society will start to throw out people with disabilities or those who don't meet our standards. i have to say that in slovakia, the state does not provide adequate palliative care. in july this year, the parliament pointed out that the youth employment initiative should not be the only remedy for youth employment problems, but that it was an additional aid which should not replace the role of the member states and their own responsibility in addressing youth problems. and now for the very guarantee of youth. the success of youth programmes and initiatives depends in particular on the ability of member states to deliver on these commitments in terms of actual content, and this depends on the implementation capacity of states. unfortunately, the countries that need the most help often have the lowest and make only limited improvements. employment services tasked with helping young people are often unable to provide personalised and quality services. they tend to help where it is easiest, renounce aid to the long-term unemployed and benefit from measures to those who have the least difficulty finding employment. for example, preference is given to funding young people's places, but the long-term sustainability of the jobs thus created is often secondary. if we are to help young people find work, the member states, and particularly those most affected by youth unemployment and inactivity, must first of all know what they are doing and evaluate the quality of the services they provide to young people. the commission must ensure that member states take the easy way out, but also that they are able to make use of the possibilities offered by the single market.
