in writing, i voted against. the eu will give spain €1.66 million, 475 dutch people €1.6 million, 508 greeks more than €6 million and 1,000 romanians less than €3.6 million. but these contributions are arbitrary, collected from other people's taxes, and do not address the cause of the problem, only the mild consequences. the root of the problem is the lack of competitiveness of european economies, which continues to be undermined by excessive climate targets and unnecessarily high social protection and other standards. as long as the eu sets itself conflicting goals, for example climate goals versus increasing industrial production, jobs will disappear.
ebola is something that we can boldly compare to an epidemic, a plague in the middle ages. it's something that can destroy the functioning of any country. we know that liberia, but also sierra leone, are on the verge of collapse as countries. multiple agencies brought it in. unfortunately, the number of people infected is growing exponentially, and you know that we doctors don't have an effective antidote or vaccine yet. work is underway, but the eu certainly needs to make a much greater and more concentrated effort to promote a faster delivery of sanitation solutions, not just preventive and epidemiological measures. we must show more solidarity and we must show that this is a human rights issue and that it is the eu's duty to help our neighbours in africa.
what we're seeing in iraq and syria is a genocide of christian communities. terrorists calling themselves the 'islamic state' are depriving christians of their basic rights, driving them from their homes, killing, torturing, kidnapping, raping women, and enslaving them. christians become refugees for their faith and religious beliefs. the right to freedom of speech and religion is part of the universal declaration of human rights. the promotion of respect for human rights is a fundamental principle of the european union. but even deeper in the foundations of european unity are the christian values that have shaped the morals, social relations, and public institutions of all european nations for two millennia. solidarity with members of the christian communities in iraq is not a goodwill gesture, it is our duty. yes, i believe we have an obligation to protect the christians in iraq and syria, they have only us. if we don't stand up for them, no one else will. it is not enough to help them survive the winter, our goal must be to allow the iraqi and syrian christians to return home. if they need defense, the protection of peace congregations, we must provide it. i welcome the creation of an international coalition that can take action against them. but let us also think about the need for reconciliation, because without it there is no way to bring stability to this region. only reconciliation can heal relations between muslims and christians. and the path to reconciliation leads through inspiring personal examples. that is why i ask you, dear colleagues, to sign your name in support of the nomination of patriarch saak and professor al-assali for the sakharov prize. their stories contain a message of reconciliation and mutual respect that appeals to both iraqi christians and muslims.
the palestinian conflict in gaza has already cost many lives and i want to condemn this unequivocally. but we must clearly name the cause of the outbreak of tension and condemn the attacks on the state of israel. hamas is not warning the residents of the attacks, but instead is urging them to stay home and even putting them in the front lines. hamas hides rockets in schools and hospitals, forcing israel to attack these civilian facilities where women and children are present. a renowned czech psychiatrist characterized it as israel protecting its people with missiles and hamas protecting its missiles with people. the response from israel must be proportionate, but we have to see the bigger picture, although i think israel has gone too far in this war. the european security strategy 2030 clearly states that resolving the arab-israeli conflict is a strategic priority for europe. without it, there is little chance of solving the other problems in the middle east. resolving this conflict is a key prerequisite for us to be able to confront the threat of terrorism. i therefore call on the eu to develop a peace effort to try to stop the violence in gaza. but i realize that the civilian casualties also significantly damage israel's image in the world. at the same time, i am alarmed by the rise of anti-semitism in europe. hatred of israel is openly transformed into hatred of jews. angela merkel said that there was no jewish institution left in germany that had not recently asked for police protection. we all have a duty to stand up against anti-semitism and not allow it to spread. the european union is preparing a document on freedom of religion. i want to ask how the work on these texts has progressed and how freedom of religion is defined there. according to 2011 data, up to 76% of europeans profess christianity, but the word christian is not even mentioned. in general, it speaks of freedom of religion, but the rights of religious groups, such as muslims, are mentioned far more often. nor does the report of the european union agency for human rights entitled "fundamental rights: challenges and achievements for 2011" even mention the word christian. i would ask the commission to take the right to freedom of religion seriously in preparing these documents and to take into account the fact that many citizens profess christianity. i would also like to call on the european commission to take citizens' opinions more seriously. i am disappointed by the commission's attitude to the most successful european initiative, called one of us, which also concerned human rights, namely the unborn. the fact that almost 800,000 citizens have expressed an opinion that the commission has literally swept off the table, unfortunately, damages the image of the european institutions... today's debate also shows that the situation in ukraine and the advance of the islamic state in the middle east are the two most important crises. closely linked to this are our efforts to defend the values on which the eu is built: the democratic political establishment and respect for civil liberties. last year we called for human rights and democracy to play a more important role in the union's external relations. we have called for a more systematic and effective promotion of human rights and democracy in the world. as rapporteur for last year's eu human rights report, i repeat what i said then. the protests in ukraine and the arab spring have reminded us that people do not accept the curtailment of human rights and want to decide the direction of their countries. and it's our duty to help them do that.
for the ecr group. i am pleased that in this resolution, at my instigation, parliament also decided to condemn the july attack on nuns in boldipukur, when nuns, who are otherwise highly respected in bangladesh, were brutally attacked. i would like to add that this was not just a religiously motivated attack. the diocese there helps to legally protect the property of the poor. for a long time, various groups have been trying to take over their fertile soil illegally, and attacks on missionaries are acts of intimidation. following the july attack and subsequent protests by the bangladeshi bishops, the government ordered a temporary police presence around the monastery. hopefully, this will not only deter the perpetrators from similar attacks but also end the persecution of indigenous minorities in the region.
i welcome the european parliament's clear position on the occurrence of a serious infectious disease. the crisis surrounding the ebola outbreak is complex. it has political, economic and social implications and consequences that will persist even after the primary health threat has passed. the outbreak of this disease is no longer a west african problem, but it is a global problem. there are two ways to do this. on the one hand, to promote the accelerated development of effective medicines and therapies, but on the other hand, to pay constant attention to the provision of quarantine and clinical care. it is shown that this quarantine only works if the population has confidence in the health workers and the government, which is not the case in liberia, and therefore the eu should use all diplomatic options to stabilise the political situation in liberia as well.
in writing. - what ? i am very pleased that the european union has taken a position on the outbreak of this deadly virus. the large outbreaks in west african countries such as guinea, sierra leone, liberia and nigeria confirm the need to develop new medicines, possibly by increasing the production of generics. in particular, they must be affordable so that they can be effectively deployed in developing countries where the virus has occurred. the west african countries have a history of destabilisation and therefore there is a need to link humanitarian efforts to negotiations with a view to de-escalating the situation in the region, in particular in liberia.
in writing. - i supported the motion for a joint resolution on the eu response to the ebola outbreak, because every day the media is reporting on the alarming spread of this deadly disease and people are dying every day from the consequences of this disease. even the world health organization acknowledged that the epidemic was underestimated in its early days, and it estimates the number of possible patients at up to 20,000. although the deadly ebola virus has struck african countries, we must establish robust control mechanisms before it spreads to other continents, and at the same time help the affected african countries with a robust information campaign, as well as humanitarian and medical assistance. the €147 million allocated by the european commission's development aids to humanitarian aid and civil protection to prevent the spread of the virus, to provide the most necessary equipment for infected people and to deploy humanitarian experts will certainly help.

, in writing the world health organization warned last week that in the next two months, there could be up to 10,000 new cases of ebola each week in west africa. so the biggest fight against ebola is still ahead of us. we need to provide adequate assistance to west africa and also start crisis management within europe. europe will very likely soon also be the territory where ebola will spread. the question is whether we are prepared for this situation, whether we can identify cases of infection in its early stages, ensure adequate quarantine and prevent further spread. the question is also whether our health systems are prepared for this situation. the cases from the united states show that underestimating the situation can be critical to human lives. international coordination is needed to prevent ebola from becoming a pandemic. we should establish crisis management within the union and prepare for further spread scenarios of this epidemic.
i have to ask you, mr. andor. the european union and you personally still have a complete misunderstanding of the causes of this crisis. in its efforts to help jobs and growth, the european union is blindly using only two instruments. more regulation and more redistribution. if it doesn't work, it does more of the same, more regulation and more redistribution. the opposite is needed to create jobs. the current eu strategy is much worse than the eu doing nothing, because it only exacerbates the very problems it is trying to solve. four things are needed to really solve the problems: firstly, to strengthen the internal market as the basic pillar of the european union; secondly, to put an end to the rescue of irresponsible countries, which is contrary to the lisbon treaty; thirdly, to depoliticise the european central bank; and fourthly, to respect the principles of subsidiarity. if you don't follow these points, you can meet as much as you want, but you're not going to solve youth unemployment.

in writing it is very important that young people find employment after leaving school and are able to make full use of their education, because if they do not succeed and develop a work habit at a young age, they can easily fall into frustration and lose confidence in their own abilities, which will ultimately increase tensions in society and put additional strain on the social systems of the member states. i think it is time to acknowledge that the european employment model has serious problems and shortcomings, as evidenced by the 7.6 million jobs lost between 2007 and 2013. we need to show young people that they have a chance to work and give them hope for a better future. it must be admitted that the reforms carried out in the individual member states have managed to stabilise the labour market situation in part, but i do not share mr barros' boundless optimism, because the situation is still serious and we must approach it as such. however, i can agree that growth in europe is very fragile and uneven and that member states that have been responsible and have implemented structural reforms are in a much better position and more advantageous situation today. a good example is italy, where a major reform has been implemented to help young italians under 25 get into work.

in writing. – during mr barroso’s term of office yet more power has been transferred from nation states to the eu. i would like to highlight one particular area which is both topical and highly dangerous and concerns europol, the eu’s emergent police force. in a recent bbc radio interview mr troels oerting, the head of europol’s cybercrime centre, revealed that: ‘most of the world’s cybercrime originates from russia’; that he is ‘increasingly happy’ with europol’s ‘cooperation’ with russia's ‘law-enforcement authorities’ and that he had recently visited moscow and agreed to share information with them on a number of ‘interesting cases’. this is an extraordinary statement given that in russia it is impossible to distinguish between organised crime and the so-called law enforcement agencies. meanwhile, a respected internet security company, isight, has uncovered a massive russian cyber espionage campaign against western governments and nato. this is at a time when the british parliament is on the verge of voting to permanently transfer yet more power to the eu on policing and criminal justice matters. it is utter madness to share sensitive information with russia, which is a gangster state, and seriously puts at risk our security and the protection of our citizens.
, in writing. i consider the second european commission under josé manuel barroso to be a major failure. the commission's failings have been evident since its inception. the treaty of lisbon was adopted just over a week after the second barroso commission formally began its work on 1 december 2009. but already at the beginning of may 2010, only six months later, aid to greece was approved, in direct contradiction to the newly approved treaty of lisbon. shortly afterwards, the breach of the lisbon treaty became systemic, as the temporary euro (efsf) was created first, and the permanent euro (esm) later. the systematic breach of the rules by the european union under barroso was not the only negative of this european commission. of the 27 commissioners at the start of her term, at least 10 had a communist background or are still committed to communist ideas. similarly, the response of the second barros commission to the crisis was very unhappy. the european union continued to push for more of the same, which caused the current crisis redistributing taxpayers' money and issuing a host of harmful regulations. as a result, barroso is now leaving the eu budget in a state where up to four-fifths of it is used for payments to farmers and the cohesion funds, with no prospect of fundamental change.
the european union has a strategy, but it does not allocate enough budgetary resources to implement it. it's all about 26 billion euros in unpaid invoices. we are endangering the confidence of the whole of europe. we see that the budget in the future cannot depend on whether or not the council meets its commitments. we therefore need to create a smart european budget that will provide the european union with the resources to achieve its ambitions without increasing the overall tax burden on citizens. let us take from the national level all that we can do more effectively at the european level. there are savings and resources in more intensive european integration. to be credible to citizens, the budget of the european union must set an example in terms of governance, efficiency, transparency and control.
in every decision taken in the european parliament, we should keep in mind our objectives, and those objectives are, first and foremost, growth and improving the employment of our citizens. the budget structure always has a strong influence on such objectives. that's why i'll always push for less spending on consumption, less spending on administration, but more spending on supporting small businesses, science, research, innovative technologies. i will always support such proposals. even if we are trying to balance the budget here, our debts are still growing and even a year should reach the level of 30 billion euros, so we need to improve the payment discipline and above all to follow the rules. on the one hand, we're talking about 30-60 days, but on the other hand, we're running up debts. i therefore think that we need to introduce penalties for non-payment also at the level of the european institutions, in order to improve payment discipline and to comply with the rules, only then can the european budget really work. we often talk about the expenditure side, but i consider the revenue side of the budget to be even more important, and it can only work if the environment for job creation is functioning. so we should talk more about improving competitiveness, lower taxes, fees, better labour laws and supporting education, science, research. i hope we'll talk more about this and i hope we'll do well in this.

in writing - i did not support the draft budget of the european union because it is primarily about redistributing money that it takes from those who create value. a large part of the budget is consumed by the common agricultural policy, which should have ceased to exist long ago. similarly, much of it goes to the european funds, which are being stolen and distorting the business environment. the european union needs a fundamental budgetary change: more savings on its administration, less regulation, the abolition of agricultural subsidies and so on.
in writing. - what ? at first sight, it seems logical that member states, in an effort to save budgets and reduce deficits, are proposing substantial cuts in the draft budget for 2015. however, on closer inspection, many of these proposals concern mainly key areas such as innovation, research, infrastructure, support for smes and energy. in particular, energy security cannot be achieved without increased investment in energy infrastructure, energy efficiency, interconnections and renewable energy generation. the appropriations approved by the council in the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework for the years 2014-2020 are already missing from the 2015 budget. i therefore support parliament's position calling for a return to the commission's proposal to avoid delays in payments for research, universities and business development. i also consider it populist for heads of state and government to speak at every summit about the importance of growth and job creation, while at the same time the same politicians propose cuts in the eu budget in these areas.

in my view, the role of the european parliament's resolutions is to draw attention to breaches of commitments, to seek to correct undesirable situations, to reward and encourage successful achievement of objectives and commitments. the institutions of the european union have clearly defined powers and instruments to achieve their objectives. it is therefore unnecessary to resort to expressive language that is not really valid. this is what is happening in the resolution on the european semester. as an institution, we regret that europe is in danger of falling behind. we can express grief when someone dies, when some natural disaster occurs, when things happen that we cannot control, but not where we are expected to offer suggestions and concrete solutions. i therefore call on parliament, and therefore on all of you, to avoid such language in future and to focus our attention on proposing real solutions that will move europe forward.
in writing. - the economic and financial crisis has made it clear to us that we are vulnerable. the degree of economic inequality between states is increasing. this is why there is an urgent need to develop an integrated economic policy that will boost the economies of the member states of the european union. one such instrument is the large and integrated market, with key sectors being financial services, transport, energy and digital. the european semester plays a fundamental role in coordinating the economic and budgetary policies of the member states. it is essential to growth to maintain jobs and create new ones. although unemployment rates have fallen and employment is improving in europe, youth unemployment is still critically high. further action is needed to eliminate discrimination against young workers. furthermore, the european union must assure its citizens that it is committed to equal treatment in all areas. i also supported the motion because it calls the attention of the european union agency for fundamental rights to the impact of the measures on fundamental rights. we must promote overall, harmonious development leading to a strengthening of economic, social and territorial cohesion. therefore, particular attention should be paid to rural areas, areas affected by industrial change and regions with natural or demographic handicaps. many crossborder regions also need further development to rise to the eu level.
in writing - this report marks the entry into the second phase of the implementation and implementation of the priorities for the 2014 european semester for economic policy coordination. the original report was completely unacceptable. despite the fact that many positive changes have been achieved and incorporated, the report still refers to the macroeconomic conditionality introduced by the european structural funds. significant achievements include the call for the swift implementation of a new 300 billion investment plan, the provision of a degree of flexibility to member states with regard to the rules of the stability and growth pact, and the need to increase public investment.
we see how most eu member states are failing to address the financial crisis, the sovereign debt crisis and the competitiveness crisis. it is therefore necessary to continue with thorough, balanced and socially sustainable structural reforms. without them, the economic growth on which employment depends cannot be launched. key in this regard are in particular investments in research and development, industrial development, education and skills and the efficient use of resources at both national and european level. however, i myself still have doubts whether and to what extent investments can be effective, if they are not decided by private investors motivated by the desire to make a profit, but by officials and politicians. i am therefore pleased that in this resolution parliament has reiterated its support for structural reforms and has reiterated the importance of reducing the level of public debt. only then can the conditions for economic growth, on which jobs depend, be created. they are the main prerequisite for success in the fight against poverty and social exclusion.

the berlin wall was a tangible symbol of the cold war, which divided the peoples of europe for more than 40 years. the fall of the berlin wall heralded the collapse of the soviet union and the unification of germany. this weekend, we commemorated the 25th anniversary of a historic event for the entire democratic world. it is worth noting the democratic movements in poland, hungary and the former czechoslovakia, which inspired the people of the gdr to a great extent to resist dictatorship. it is very important that we do not forget the horrors of the cold war and constantly remember the heroism of the people who fought against an unjust system, for which they paid the highest price, with their own lives. it is very sad that we have failed to learn and we have failed to change things for the better, as evidenced by the situation in ukraine, syria and iraq, where hundreds of innocent people are still being killed and where individual states are suppressing the fundamental rights of their citizens. europe must remember the very ideals that led to the fall of the berlin wall and protect with far greater intensity those who are the targets of political oppression and violence in many regions of the world.
the fall of the berlin wall was one of the greatest milestones of the last century. it was proof that central planning and totalitarian restraint of freedom don't work and that the market and freedom are much more efficient. sadly, many people have forgotten these principles or have never identified with them. that is why more and more people today want to solve the crisis by more restricting the market. but the cause of the current crisis is not market failure, but, as in 1989, excessive central planning. so the solution today, as then, is more markets and more freedom. in the 25 years since the fall of the berlin wall, the world has changed dramatically, but the economic principles remain the same. and if anyone thinks that greater state intervention is beneficial, there is no better counterargument than the differences in living standards between the so-called eastern bloc and the western countries. that is why, even after a quarter of a century, we must not forget the fall of the berlin wall and, above all, the legacy it symbolizes.
in writing the fall of the berlin wall is a major historical event for the whole of europe. its anniversary commemorates the defining moments of our common history - the fall of the iron curtain that divided the continent for decades. the end of the cold war 25 years ago brought human courage in the form of the fall of the berlin wall in germany, the solidarity movement in poland, and the velvet revolution in what was then czechoslovakia. the year 1989 saw a series of events that are now key symbols of european unification. without them, the european union would not exist in its present form. the celebration of the quarter-century is proof that the european project is unique and still relevant, and only its continuation will bring the real and final erasure of the iron curtain from the mind map.
in writing. 25 years have passed since the fall of the wall that divided europe in two. everything seems to have gone so fast and the continent, despite the current economic crisis, seems to have found a modus vivendi that is unparalleled in the world. but we must remember that this peace is fragile. there are many examples of "stable" countries that have fallen into collective madness, and history is here to remind us of this. this reminder must be all the more important in view of the sensitive international context. let us therefore continue to give priority to dialogue without renouncing ourselves. the temptation to totalitarianism is never far away and the best way to achieve it is to give up. that is why, although peace is a matter of course for the younger generations of europeans, it must never be placed on the balance sheet of any negotiation. freedom is not negotiable. parts of europe have suffered too much, but, paradoxically, it is this same lack of freedom that has helped shape europe.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your report. in my speech i would like to address you on the statement made by president schulz. after the scottish referendum, president schulz said: in catalonia, flanders or south tyrol, much depends on what happens in scotland. if scotland were to achieve 'reasonable' self-determination 'under the united kingdom's roof', this could 'become a model that can contribute to peace in other regions'. i agree with what you have said, mr president, but i would like to draw the attention of all colleagues to the fact that, according to your statement, there is still an imaginary berlin wall. there are also communities in eastern europe for which democratically established autonomy can be an important advantage. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. we should not forget that there are also minorities living east of the former iron curtain, whose situation is unresolved and whose democratic and legally guaranteed development should be in the interests of european democracies.
november 17th is not only a day to remember the struggle for freedom, but it is also a day to remember the world's premature babies. those who are with us, but also those who were too fragile to leave us. each year, as many as 15 million babies are born prematurely worldwide, and more than a million of these do not live to see their first birthday. this means that as many as one in ten babies are born weighing less than one ounce [2,500 g], and many times they are below the 1,000-gram mark. premature babies are exposed to many health complications, and many will face lifelong disabilities. it is therefore essential to improve the quality of healthcare for women during pregnancy and thus halt the rising trend of preterm births. it is also crucial to improve early care for these children to improve their quality of life and that of their families.
november 17 is as symbolic for the czech and slovak people as the fall of the berlin wall was for germany. twenty-five years ago, our people returned to europe. a united europe stands on the defeat of nazism and communism. it would be a threat to europe if we did not remember nazism and its crimes. but so does ignoring the crimes of communism. communism was defeated by the courage of men and women who believed in freedom and human dignity. the door to november '89 was opened by thousands of underground church activists and a candlelight demonstration in bratislava the year before. i also want to mention two great figures whose support meant a great deal to us at the time: pope john paul ii and president ronald reagan. the law finally triumphed over the lawless. freedom from totalitarianism. and i am very pleased to be able to remind you of this as a representative of the citizens of the slovak republic here in the european parliament.
in writing. - i supported the motion because i do not agree that a member of the european parliament should be punished for his statements, however controversial or inappropriate. the appropriateness or inappropriateness of members' statements should be assessed solely by the electorate, which can also decide whether to vote on the basis of those statements.
in writing - i voted against this motion because i am convinced that it will do more harm than good. special support for selected redundant workers at the expense of all eu taxpayers creates harmful incentives and further exacerbates the problems it seeks to address.
in writing - i did not support the proposal because it is wrong in principle and harmful. support for the unemployed who have lost their jobs as a result of globalisation is financed by taxes from all other employees, or companies, which consequently have less money left to create sustainable jobs.
in writing the deteriorating relations between the european union and the russian federation have a significant impact on the entire region and thus also have a significant impact on the security situation in the republic of moldova. the republic of moldova has recently implemented the necessary reforms, indicating its readiness to apply for membership of the european union in 2015. relations between the european union and the republic of moldova have improved in recent times, as evidenced by the ratification of the association agreement, including the deep and comprehensive free trade area. it is important that the european union continues to support the highest representatives of political life in the republic of moldova in the implementation of fundamental reforms in the areas of justice, economy and human rights.
in writing. – i voted against this report as in this report the importance of anti-discrimination legislation to ensure equality was mentioned. this referred to lgtbi issues and is in my opinion not the competence of the european union but of the republic of moldova.
in writing, the republic of moldova is the next country to which we must open our doors if it meets all the conditions and its people wish to participate in the european project. moldova deserves such a chance. i support the pro-european government and its efforts to resist russian pressure, and i believe that in the elections scheduled for 30 november 2015, the moldovans will appreciate this.
in writing - i supported the proposal as it helps to eliminate trade and other barriers between moldova and the european union. the removal of these barriers is mutually beneficial for both parties as it will contribute to greater competition and economic freedom and thus ultimately to greater economic growth and employment.
in writing. - for moldova, today's vote on the association agreement is a promising sign in its march towards the european union. the european parliament voted by a very large majority (535 votes in favour, 94 against and 44 abstentions) in favour of this agreement, which at the same time allows chisinau to benefit from a comprehensive and in-depth free trade agreement with the european union. while this positive signal is not an end in itself, it does encourage moldova to continue its reforms in order to establish itself definitively in a european perspective. despite an unenviable geographical situation in these times of regional unrest, we must continue to encourage moldova which seems to have chosen a forward-looking orientation rather than the status quo. the elections on 30 november will determine the future of this country, but the european parliament has sent a clear message by reaching out to it with this vote.

in writing. – i voted in favour of this association agreement as it will give the republic of moldova the chance of building a prosperous future for itself and its citizens within europe. it will help the citizens to get a better life and visa-free travel to the eu and reform their economy.
in writing - northern ireland is in a much better situation today than it was during the conflict period of the 1960s-1990s, when politically motivated killings were a regular occurrence and some 3500 people lost their lives. the peace process has progressed since the adoption of the belfast agreement, but significant conflicts related to sectarian and national identity persist in the country, contributing to occasional outbreaks of violence and disorder. today, paramilitary organisations in northern ireland continue to recruit new members and to destabilise the political situation and peace in the country. the government has recently reached a dead end on contentious issues and is unable to reach a compromise and adopt the necessary legislation to solve the country's fundamental problems.
in writing - i supported the motion, rejecting any violent solution to conflicts. violence harms all parties involved, and therefore i support instead the resolution of the existing economic problems, which is also spoken of in this resolution. the way to solve these problems is greater economic freedom. even greater prosperity achieved by increased freedom can contribute to the elimination of violence in northern ireland.
in writing. - for almost a year now, the dialogue on the northern ireland peace process has been stalled or even weakened by disagreements on issues such as social spending, the use of flags, emblems and parades. the text voted today is important in that it stresses the need for stakeholders to join the talks launched on 16 october 2014 and whose main objective is to support stability and the functioning of democratic institutions in northern ireland. the text also calls for work on a lasting resolution of the conflict, including through improved relations between communities and economic and social development. finally, he mentioned that the eu peace programme is providing €150 million to tackle priority issues in northern ireland and the border, for the benefit of all, north and south. the text can therefore only encourage the resumption of negotiations and is an encouraging sign of the european union's determination to achieve the ultimate objective of peace and reconciliation in northern ireland.

in writing. - the condemnation of turkey for its actions in the exclusive economic zone (eez) of the republic of cyprus is a clear message that needed to be sent. by carrying out 'maritime studies' in the cypriot eez, turkey has blatantly violated the sovereignty of the republic of cyprus and international law. that is why this vote is a signal of firmness against the threats and actions of ankara which are undermining the process of negotiations to settle the cyprus question. it is also important to mention that the aim of the text is to prevent an escalation which could, in the short term, penalise turkey itself in its relations with the european union. in a particularly sensitive international context, dialogue must prevail more than ever. however, when a member state is faced with external pressures threatening its territorial sovereignty, parliament must play its part. that is what he has done today by sending a warning to turkey.
in writing. - what ? long-running fighting has already claimed more than 10,000 lives in the world's youngest and most fragile state. several observers note that the peace talks have brought only an imperceptible improvement in the situation and that the ceasefire agreement, which was concluded in january 2014 and subsequently confirmed in may, is being violated continuously and without any repressive measures. for a just and sustainable solution in the region, it is essential to establish an international commission of inquiry to investigate all allegations by the parties to the conflict and draw criminal conclusions from them. the commission in question should be given full support by the international community. it is essential that all countries in the region cooperate with south sudan to prevent the conflict from spilling over into other areas and thus contribute to a peaceful resolution of the ongoing crisis.
in writing - sudan faces violence and civil war. i voted against, in accordance with the agreement within the ecr group of which i am a member, and so other ecr colleagues joined me.
in writing - i supported the motion because i reject the violence being committed in south sudan. this violence has resulted not only in a number of needlessly lost lives but also in keeping the people of this country in poverty and degradation. as a country rich in natural resources (up to 70% of gdp is oil exports), south sudan's desperate situation is inexcusable.
in writing. - the resolution adopted by the european parliament on thursday reflects the importance which the european union attaches to the humanitarian situation in south sudan. the resolution also calls for a ceasefire and calls for targeted international sanctions and support for the establishment of a special international tribunal to prosecute leaders involved in human rights violations. this conflict, which has already killed more than 10 000 people and displaced 1.4 million, has created a dramatic humanitarian situation and we cannot stand by and watch this tragedy unfold. as the leading international contributor (38% of international contributions), the european union, and through it the european parliament, wishes to stabilise this young country whose tragedies weigh on the neighbouring countries as well as on europe. however, i very much regret that there are references in this text to sexual and reproductive rights and contraception. it is clear that such concepts will not solve the humanitarian situation in south sudan in any way and are only intended to discredit and destabilise a population already weakened by daily tragedies.
in writing. i have long been a critic of the policies of european commission president jean-claude juncker, but juncker has shown his inappropriate approach particularly openly in response to the current case of preferential tax regimes in luxembourg during his administration. the president of the european commission responded to these allegations by saying that it was precisely because of cases like these that the european union had to do more to harmonise taxes. in the case of the allegation of distortion of competition, juncker is basically seeking to ban competition altogether, which is absurd. unfortunately, a similarly incorrect approach is shared by several other members of the juncker commission. this is why i think that the european commission should not continue in its present composition.
if we want to talk about the millennium development goals, we have to decide whether we consider them a serious plan or a publicity stunt. if we take them literally, it means they ended up with a partial failure. extreme poverty and hunger have not yet been eliminated. we have not defeated hiv, malaria, or tuberculosis. a sustainable environment has not been achieved. the infant mortality rate was only cut in half, not two-thirds. it has failed to provide basic education for all children. but i think that kind of view would blind us to what's really important. a huge shift for the better has been achieved in all these areas. the millennium development goals should therefore be seen as a call for concrete action rather than a burning call for action. it's the most successful global development project. and if something works, you have to keep doing it. but abortion has never guaranteed any woman's health. the right to abortion, which is included among the so-called reproductive rights, is not the solution to infant mortality or maternal health, as the text presented in some paragraphs tries to tell us. women in developing countries need hospitals with doctors who abide by the hippocratic oath, not abortion clinics. we can ask dr. mukwege about it tomorrow. i support the efforts to continue successful development projects that deliver results.
decisions at european level affect over 500 million of our citizens, so it is really crucial that we know the impact and consequences of these decisions. the quality of the whole impact assessment process is therefore important for the whole development of our union. i want to emphasize the following three points. firstly, i consider job creation to be the most important priority, and therefore every proposal and every decision must be evaluated against this priority and, of course, the related impact on small businesses, because we know that the biggest employers are small businesses. secondly, the european commission has identified 10 priority areas and we should know the impact of individual legislative acts on these areas when making decisions, and it is important that these criteria are not contradictory and counterproductive, but that they are interrelated and complementary. last but not least, thirdly, i would like to stress the irreplaceable role of the european parliament as the only elected institution at european level and i personally look forward to working with the european commission on the impact assessment process.
if we are to start employment, we need to invest in projects with high added value. in many member states, more than 80% of public investment exists only because it is financed by the european budget. the europe 2020 strategy is to help us create new, quality jobs, improve quality of life, reduce poverty and social exclusion. the paradox is that on the one hand we know where the road to achieving our objectives leads, on the other hand the council has a problem financing what it has approved. she's not even able to give us a payment schedule for invoices for projects that have already been completed. this problem has been growing like a snowball for five years. if the council is sincere, if it wants jobs and smart, sustainable and inclusive growth, it must respect the rules on economic governance and budgetary discipline and deliver on its commitments.
in writing. the statistics we have are alarming: various indicators show that there are almost 25 million unemployed in the eu, with an unemployment rate of 10%. focus on poverty reduction and job creation is needed. it is also important to bridge the gap between member states, especially in regions far from the european average and standard. it is therefore in our interest to promote the single market, sustainable and inclusive growth and to focus on job creation. although youth unemployment is a serious problem for the eu, i am also convinced that we must not forget the older members of our society. demographic change and population ageing will directly affect the eu's social and economic model. so they can't surprise us.
in the case of a contract for the sale of goods, the contract is concluded between the debtor and the creditor, and the debtor is entitled to the payment of the amount of the debt. the european union has a significant position in the manufacture of railway rolling stock through a number of companies operating in this field. it is therefore important to provide strong international guarantees to creditors to increase investment in rail transport and, in the final phase, to create a leasing market for mobile rail equipment in europe.
in writing - the european union signed the convention in 2009. this convention concerns exclusive jurisdiction agreements concluded in civil and commercial matters. the european union should accede to this convention, which the united states of america is likely to ratify in the near future. if the european union does not accede to the convention, ratification by other countries may be delayed. consumer insurance contracts should be excluded from the scope of the convention.
in writing, the european commission received the request to mobilise the egf in may this year after 577 jobs were lost as a result of changes in the global trade pattern, namely in the global maritime industry, where the european union's share in the global context of new shipbuilding decreased. the closure of the undertaking in question will increase unemployment in the region and the high age and low level of education of the redundant workers will make it impossible for them to find adequate employment. the financial envelope should be eur 1 426 800.
in writing - this request concerns the release of funds of eur 918 000 for staff who worked in slaughterhouses in france which had to reduce production and capacity as a result of the economic and financial crisis. since 2007, per capita consumption of pork has decreased by up to 4 kilograms. gad is one such company, having recorded losses of eur 65 million over the last four years. the european globalisation adjustment fund should be used to cover personalised services for redundant staff.
in writing. - it is very important that the fundamental rights of every citizen of the european union are protected. the right to data protection is also a fundamental right and so, as with previous pnr proposals, the agreement between canada and the european union on the transfer and processing of passenger name record data places the primary responsibility on the european parliament and each member to represent the interests of the citizens of the member states.
in writing - in 2000, the millennium development goals were adopted, with which world leaders sought to contribute to the reduction of poverty in developing countries by 2015 through specific targets. however, we now see that society is still very far from achieving the set objectives and that the international community needs to focus on defining a new framework in this specific area. it is essential that society learns from the past and develops a new global framework for developing countries' development that is politically acceptable but also decisive in the fight against poverty.
in writing. in 2000, the united nations adopted the millennium declaration, setting out the millennium development goals to be achieved by 2015. since the adoption of the sdgs, stakeholders have made significant efforts to reduce poverty, hunger and violations of fundamental human rights in particular, but this represents only a step in the right direction, which needs to be further deepened, in view of the scale of the situation of extreme poverty and humanitarian problems in the world. negotiations on a new framework after 2015 should therefore result in a renewed global partnership to ensure that the remaining global challenges are effectively addressed. like many other colleagues in the european parliament, i support an ambitious approach that would prioritise the protection of human rights, the eradication of poverty and sustainable development. on the contrary, i am opposed to the promotion of abortion as a sexual or reproductive right or a method of modern family planning in development policy, because the european union has a duty to protect the right to life and human dignity, and this duty under the eu charter of fundamental rights must be respected in all eu policy areas.
in writing. — i have abstained for the vote on the stier report as in general it calls for good issues (protecting of people and minorities), but it has some bad points in it as well, as for example point 28 which asks for ‘the promotion of universal access to sexual and reproductive health and rights’. i do not agree with this and therefore abstained.
in writing. as global problems such as poverty, hunger, lack of access to quality healthcare, poor education, high unemployment, especially among young people, gender inequality, environmental degradation persist, it is essential to find new solutions for development. i advocate that the european union actively engages in addressing these problems and encourages the involvement of other countries in activities aimed at ensuring sustainable development on earth.
in writing. - to achieve the europe 2020 goals, we need a more ambitious approach, especially in the area of unemployment and poverty. i agree with the european commission's proposal to review the strategy. economic growth alone does not guarantee better jobs, reducing poverty or reducing social inequalities, and these goals require appropriate policy choices. in addition to macroeconomic considerations, employment and social considerations should be taken into account to the same extent. administrative and bureaucratic burdens still make it difficult for self-employed persons, micro-enterprises and smes to make a living, and burdens need to be reduced. start-ups also want better conditions. the europe 2020 strategy is a strategy for the european union, which aims to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy by: this can help to increase the flexibility of the labour market and create conditions that support the capacity of enterprises to create jobs. however, it is regrettable that the mff adopted for the period 2014-2020 is the first eu budget with a net budget deficit of eur 960 billion. it may be useful for the purposes of the review of the strategy to consult together the parliament and the commission on the proposals made and the subsequent decision. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution.
in writing - it is very important that member states start to translate the european union's objectives into their own national policies in a more ambitious and concrete way, especially in the areas of unemployment and poverty, as well as in the field of education. the european union must become competitive and this can only be achieved by improving education systems, which will lead to the creation of new highly qualified workers who will meet the demands of the modern labour market. the multiannual financial framework that has been adopted is unlikely to be sufficient to meet the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy, especially in the area of employment and social programmes and objectives.
in writing i supported the european parliament's motion for a resolution on the employment and social aspects of the 2020 strategy, as i believe it is essential that employment in the eu is already on the upward curve. the resolution clearly identifies all the causes of high unemployment, especially among young people. the biggest cause is the mismatch of the education system with the working environment and the requirements of the labour market. the member states must therefore restructure their training and study programmes very quickly and support, also through the social fund, those sectors in which young people are able to find a real job after leaving the labour market. action needs to be taken very quickly and effectively, as we are already in the fourth year of the implementation of the 2020 strategy and an additional 16 million citizens need to be employed to meet the measurable target of 75% employment. the work plan of the new commission under mr juncker provides the basis for the employment target of the 2020 strategy, but the commission will need to make a stronger appeal to member states to also integrate this strategic objective into their national programmes.
in writing. social aspects are becoming increasingly important in the european union. the main priority of the europe 2020 strategy should be to ensure inclusive growth with high levels of employment and to reduce the number of people living in poverty or at risk of social exclusion. it is also essential to support the young generation at the beginning of their professional careers and therefore to combat youth unemployment, as the youth unemployment rate is up to twice as high as that of adults (22%, compared to 10,2%).
we have a clear vision of a budget that will support growth and jobs in the european union. that's not enough for a good result when the council can't even tell us when it's going to pay off the old debts. if the path to our common goals were to look like a budget conciliation procedure, the common project called the european union would be seriously jeopardised. that is why, commissioner, we must base the european union's budgets on different principles and independence from the economic and political situation in the member states. what we can do together more effectively needs to be transferred from the national to the european level. for example, the common security and defence policy alone could save europe €26 billion a year, the amount the council owes on unpaid invoices. the question is: what do we do to agree on the 2015 budget? we will have to ask the heads of european governments to step in with all seriousness. the european union must be a trusted partner even in times of trouble.
for the ecr group. we know the rough figures on how many women are victims of violence. we know from the anonymous forms and the inquiries how big a problem this is. but let me draw your attention today to another number, the anonymous one. only 14% of domestic violence victims actually have the courage to turn to the authorities whose job it is to protect us. what does it say about trust in the police that while sweden has 47 rapes per 100,000 inhabitants, greece, hungary, or slovakia, for example, have only two or three rapes per 100,000? no, it's not the fundamental differences between people, the difference is in trust. trusting that if i go to the police, i won't have another painful experience, but i will encounter a sensitive investigative approach that is tailored to the needs of the victims, that i won't be recording the perpetrator, that i will find safety and no reason to doubt whether the step of reporting the crime was really the right one, that the medical staff will recognize my problem and immediately advise me on the options to protect myself, that the investigators and judges will respond quickly, not condone the suffering, but punish the crime and protect the victim. the issue of violence against women is not just a problem for women and their families, but for all of us. each of us can help by not being indifferent or judgmental. so please, let's not ask if the victims had clothes, what they were wearing, were they where we judge they shouldn't have been, but let's always ask first and foremost how we can help them.
in writing. even in the 21st century, we still face the mental and physical violence that all women face around the world. violence against women is the most brutal form of gender inequality and violates fundamental human rights. one in five women in the eu has been exposed to psychological violence and one in ten has also been exposed to sexual violence. the commission is working to adopt legislation to prevent violence against women. the protection of women is needed throughout the eu, and we must therefore ensure that detention and protection orders issued in one member state are recognised and automatically recognised in all member states. this can be achieved by ensuring that women at risk of domestic violence can move freely throughout the eu. however, it should also be borne in mind that women who are subjected to physical violence rarely report the attack on their person to the competent authorities. in the future, therefore, complaints should be collected and recorded more thoroughly and a database of abusers should be created which could be compared across member states. violence against women is a very serious social problem that must not be ignored. i therefore warmly welcome the activities of the european commission through the daphne iii and progress programmes, which will continue in the future through the rights, equality and citizenship programme.
for the ecr group. for more than 10 years, protection against discrimination has been one of the areas where european law has sought to positively impact people's daily lives in the european union, yet today too, many children, women and men face discrimination on a daily basis. as i have been involved in helping people with disabilities or people from socially disadvantaged backgrounds for a long time, as well as in education, i see unequal access to education as one of the main areas of discrimination in slovakia. although our legislation speaks of integration, in practice it hardly happens at all. similarly in employment, many employers would rather pay penalty payments than hire a disabled person. according to a recent survey by the institute for financial policy of the ministry of finance of the slovak republic, if a job applicant has a name that suggests that he belongs to the roma national minority, he has a significantly lower chance of being invited to an interview at all. but our society is paying and will pay for this segregation, not only in the enormous economic loss of these people's untapped potential, but also in the consequences of denying self-realization and human dignity to this broad group of people. i therefore call on the member states not only to create a legislative framework to help discriminated groups of people, but also to create the conditions for its effective application, so that individual governments ensure that if someone is denied such a right, there is someone to turn to for effective help and redress.
written. i support the plan put forward by the european commission, which is a good stimulus to kick-start the growth needed. europe must become more attractive to investors and this investment plan will help to improve our competitiveness. the return on private investment is always higher and it is therefore good that this project has the ambition to mobilise mainly private investment.
in writing. - what ? the main objective of this regulation is to take account of the new supervisory powers of the european central bank under regulation (eu) no 1024/2013. this particular regulation gave the european central bank new powers in the field of the supervision of credit institutions. the final version of the proposal incorporates some important changes to ensure the protection of private and confidential data that the european central bank will use in its new supervisory tasks.
in writing. - i support the proposal because it contributes to a more efficient collection of statistical information by the european central bank. several provisions of the proposal also limit the possibilities of falsifying statistics or providing misleading statistical information. this was one of the reasons for the current debt crisis, as we saw in the case of greece.
in writing. - the financial and economic crisis that hit the european union and its people hard has highlighted the lack of regulation and supervision in the financial sector. these new powers of the european central bank are the result of the extension of its role in the single supervisory mechanism. the main changes concern in particular the definition of penalties, the general principles of applicability, the sanctions for failure to comply with obligations and the procedural rules relating to the initiation of infringement proceedings. a more tightly managed and integrated economic and monetary union is a prerequisite for the euro to remain the single currency for the countries in the monetary union.
in writing. - what ? i abstained because some parts of the bill are favorable, but others i disagree with. in particular, provisions that make the european central bank's work more efficient are beneficial. however, several other provisions increase the ecb's lack of transparency and even push it to the systemic level. in particular, the provision provides that the ecb may postpone the publication of a decision for up to three years even if the ecb considers that immediate publication of the decision would jeopardise the stability of financial markets. this provision will allow the ecb to refuse to publish virtually any decision at its discretion, provided that it 'considers' that its publication will have a negative impact on the stability of financial markets.
climate change is threatening the entire planet. its effects are slowing economic growth, making it harder to eradicate poverty and weakening food security. i supported the resolution because the urgency of the need to act to address the global climate crisis is very acute. daily, the media report on various natural disasters that affect mankind on all continents. every year, at various international conferences and here in the european parliament, we adopt resolutions and challenges, but over time we again find that there is little or no progress in this area. i therefore expect the new commission to take a more active stance on tackling the global climate crisis and to make clear that climate change is one of the most important strategic priorities. the european union should be a leader in international agreements and a unifier of the positions of the various international institutions on the adoption of concrete global action to combat climate change.
in writing. the cop 20 conference of the parties to the un framework convention on climate change will be held in lima, peru, from 1 to 12 december. it is of the utmost importance to the european parliament that the conference in question makes substantial progress in defining the main points of the forthcoming climate agreement, which should be concluded in 2015. the main objective is to increase global climate ambition over the next five years, in particular by increasing funding to reach the target of usd 100 billion per year by 2020. the planned lima conference will be key to the establishment of an ambitious and legally binding international agreement to be signed in paris next year, setting out climate action beyond 2020.
in writing. - i did not support the motion because i am convinced it will lead to exactly the opposite of what is intended. already, current climate targets and other eu environmental policies are driving many companies out of europe, to countries such as china or india, where environmental standards are much lower. as a result, the eu economy and employment suffer, but overall emissions may rise. in such a situation, efforts to further enforce climate goals are irresponsible, as they will further reinforce the aforementioned trend.
mr president, palestinian people deserve a sovereign state just as israel has the right to live in peace. as a friend of israel i support the recognition of palestinian statehood. the move is not meant to undermine israel’s position; on the contrary, its purpose is to rescue the two-state solution, which is the only alternative for israel’s security. today, after the failure of kerry’s initiative, after violence in jerusalem, it is time for the eu to assume more responsibility. recognition of palestine is a powerful diplomatic tool; we should use it wisely to help restart peace negotiations. recognition of the palestinians in the pre-1967 borders will strengthen all those israelis and palestinians who believe in the two-state solution. mr netanyahu may not like to hear it, but i am convinced that recognising palestine could be one of the eu’s greatest contributions to israeli security.
we are 25 years into the adoption of the convention on the rights of the child, ushering in a new era of seeing the child as a human rights holder. much has been done over the years to implement children's rights. however, there is a large group of children who are discriminated against because of their age and whose rights are ignored. they're babies conceived before they're born. in slovakia, four non-governmental organizations issued the declaration of the rights of the unborn child 15 years ago to draw attention to the need to enforce the rights of unborn children. under the laws in force in several european countries, children conceived have the right to inherit, to be gifted, to be given a decent burial. yet, they are legally denied the right to be born. it is therefore necessary to emphasize that every unborn child has the inalienable and inalienable right to be born, as the pope emphasized here yesterday. he has the right to life, to be protected by law from conception to natural death. i believe it is in the best interest of the child to be conceived and born in the marriage of one man and one woman, and has the right to be conceived and carried in the womb of his mother.
last week we commemorated the 25th anniversary of the adoption of the convention on the rights of the child. on this occasion, the european commission hosted a 17-year-old slovak, dominik drdúl, who was born with a disability. in his speech, he pointed out, among other things, that the number of children in institutional care is increasing and that i quote 'it is a terrible place for children and i am very angry that this is still happening in the european union, as it is in breach of the convention on the rights of the child.' it is shocking and unacceptable to me that there are still places where children with disabilities live in inhuman and degrading conditions, such as the children's home in the greek city of lechaina. according to media reports, children with mental disabilities are drugged and literally locked in cages in this institution, their personal movement is limited to a minimum. we cannot accept this kind of treatment. i therefore call not only on greece but also on other member states where similar practices occur to ensure that these children have decent living conditions in community facilities in accordance with the convention on the rights of the child and the convention on the rights of persons with disabilities.
according to article 3 of the convention on the rights of the child, states are to respect the rights and obligations of parents in ensuring the protection of the child. according to article 14, states are to respect the rights and obligations of parents in guiding a child in the exercise of his or her right to freedom of thought, conscience and religion. according to article 18, parents have the primary right to educate their child. under article 29, they are to ensure that the education of the child also develops respect for his or her parents, cultural identity and values. article 35 requires them to prevent trafficking in children by all means. today, in europe, these provisions are routinely ignored. many member states do not respect the rights and obligations of parents in relation to their own children. this is especially evident in situations where parents disagree with aggressive sex education, and states do not hesitate to use violence to violate one of the rights of the child guaranteed by this convention. and legalizing surrogacy is just turning a blind eye to child trafficking. but we find none of that in this resolution. family is mentioned only twice. once in relation to family allowances and then again in point 23, which deals with family reunification. instead of condemning the gross violation of children's rights in the european union, the proposal seeks to problematize their gender and sexual identity. the convention defines a child as a person between the ages of 0 and 18 years, and sexual identity is not defined in international law at all. the resolution seeks to protect children in different situations and at different ages, but not one sentence protects the right of a child to be born. for these reasons too, i do not consider that the resolution as it stands is a worthy reminder of the adoption of the convention on the rights of the child.
the 25th anniversary of the adoption of the un convention on the rights of the child should also be an opportunity to highlight the fact that many children are still discriminated against, suffer from hunger and poverty, lack access to education and health care, and are homeless. children are the most vulnerable in every state, in every institution, and that's why protecting them is so important. we know the problems of children in underdeveloped countries and in countries where there is violence and war, but even developed europe has not yet dealt with the problems of child sex discrimination, sexual exploitation, child labour, poverty and lack of access to education. modern technology, such as the internet, aggressive advertising, and cyberbullying, also contributes to the threat posed not only to children's education but to their own education. that is why it is important to talk about children's issues, not only at the anniversaries of the un convention, but also in the adoption of various policies in the context of education, health care, poverty, support for families, foster families, and homeless children. i support the resolution and hope that at the next anniversary we will be able to talk about improving the implementation of the un convention on the rights of the child.
commissioner, at the g20 summit you again stated that europe lacks growth. this is not surprising at all, because europe is doing everything possible to ensure that there is no growth. it raises taxes, it degrades the business environment, the european union is constantly inventing new regulations and setting unrealistic climate targets. and then everyone is just surprised that there is no growth, that companies are leaving europe. the only solution that the european union can come up with is more government spending, more of what caused the debt crisis, more spending, more debt. if the european union is to really tackle growth and really reduce unemployment, it has to bankrupt banks and fail-safe states, it has to reduce regulation, it has to stop overprotecting the environment, it has to stop over-targeting climate change, and it has to systematically improve the business environment.
written completion of the digital market is key to stimulating growth and jobs in europe. however, this cannot be done without the creation of an eu data protection framework and a cybersecurity directive. the current digital landscape is highly fragmented, with 28 national markets and different legislation in each. too many barriers block the free movement of goods and online services across national borders. current research suggests that a deeper and more complete digital market could boost the gdp of the eu-28 by at least €340 billion a year and create around 2.5 million jobs in total. a competitive digital single market cannot exist without fast, high-capacity broadband and telecommunications networks in all eu regions, but neither can it exist without the protection of personal data, privacy and security of electronic communications. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of electronic communications networks and services. and we can build it.
for the ppe group. first of all, i would like to thank the chair of the committee on development policy, mrs linde mcavan, for taking on the role of rapporteur for a document which the committee had actually approved in the last parliamentary term. i also thank the colleagues responsible for this subject in the various political groups for their cooperation in the submission of amendments. it has already been mentioned that this week we voted on the post-2015 document with the millennium development goals. malnutrition is a truly complex problem that weighs on society as a whole. it is a burden because malnutrition does not respect the basic human right, namely the right to a life of dignity for the very youngest, for those children who are hungry. when you consider that every minute a few children die, not because their countries are at war and not because they are sick, but because they are hungry. and it's not just these children who are dying, it's other children, thousands and thousands of children who are suffering from this malnutrition for their entire lives. we're talking about numbers here -- i think we know those numbers pretty much by heart, but it's important to collaborate, it's important to collaborate with the institutions of the european union, but also with other organizations that deal with food, especially with fao. there's supposed to be a meeting this week, and i think that cooperation should be closer. and just one sentence: i believe what the commissioner said at the hearing, that we will look more at poverty, we will look more at hunger than we will look at climate change.
nearly one billion people in the world suffer from hunger, and 2.6 million children in developing countries die each year from malnutrition. in mali, it is estimated that by the end of 2014, some 500,000 children will suffer from acute malnutrition in that country. the causes of child malnutrition are varied, and the overwhelming majority are human-caused. that means they can be avoided. often, conflict and political instability are the main causes of child malnutrition. we therefore need more methods, statistics and monitoring of the impact of different types of conflict on children and their nutrition. governments in developing countries must create an environment for better child nutrition and care. this is why national plans and strategies need to be better coordinated. for our part, the commission and the member states should mobilise long-term financial resources and investments in the field of nutrition and cooperate effectively with international organisations. financial assistance, investments and financial resources are of paramount importance in combating the threat of acute malnutrition in developing countries. however, in many cases - mali or syria being examples - stopping the conflict, preventing violence and creating political stability through diplomatic and political negotiations is certainly as essential as foreign financial assistance.
ladies and gentlemen, try to imagine that your sister is on death row in pakistan and you can't help her. or there's a citizen of our country locked up, what do we do? asia bibi, for example, is a victim of religious intolerance, and we europeans, as human rights defenders, have an obligation to help her. she's a christian woman who tried to quench the thirst of muslim women, and they turned her in under blasphemy laws and she went to jail. she's in solitary confinement, often beaten, hungry and thirsty. our society is built on helping those in need. saving a mother of five who is being punished for helping a neighbor just because she is a christian is worth the extreme effort to save her. people in eastern europe know this, we have experienced persecution for our faith by communist regimes and christians have suffered. after 25 years of the regime, i must say that in many countries freedom of religion is a luxury. many countries like pakistan, iraq and syria do not allow the freedom to believe in god, that's not right. spreading fear through religion is not worthy of these times. mercy is not rewarded with death. in the civilized world, help is rewarded. irrational blasphemy laws create an opportunity to persecute, persecute, criminalize people of other faiths.

in writing. – today i voted for the juncker-led commission, because political stability should be maintained. however i do not agree with mr juncker’s view expressed during the monday plenary debate that we could avoid the reoccurrence of the current situation only if we introduce tax harmonisation in the eu. i am of the view that tax harmonisation would not solve the problem. what is more, it would only worsen the overall situation. in my opinion, the key is not to provide exceptions and to prevent the possibility of trickery, which is not by any means equal to tax harmonisation. introducing eu tax harmonisation would lead to the end of internal competition, which would encumber the situation of those member states behind the eu average. it would significantly reduce their ability to contribute to their own economic development and in the end it would create new burdens for the eu. if member states are not able to self-develop because their advantages from lower tax rates are eliminated by tax harmonisation, sooner or later it will be the eu who will finance their development through its funds. we should also bear in mind that in those member states that apply lower tax rates wages and the standard of living are often also lower.
