in writing. twenty-five years have passed since the adoption of the un convention on the rights of the child and some progress has been made, but children's fundamental rights continue to be violated not only in the world but also right here in the european union. i would stress that the un convention on the rights of the child and its protocols represent only the basic standard for the protection of children's rights and the quality of their lives. however, a modern 21st century society should commit to the deepening of the fundamental rights of the child in accordance with the principles of the best interests of the child and non-discrimination, as well as the right of the child to life, survival and development. i am highlighting above all an essential right, namely the right to life, on which all other rights of the child depend. unfortunately, the right to life is not explicitly guaranteed or protected in the prenatal stage, allowing for practices that destroy human life before birth and even allocating public funds to do so. i consider such practices, like the two million eu citizens who officially supported the 'one of us' citizens' initiative, to be unacceptable and contrary to the rights of the child. i therefore reiterate my call on the european union and the member states to adopt coherent legislation and policies to protect the rights of children and not to overlook the need to protect their life and integrity in the prenatal stage.

i believe that we all share a responsibility to protect nature around the world, which is why we support this convention, which the european union as a whole should join. the common european market is one of our greatest assets, and it is therefore essential that we act together in this area as well, because this convention on international trade concerns the common european market and our common european approach. as is well known, the history of the convention dates back to 1 july 1975 and covers more than 35 000 species of endangered animals and plants. let me emphasize that among slovak species, these are such rare species as the golden eagle, the falcon, and the river otter. the accession of the european union to cites provides for greater transparency of the union's legal position within cites vis-à-vis third parties to the convention. the current observer status has rather been a disadvantage and has not allowed the union to fully implement the objectives of environmental policy. the union will contribute each year 2,5% of the total cites trust fund, which is approximately eur 115 000 for the next year, with a corresponding reduction in member states' contributions. this is further evidence of the advantage of a common european approach. accession will enable the commission to take a unified position on behalf of the union and thus improve the eu's position in the control of international trade. i believe that our agreement of the european parliament to the accession of the union to this convention on international trade will not only contribute to a more effective protection of endangered species of wild fauna and flora, but will also speed up the decision-making process and, in particular, harmonise the necessary common european procedure.
international trade in wildlife severely limits the survival and development of animal species and plant specimens worldwide. after all, many of you have already mentioned it. cites thus ensures the protection of over 35 000 species and makes a significant contribution to controlling illegal international trade in this area. at present, the member states are parties to the convention, but we believe that the time has come for a change in status and for the european union, which is currently only an observer, to be given new powers. accession to cites will not affect the rights and obligations of the member states, as the european union will only vote on those issues that relate to the acquis and individual member states will continue to vote on all other matters. however, the accession of the european union to cites will ensure greater transparency of the legal environment and the position of the european union vis-à-vis third parties to the convention.
i very much welcome the european union's accession to cites, because i expect a united stance in protecting endangered species and biodiversity throughout the european union, which is our moral responsibility. we often think of them as exotic species, but many of our rare domestic protected species are preyed upon by local and international gangs, often in very sophisticated ways, and under the guise of being legally bred. within the european union, largely without border controls, it is simply impossible to maintain order without a concerted effort. there must be a unified and interconnected system of registration, tracking of movements of individuals coming from the trade and from breeding stations, and interconnected supervision of breeders' activities in order to prevent fraud. and what the eu will be able to do much more effectively than the member states in the future is to monitor trade via the internet, because this is the biggest threat to endangered species.

250 million europeans use the internet every day, but there are still millions of european citizens who have never used the internet. i therefore see the creation of a digital single market as an opportunity that can make a significant contribution not only to improving employment but also to improving the skills of europeans. the creation of a digital market requires focusing on: 1. better access to the internet; 2. the expansion of electronic commerce; 3. the accessibility of electronic services. the development of high-speed networks today has the same revolutionary impact as the development of electricity and transport networks a century ago. the union should also ensure two objectives under the digital agenda: broadband coverage of 30 mbit/s by 2020 and provision of 50% of households with broadband connections above 100 mbit/s by 2020. the digital economy is growing at up to 12% per year and is growing seven times faster than other sectors of the economy. for these reasons, i consider the creation of a digital single market to be a key priority in our common european market.
thank you for the word. i regret to report that in my country, slovakia, in the last two years, there has been a radical and unjustified reduction in state support for minority cultures. not only is the amount uncertain from year to year, but more recently the government has been very arbitrary in its decision on how to allocate the money, often ignoring successful projects and ignoring the proportions among individual minorities. in many european countries, the way and amount of support for minority cultures is guaranteed by law. for example, it is defined as a fixed percentage of the current state budget or of the gross national product. the distribution of the funds is left to the parties themselves. this could be the solution within the whole european union, because it would greatly improve the relationship between the majority and the minority, and minority ngos and cultural institutions would not be subject to financial blackmail and could not be easily used as a political tool. i think it is time for an eu recommendation to be made to the member states to deal with this issue in this way. thank you for listening.

in writing according to the information available and current trends, 2-pack and 6-pack appear to be marketing names for a non-existent strategy. how else can one view inconsistent compliance with the rules, as is the case in france today? if the european commission wants citizens of the european union's member states to have confidence in the european union's economic governance project, then it must first demand that the rules it has set be respected, and impose tough sanctions on any state that breaks them, regardless of its political influence or geographical importance. in addition, the european commission should also focus on considering the rules for member states' budgets, which often make pro-growth measures and thus real growth of economies or improvement of the social situation of the population impossible. last but not least, it is essential to introduce real measures against tax evasion and restrictions on tax havens, both within and outside the european union. such measures could provide the necessary funds for the budgets of the member states and at the same time be a moral satisfaction for the inhabitants.

‒ international trade in wildlife is a major threat to the survival and development of animal and plant species worldwide. cites provides protection for over 35 000 species by conservation status, making a significant contribution to the control of illegal international trade in this area. the original cites convention allowed only states to become members, but it is time for the status of the european union, which currently only acts as an observer, to change, especially since accession to cites will not affect the rights and obligations of the member states, as the european union will only vote on those issues that relate to the acquis and individual member states will continue to vote on all other matters.
‒ the convention on international trade in endangered species of wild fauna and flora is a major international instrument for the protection of 35 000 species of endangered species of fauna and flora. since the entry into force of the convention, all eu member states have become parties to it, but the european union has only had observer status. i voted in favour of the eu's accession to the convention because i consider it to be the logical outcome of the eu's efforts in the field of environmental protection, which is within its competence. accession will also make it possible to adopt a common position on matters arising from the convention and to promote a common position in negotiations during the conferences of the parties.

in writing, the agreement on the european economic area brings together the member states of the european union and the three efta members iceland, liechtenstein and norway in a single market. the eea agreement provides for the inclusion of european union law covering all four fundamental freedoms the free movement of persons, services, goods and capital across all eea members. in addition, the agreement in question also covers cooperation in important areas such as research and development, education, social policy, the environment, consumer protection and, last but not least, culture. the agreement guarantees equal rights and obligations without discrimination for all economic operators and citizens within the internal market. it is important that an agreement on the participation of croatia in the eea and a protocol thereto be concluded and that account be taken of croatia's accession to the european union.

in writing ‒ the main objective is to allow france to apply the exemption or reduction of the dock dues to certain products produced locally in the outermost regions. the purpose of these tax differentials is to offset the disadvantages for the outermost regions, which have to cope with the increased production costs and the associated high price of these products. if the measures in question are not taken, these products will be less competitive than those produced elsewhere. the proposal sets out a clear and precise legal framework for maritime taxation from 1 january 2015 to december 2020.

in writing tax evasion and tax fraud cost the eu member states €1 trillion a year. this is 2000 euros for every eu citizen. these leakages contribute to sovereign debt, rising unemployment and increasing the risk of poverty. half the world's financial transactions flowed through tax havens before the crisis. this money is sorely lacking in solving the crisis and jobs. i therefore welcome the commission's commitment to present a proposal within six months for measures to combat tax evasion and tax fraud. i expect commission president juncker to deliver on his commitment made in july to prepare measures to tax profits in the country where they are generated.
in writing. creating a single plan for recovery, investment and growth for a common europe is not easy. the commission's work programme responds to the most pressing problems, such as unemployment, poverty, the fight against fraud, cutting red tape, cutting spending and the problem of migrants. it is not possible to do everything at once, but i believe that better cooperation between the individual commissioners will make the commission's work more efficient and that the promises made when it was elected will be gradually fulfilled. the work programme shall be targeted and shall focus on specific objectives. achieving them will also depend on better coordination of member states' policies. the heads of government cannot say one thing in their countries and do something else in a joint council meeting. only a common policy, a common action, has a chance of success. it is time to stop dividing europe into a richer north and a poorer south, a more developed west and a less developed east. a common europe is also about solidarity and reducing regional disparities, and the commission's work plan for 2015 can eliminate this.

the agreement on the future budget provides a realistic basis for the three main objectives of paying off outstanding debts, supporting member states' structural reforms and, above all, promoting job creation. it is important that the union invests more in competitiveness, growth and employment and that the budget structure is therefore also aligned with these objectives. the budget should always focus on the future - innovation, education, smes and, above all, young people. it is also important to direct the budget to union research and development programmes and horizon 2020. i therefore welcome the new agreement to increase the contribution to these programmes by eur 45 million. the positive aspect of this budget is also the inclusion of the first tranche of 8 billion to launch a new 315 billion investment plan. i believe that through private investment and credit guarantees, the union will gain access to new resources and ensure greater growth for our economy. still, we need to find a way to pay off the outstanding bills, which already exceed 23 billion. it is therefore important that the commission monitors the level of unpaid invoices and prepares a mitigating measure such as penalties. the new budget should therefore also bring improvements in the level of payments.
in writing. letter of amendment no 1 of the college of commissioners to the 2015 budget proposal, which proposes to reduce direct payments under the agricultural chapter of the eu budget for 2015, is unacceptable to europeans in their right mind. the measures taken against european farmers as a result of the russian embargo would be financed by the eu budget from the crisis fund. the application of the crisis fund, however, may be effected by deducting direct payments placed in the reserve under the legislation in force. this was objected to by the agriculture ministers of 21 member states at the council meeting. i estimate that this would result in a reduction of around eur 15 million per year for hungarian and slovak farmers. the commitment of the crisis fund before the beginning of the year also deprives the eu of any budgetary room for manoeuvre to respond to any crisis situation that may arise during the year and directly affect the agricultural sector. we must not forget that the russian import ban was not caused by agriculture, and i therefore find it particularly unfair that the measures introduced should be paid for by farmers.

since 2011, millions of civilians - innocent men, women, but also children and the elderly - have suffered as a result of the military conflict in syria. millions of syrians have been forced to leave their homes, with up to a third of them forced to flee across the border into neighboring countries. these people have lost their homes, their jobs, their regular income, and thus their access to the very essentials of survival - food and warm clothing. these people have lost their dignity. the world food programme, to which the european union contributes, plays a key role in helping the victims of this terrible war. however, at the beginning of december, part of this aid had to be suspended due to lack of funds, leaving a large proportion of syrian refugees in very difficult living conditions, further complicated by the harsh winter. the necessary funds for december and part of january were finally collected. but we must not allow these defenceless people to experience a sense of threat and, above all, insecurity again in the future. in such difficult situations, it is important not to give up.

in writing on the one hand, there is the criticism that the european union is not able to pay its commitments under the multiannual financial framework for the years 2014 to 2020. on the other hand, it is also essential that the rules are set correctly in relation to the member states' contributions to the budget of the european union. although the conciliation committee did not reach an agreement and although the high amounts of vat and gni own resources adjustments in 2014 may represent a high financial burden for individual member states, it is important that we are able to set up and operate this system effectively in the future.
in the past, the european parliament has always strongly defended the provisions of the new solidarity fund regulation in the relevant negotiations with the council, recognising the need for member states to receive financial support in order to respond to natural disasters more quickly than would be possible under the normal procedure. i therefore support the inclusion in the budget of up to eur 50 million for this purpose, which will allow the member state to receive an advance of up to 10% of the estimated financial contribution. the european union solidarity fund contributes to sending a clear political signal of solidarity and provides real assistance to the affected citizens and regions.
the european union solidarity fund is an important instrument of the european union for the recovery of victims of major natural disasters. the financial assistance constitutes a concrete expression of solidarity of the union and its citizens with the affected regions, where the damage caused by the disaster exceeds eur 3 billion or 0,6% of the country's gross national income. in view of the unfortunate experience of previous years with delays in the payment of aid, i voted in favour of the adoption of a decision to mobilise the european union solidarity fund under the general budget of the european union for the coming financial year 2015, with a view to providing the possibility to make advances of eur 50 000 000 in commitment and payment appropriations. this measure will make it possible to pay out financial support to the disaster-stricken region more quickly and flexibly next year.
in writing i am against the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument because it leads to higher eu spending. the structural funds programmes already receive an excessive amount of money and, as the european court of auditors noted in its report, the main criterion for their use is the very minute amount of funding. in view of this fact, and in view of the chronic inefficiency of the structural funds, i consider that a further increase in the funds is unjustified.
in writing the challenging negotiations of the european parliament and the council of 8 december 2014, which preceded the draft package, created room for a very fragile compromise. the adoption of a joint statement on the payment plan is therefore inevitable, as there are legitimate concerns that the level of payment appropriations will not be sufficient, thus creating a recurring effect of an increase in outstanding accounts. however, in this context, i support the joint commitment of the european parliament, the council and the european commission, as set out in the joint statement on the payment plan, to reduce the level of the accounts in question. it is regrettable that the council has again been unwilling to supplement its political declarations with sufficient budgetary resources to promote employment and growth, and is concerned in this context only with horizontal spending cuts. it is essential to create resources for pro-growth policies of the european union which will lead to an improvement in the social situation of the population of the member states.
in writing this year's negotiations on the eu budget were difficult, given the council's reluctance to commit to replenishing funding for political priorities that it has announced in the past. addressing the shortfall by means of an agreement on a detailed payment plan can only be considered a temporary satisfactory solution in view of the overall circumstances and the urgent need to adopt the budget by the end of 2014. i personally see the priority in securing sufficient budgetary resources to cover the most urgent invoices for contractors working directly on eu-funded projects in the regions of the member states. the effective functioning and credibility of cohesion policy must not be jeopardised by delays or even non-payment of bills by the eu. the commission should also be able to provide information on the impact of the measures on the environment and the environment protection. in conclusion, i would like to express my satisfaction that sufficient funds have been allocated to the erasmus education programme and also to science, research and innovation.
in writing i did not support the eu budget for next year because i have fundamental concerns about its current form. the european union's budget for next year will increase, despite the fact that this year's budget already contains a number of expenditures that would be desirable to cut significantly or eliminate altogether. to increase the budget in such a situation and in a situation where the european union's economy is severely weakened is extremely irresponsible.
in writing. - classification of serious infringements in road transport i voted against the ep report on the draft commission regulation. i voted in favour of the new member states' view that the ep's formal position against the european commission's draft regulation would be detrimental to carriers in the new member states. the so-called 'old' member states invoke the 'social dumping' which is commonplace at the time, alleging that the 'new' member states enjoy an unfair advantage in terms of lower wage levels, thus making it impossible for carriers from the old member states to gain a foothold on the market. this is a classic example of the tension between the old and new member states, which has unfortunately always been won by the old member states, mainly because the new member states have not been able to act together. but today's close result suggests that these member states are beginning to wake up and find common ground. it is becoming clear that the interests of the old member states are not always the same as those of the new ones, and that these common interests, which are different from those of the others, can only be achieved by joint efforts and concerted action. i hope that during this election period we will also be able to set a positive example for our electorate and succeed in defending the interests of the new member states while protecting european integrity.
in writing the objective of regulation (ec) no 1071/2009 was to complete the internal market in road transport, characterised by fair conditions of competition. this requires the uniform application of the common rules for the authorisation of the occupation of road haulage or passenger transport operator. these common rules are intended to contribute to the attainment of a higher level of professional qualification of road transport operators, to the rationalisation of the market and to the improvement of the quality of services in the interests of road transport operators, their customers and road safety. however, the proposed regulation of the european commission is not compatible with the objective and content of the regulation of the european parliament and of the council of 21 october 2009.
in writing the motion for a resolution tabled called for the commission's proposal to be withdrawn. i voted against its adoption because if it were approved, the commission would have to develop a new alternative, which would not only block the whole process for a longer period, but would also probably introduce into european legislation such offences by carriers that would reduce the competitiveness of companies not only from slovakia but from all of central and eastern europe. for slovak carriers, such measures would mean an increase in costs and lead to redundancies, perhaps even to bankruptcy. in the long term, efforts are needed to ensure that slovakia can establish itself on the european single market, in particular through the high quality of its products and services. but until that happens, we must not let administrative hurdles take away the often only advantage - the lower cost of labour that comes from lower living costs.
in writing the revised fuel quality directive requires suppliers to submit a report on greenhouse gas emissions every year. by 31 december 2020, the greenhouse gas intensity reduction target is set at at least 6% in each member state. in its impact assessment, the european commission considered a range of options beyond the basic reporting approach based on average standard values of greenhouse gas emissions by fuel type. however, the breakdown of fuels from unconventional sources has not taken into account the latest developments in infrastructure and the oil market, and it is therefore important that the european commission further examine the method used to calculate the level of emissions and take into account the projected increase in the use of fuels with high greenhouse gas intensity and fuels derived from new raw materials.
the slovak government, through the permanent representation of the slovak republic to the eu, asked slovak meps to vote against the draft directive on the quality of petrol and diesel fuels. this proposal improves the conditions for diversification of fuel sources by facilitating the entry of fuels from non-conventional sources into the european market. it represents a compromise agreed by both the processors of russia's lower co2 oil and the states where higher co2 oil is being extracted or is being prepared to be extracted. nevertheless, the slovak government has pushed for the rejection of this compromise. this would strengthen the position of the so-called 'old' eu member states' refineries in the further negotiations. the result would be a new proposal that would further weaken the position of the only slovak fuel producer on the european market. that is why i voted against the environment committee's proposal and i am glad that it was rejected.
in writing article 290 tfeu provides that delegated acts are to supplement or amend certain elements or to lay down uniform conditions for the implementation of the provisions laid down in legislative acts. article 101 of directive 2009/138/ec provides that, in the case of insurance and reinsurance, all quantifiable risks to which insurance or reinsurance undertakings are exposed are to be taken into account and that the solvency capital requirement corresponds to the value at risk of the basic own funds of the insurance or reinsurance undertaking with a confidence level of 99,5% over a one-year horizon. the european commission has adopted a regulation involving the granting of powers which in several significant cases significantly deviate from the submitted technical recommendations, leading to significantly lower calibrations of risk factors.
the treaty of lisbon laid the foundations for the development of the european union's security policy, in which it participates jointly with the member states. it is based on the rule of law, respect for fundamental rights and solidarity and is subject to democratic oversight at european and national level. the security situation in europe has changed significantly in recent years, due to new conflicts and unrest in the european union's immediate neighbourhood, but also due to the rapid development of new technologies. the increasing radicalization is also leading to violence and terrorism. most of these aspects and security challenges are of a cross-border nature, exceeding the capacity of individual member states, and therefore require a common european approach. i therefore welcome the preparation of the internal security strategy for the next four years.
mr president, we voted on the recognition of the palestinian state. although i think that the two-state solution might be one of the solutions, i had to vote against this resolution for the following reasons: first of all, supporting this resolution is a violation of the oslo ii accord of 1995, article 16.7, stressing that both sides will do nothing to change the status of the palestinian authorities pending the outcome of the permanent status negotiations. even the eu signed this agreement as a witness, so it seems we can question this signature now. furthermore, the recognition of the palestinian state with its current government might seem to be a de facto recognition of the terrorist organisation hamas, which is officially in government at the moment even though today i received an intriguing press release stating that the eu general court is removing hamas from the list of terrorist organisations on procedural grounds. this is intriguing after 13 years. besides this, although i do recognise the rights of the palestinians, recognition today will be an extra barrier to the peace negotiations. the statement calls it ‘in principle recognition’, but everyone will see it as a full recognition. for both israel and palestine, this would be a step back.
in writing. - i abstained from the vote on the recognition of the state of palestine because i do not think the timing of the resolution is right. as regards the content of the resolution, i am in favour of the two-state model, as the union has repeatedly confirmed. the eu supports the 1967 borders solution, whereby a secure state of israel and an independent, democratic, territorial coherent and viable state of palestine coexist in peace and security, based on the right to self-determination and full respect for international law. i support the re-launch of direct peace negotiations between israelis and palestinians, which requires a climate of trust and serious steps on both sides. however, i am convinced that the ep's resolution is not timely, because it is not fair in view of the upcoming israeli elections. i think any resolution of this type is only fortunate to be adopted when neither side is under pressure to campaign, because it can negatively influence, distort the efforts and aspirations of the peace parties.
in writing the repeated attacks on the citizens of the state of israel by hamas and other militant groups and the continuing escalation of violence, as well as the fact that any palestinian government gains its legitimacy by having to be supported by hamas and fatah, do not constitute sufficient guarantees for the recognition of the existence of the state. there is no doubt that the region is in dire need of a solution and the european union has repeatedly recognised the palestinians' right to self-determination. but we would need much more time to make an objective assessment and a decision on the recognition of the new palestinian state.
in writing. my country recognized palestine in 1988. in this spirit, i welcome the european parliament's resolution. i think it is extremely important that we do not try to solve the situation in the middle east today by sweeping the problems under the carpet, by putting off embarrassing issues, but by showing a clear path that is acceptable to the peoples living there and by offering a helping hand to a peaceful settlement. all under the 1967 boundary treaty and international law. yes, we must recognise the state of palestine, but this recognition is conditional on its active and fruitful participation in the peace process. reconciliation is not a matter of a momentary decision but a process that both sides must work on, and in which the international community can provide a major contribution. enough of the bloodshed, the many victims. both sides have lost too much over the decades. i believe that an independent democratic state will help palestine to participate responsibly in the peace process.
the european union has no competence to recognise the existence of states. this competence is exclusively the responsibility of the eu member states. this has also been confirmed by the european commission. for this reason, i refused to take part in the vote on the motion for a resolution on the state of palestine. by adopting this resolution, the european parliament has made a political gesture that is at best unnecessary and at worst counterproductive. but it certainly does not help in finding a peaceful solution to the arab-israeli conflict.
written the steel industry is of vital importance to the european economy and the industry is currently suffering from a significant drop in demand, leading to continuous job losses and reduced competitiveness. the european union has a duty to promote industrial development policy in all member states and thus to protect jobs in the european union. therefore, the european union's objective in supporting the steel industry must be to remove obstacles and threats to its competitiveness and to respond flexibly to changing conditions on european and non-european markets. one of the main objectives must be to create safe and quality jobs. in order to achieve economic success, it is essential to involve employees in innovation and restructuring measures and to establish a platform with the participation of the social partners to provide advice on the steel action plan.
the steel industry is one of the traditional european sectors that has long contributed to europe's growth and prosperity. the steel industry currently directly employs some 350 000 workers and millions more in related industries. the steel industry faces many challenges in the current economic and geopolitical constellation. restructuring and possible mergers often appear to be necessary in order to maintain efficient operations, but they must be carried out in a socially responsible manner and taking into account the overall economic and social situation in the region concerned. i agree with the request that the commission monitor current developments in order to protect the industrial heritage and the relevant workforce and create the conditions for the recovery of the competitiveness of the european steel industry on world markets, which the eu has unfortunately been losing in recent years. strong global competition also needs to be taken into account when applying eu competition law.
in writing - i supported the resolution on the situation in the eu steel industry with an emphasis on the protection of workers and businesses. i'm watching the steel industry very closely. the steel giant uss in košice, where i live, has long been one of the largest employers in eastern slovakia. this company is still threatened with production cuts and layoffs. i therefore welcome the fact that we in the ep are looking at the situation of steel companies and at the possibilities for their competitiveness, stabilisation and development. in the future, we can expect significant changes in the steel industry through investments in research and innovation, resource efficiency, new technologies and expertise. therefore, member states need to better align education and training policies with labour market needs in order to provide specialised professionals in the steel industry. in addition, the commission should submit a report on the main social, economic and environmental problems of the eu steel industry as soon as possible, so that we can respond flexibly with initiatives to support the sector in the eu.
in writing according to the international organization for migration, more than 3,000 people died in the mediterranean sea during the first nine months of 2014. in addition, some reports say that some 500 migrants were killed after human traffickers attacked and sank a ship carrying migrants from egypt to the european union. the italian mare nostrum rescue and surveillance operation has rescued more than 150 000 migrants in 364 days, but the italian government is no longer able to finance the operation on its own and it is unclear today what the member states' contributions to the joint operation triton will be. it is therefore essential that the european union fairly shares responsibility and solidarity with the member states facing the highest number of refugees and asylum seekers.
in writing. the mediterranean should not become a cemetery as pope francis warned us in his address to the european parliament in november. we must therefore take a responsible approach to the issue of migration in europe, and that is why i welcome the resolution that has just been adopted. thousands of people are reluctant to take small risks, and then, relying on luck to save themselves and their families, they arrive at the long-awaited land of the union. we need to get to the root of the problem. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's human rights policy and will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's human rights policy. in the common migration policy, only human rights can be the guiding principle. it is to prevent criminal gangs from torturing, enslaving, and in many cases murdering people for profit. the eu needs a new, strong strategy, with all the member states on board, sharing the burden responsibly.
in writing - what is happening as a result of the conflict in syria, what happened near lampedusa, that in nine months in 2014 more than 3000 people died in the mediterranean, that around 500 migrants were murdered after apparently being attacked and deliberately sunk by human traffickers, i voted for the resolution. i consider the adoption of this resolution to be urgent and it is high time that we make every effort to save the lives of people in danger and to prevent further loss of life at sea. in addition to the tasks that await frontex, i expect measures to be taken to ensure a common european asylum system, and i also expect a review of the overall strategy of cooperation with third countries to tackle the root causes of migration. the situation in the mediterranean leaves no one indifferent, which is why i am pleased that tackling the issue of migrants is one of the commission's priorities for 2015.
mr president, the year 2014 should be seen as an unprecedented year in georgia’s advance towards the european union. signature of the association agreement has materialised, despite the volatile situation in the region and external pressures from russia. we should welcome above all, however, the strong consensus among georgian political parties and in the population of georgia on pursuing the european path. once such strong pro-european conviction has been demonstrated, it is our duty to support the country in its strategic orientation – even more so in the situation where the main external pressure comes from russia, which continues to occupy the georgian regions of abkhazia and south ossetia. georgia’s biggest neighbour might be countering the efforts for reforms in the country, and with the recent developments in abkhazia we need to reassure georgia, really convincingly, that we stand by it. the association agreement is the first important step and visa liberalisation should, and i hope will, be the next one. we should make sure that no obstacles stand in georgia’s way. yes, there is a substantial reform process ahead of georgia. it will be long, difficult and complicated. but it can definitely be facilitated through its commitment to european engagement, of course with our active support and participation.
ladies and gentlemen, the eu's approach to georgia is also strategically important for the stability of the region and a desirable partnership for us, which is why i support this association agreement. i would like to draw your attention to an important point which i believe has received little attention in the course of the negotiations. the situation of minorities in georgia may be a key issue for the future of the country. although there have been positive developments in the laws affecting minorities since georgia's independence, even today the relationship between the majority and the minority is characterised by mistrust and inconsistent minority policies. this may be decisive for the territorial unity of georgia, but the need for reconciliation is also shown from time to time. we need not look far in time or space at the dangers of underestimating minority issues. the case of ukraine clearly shows that it is in the interest of both parties to settle the coexistence in a way that is acceptable to both the majority and the minority.
, in writing.– jailing government's political opponents and kafka-like trials that we see in venezuela, shows that venezuelan government openly carries out tactics of an authoritarian regime. the case of leopoldo lopez is merely one instance of an unfair, illegal and politically motivated trial. the national court has not been impartial in his case. all but one of mr. lópez’s 65 defense witnesses was rejected whilst 108 of the government’s witnesses were admitted. so far, no evidence has been presented against the opposition leader. it is important that the european parliament to speak loudly in this case - condemn political persecution and the imprisonment of democratic opposition in venezuela and call for their release. i had a chance to meet leopoldo personally some time ago, so this is also my personal call. i support his views and encourage him in his political fight for democratic venezuela.
author - even though it's 2014, there is a state in the world that is not just present in the so-called modern slavery that we see, unfortunately, even in developed countries. in mauritania, there are real slaves people who are the property of other people, who have absolute power over them, who decide their life and death. i condemn this state of affairs unequivocally. tolerating such unacceptable practices, which have even been outlawed in this country for several years. i call on the government of mauritania to release the wrongfully detained abolitionist biram dah abeid, whose trial is reportedly starting today. if this activist is convicted, it will not only be a great injustice, but a blow to the thousands of slaves for whom he fought for freedom. i call on the mauritanian government to respect not only human rights but also its own laws. this situation cannot be tolerated and i can assure you that there is no economic interest that would force me to turn a blind eye to this inhuman act.
in writing - what ? i voted in favour of this resolution because the agreement creates better conditions and opportunities for the further development of european-georgian relations. i am very pleased that the agreement removes trade barriers between georgia and european countries, which can ultimately boost the georgian economy, which, like slovakia, has recently been hit by anti-russian sanctions. the slovak republic, my country of origin, is keen to continue its political support to georgia and is ready to assist it in bilateral negotiations, in particular with regard to the transfer of transformative experience in the field of building democratic institutions, establishing the rule of law, respecting human rights, promoting economic reforms and building civil society. i am convinced that the approval of the agreement will open up new prospects and opportunities for georgian companies to strengthen their mutual ties with the eu, and products produced in georgia will find a more direct route to european markets, making georgia a more attractive trading partner for european investors.
i welcome the successful vote on the association agreement between georgia and the european union. i support his pro-european efforts for the mutual benefit of the citizens of the eu and georgia.
in writing, georgia, along with armenia and azerbaijan, was included in the 2004 neighbourhood policy project, through which the eu seeks to assist them in their economic, political and democratic reforms. since that period, georgia has gone through significant stages of development and i am pleased that today we can say that georgia, with its 4.9 million people, with an estimated gdp growth of 6.3%, unemployment of 13.7% and gdp per capita of usd 6 100 in 2014, ranks among the most successful countries in the region in terms of the necessary reforms. i voted in favour of the conclusion of the association agreement between the european union and georgia, as i believe that this agreement will help georgia to adopt the relevant laws and standards, which will become an appropriate instrument for strengthening the mutual ties between the eu and georgia.
